# Product direction, 2026-11-12

An exploration of where this product could go, written for its owner and only user. It answers one question: the market for meeting recorders is crowded, so where is it worth focusing to make this one interesting, radical options included.

How it was made. Six researchers surveyed the market as of late 2026, one segment each, every claim sourced. Two readers mapped what the code really does today and what the project has already considered or rejected. Seven ideators then worked from distinct lenses with that material and the independent audit of 2026-11-12, producing 47 ideas; three judges with different priorities (a market skeptic, an engineer who has to live with the code, and an advocate for a single owner with limited review time) scored every idea from 1 to 5 on differentiation, feasibility, fit and safety; a separate assessor judged the rewrite options against what the audit found. The project's "not for sale" list did not constrain the ideas; the ones that cross it are marked. The field data that arrived on 2026-11-13 (three months of daily use) came after the ideation and is used in the short version and the suggested quarter below.

Sections: The short version; A suggested next quarter; Ranked ideas; The top ideas in detail; What the judges would do; Radical options and their verdicts; What to stop doing; The market in late 2026; What this product is today; Ideas the project already considered; Limits.

## The short version

**1. The base loop is a commodity now.** Detecting a call, capturing it without a bot, transcribing on device, summarising, exporting to Obsidian or Notion and exposing an MCP server is what Talat ships for a one-time fee (<https://techcrunch.com/2026/03/24/talats-ai-meeting-notes-stay-on-your-machine-not-in-the-cloud/>), what Google gives away on the Mac (<https://techcrunch.com/2026/10/08/google-releases-a-new-local-first-granola-competitor/>), and what about sixteen open-source recorders built on the same speech stack do. More features in that loop will not make this product distinctive.

**2. First make capture trustworthy.** No direction is interesting on top of a recorder that loses meetings. Three months of the owner's own logs show about 20 calls never recorded because an unanswered prompt turns into Skip, recordings in the newest build that end a minute late or not at all, and a mute state that is wrong for long stretches. The fixes are small and ordered in `docs/audits/2026-11-13-recording-reliability-audit.md`; the click-to-feedback fixes are in `docs/audits/2026-11-13-responsiveness-audit.md`. This comes before everything below.

**3. Then be the checkable record, not the better note.** All three judges, who agreed on little else, picked the same four ideas:

- **I35. A yardstick that needs no labelling: channel energy, correction overlays and glossary firings as a standing speech scorecard** (total 17.33 of 20, effort M). Build the personal evaluation harness from ground truth the product already produces as a by-product, so it never waits on a hand-transcribed golden set. Candidate label-free scores per engine version: me-attribution error (segments labelled as the owner where only the system channel carries energy, and the reverse), term-miss rate (glossary rewrite firings per audio hour, counted before the rewrite), correction replay (does a re-run now produce the text the owner once typed as a fix), and evidence-anchor verification rate per summarizer. A fifth reading, transcription speed ratio from the event log, answers whether the menu-bar agent still reaches the Neural Engine on macOS 27.2. One command writes a scorecard file, Diagnostics shows the last result, and a FluidAudio bump, model swap or OS point release is accepted only on a scorecard diff.
- **I01. Kernel-enforced zero egress with a per-meeting egress receipt** (total 16.34 of 20, effort M). Under regulated or NDA policy, `mp` re-executes itself inside a macOS sandbox profile that denies every non-loopback connection in the kernel, so the Python guard becomes the readable error and the kernel becomes the enforcement, children and native extensions included. Each run writes `<stem>.egress.json` with four separate lines (pipeline network, daemon, storage location, sinks), and the meeting's top state is the weakest of the four, shown in the Library as Confined, Guarded or Left this Mac with the destinations named. Scope is stated, not implied: the kernel deny wraps only the Python child, while capture, ASR, diarization, the Apple Intelligence summarizer and FluidAudio's model fetch run in the unconfined daemon, and a network deny cannot see a library in an iCloud-synced folder, a LAN sink or a backup. A standing bypass harness in CI is the fence.
- **I16. The archive as a build graph: every artifact re-derivable from retained audio, so the whole library improves when a model does** (total 15.00 of 20, effort L). Make the dual-channel WAV plus the owner's overlays the only sources of truth, and treat transcript, diarization, embeddings, speaker names and summary as cached derivations stamped with input hash, tool and model version, and parameters (the run sidecars already do this for summaries). A background re-derive pass can then replay the library through a new FluidAudio, a new diarizer or a newly named voice and offer a per-meeting diff, with overlays re-anchored by time span and voice embedding instead of by index. First consumer: qualify the FluidAudio 0.14.8 to 0.18.3 bump on the owner's own corrected meetings before it touches anything.
- **I36. Voice census: name a voice once and the archive resolves** (total 14.34 of 20, effort L). Turn enrolment from a click per meeting into a short queue of recurring strangers. Cluster every unnamed per-meeting voice embedding across the library, rank the clusters by how many meetings each would resolve, and show one row per cluster: this voice is in 23 meetings, three short clips, one name field, applied everywhere as a reversible overlay. Because one person talks to a small stable set of colleagues, a later step can replace the open-set cosine gate with a closed-set scorer over that named set with an explicit unknown class, fed by per-segment embeddings taken from the system channel alone so the owner's own speech never contaminates a colleague's print.

What they share: each rests on something the funded vendors gave up by design and cannot easily take back. The best-funded botless recorder deletes audio after transcription (<https://www.granola.ai/pricing>); this product keeps channel-separated audio, so every transcript and summary can be re-made when models improve. Cloud vendors need the data to arrive; this product can prove nothing left. And it has one stable owner whose voice is always on one channel, which is what makes identity solvable. The field data sharpens the case for the fourth pick: the owner's own voice sits under an anonymous label in 35 of 111 meetings.

**4. Subtract before adding.** The strongest finding of the contrarian lens is now backed by the logs: in the two months covered, all 74 recordings took one path (on-device transcript, one cloud summary, one Obsidian note). The local, Apple Intelligence, map-reduce, bring-your-own, Notion and filesystem paths were never exercised after 2026-09-14, and there were zero retries, regenerations, republishes, re-transcribes or summary edits. The contrarian lens counts five of the 17 critical and high audit findings inside features that could be deleted instead of repaired (the real-time regulated gate, the Notion rewrite path and series continuity). The logs confirm disuse for the first two; for series continuity they cannot tell. The judges' subtraction picks: I41 (Regulated mode means zero egress, not zero recovery: delete the real-time gate and the destructive end cut); I42 (Flip the default on the secondary surfaces: deleted on a date unless a local witness shows use); I43 (Notion gets a note once: delete the rewrite path and the transcript upload, and drop the LAN sink); I46 (Stop producing claims faster than one person can check them: audit moratorium, a witness rule, and deletion of unfenced prose); I47 (Shrink sensing to what the log has seen: Teams starts by itself, everything else is a hotkey).

**5. Rewrites: Rust no, all-Swift maybe, and two structural moves now.** A Rust core is the wrong tool here: the engine is Apple frameworks (ScreenCaptureKit, CoreAudio, Accessibility, CoreML on the Neural Engine), Rust reaches each only through a Swift bridge, the evidence says the Neural Engine path for the speech model is weak from Rust, and the Rust recorders in the market chose it for cross-platform reach, which this product does not want. About nine tenths of what the audit found would survive any change of language. The one rewrite that removes a measured cost is dropping the Python sidecar for an all-Swift app, staged, and it should be decided by running one bench that became possible only recently, not by argument. Worth doing regardless: shrink the seam between the two trees, and split the 71,000-line app target into modules so the compiler enforces what text fences police today. Details under Radical options.

**6. Stop.** Stop adding parity features, stop filing audits and tasks faster than fixes can be witnessed (18 fixes recorded as done do not hold), stop carrying surfaces nobody uses, and stop debating rewrites without a number.

## A suggested next quarter

My recommendation, drawn from the judges' portfolios, the assessor and the field data. It is a sequence, not a commitment.

1. **Weeks 1 to 2: make it record every meeting and react at once.** The unanswered-prompt setting today; log attribution (build commit, stop cause, prompt withdrawn); the three newest-build detection defects; a visible Starting state on the click. Alongside, the audit's fix-first list, choosing deletion over repair wherever a judge showed the feature is unused.
2. **Weeks 2 to 6: instruments before features.** I35 (A yardstick that needs no labelling: channel energy, correction overlays and glossary firings as a standing speech scorecard), so quality has a number that needs no labelling by hand; the bypass harness and per-meeting receipt from I01 (Kernel-enforced zero egress with a per-meeting egress receipt); the direction-neutral seam work (delete unwired Python, one owner for the local model server, a version handshake between app and pipeline, generated contract fixtures).
3. **Weeks 6 to 10: the two bets.** A probe of I16 (The archive as a build graph: every artifact re-derivable from retained audio, so the whole library improves when a model does) on a handful of old meetings with the yardstick as judge, and I36 (Voice census: name a voice once and the archive resolves), which the field data says is the largest quality problem in the transcripts.
4. **One decision with a number.** Run the Swift local-model bench once. If it clears its pre-registered bars, plan the staged all-Swift move; if not, let Python own summarise and publish and retire the Swift copies.
5. **Process.** A fix closes only on a witness (a test of intent that failed first, or a replay of the field log), and no new audit until critical and high findings are at zero.

### The lens theses

**privacy-regulated.** Local does not mean legal: the market file itself says no case or regulator source on fully local recorders was found, and that the recorder's own duties (all-party notice, voiceprints, GDPR for work meetings) survive the move off the cloud. What a lawyer, clinician, security lead or works council can defend is an artifact, not a sentence: who was told, what left the machine, what was kept, what was destroyed, and proof the record was not altered. Today's audit rates the policy core sound but shows the claims running ahead of the code (F014, F005, F046, F018, F041, G032, an httpx-only guard, a wire check that was waived rather than measured), so the honest status is unproven. My proposal is to turn each privacy claim into an enforced or machine-checked property with a per-meeting evidence file, in the order prove (egress, erasure), record (consent, custody), minimise (voiceprints, quote-only minutes); a funded vendor cannot copy this quickly because its business needs the data to arrive, and would not dare to because an egress receipt or a deletion certificate would document exactly what the Otter and Granola complaints allege. This is about making the owner's own recordings defensible, the owner being the first regulated user (the shipped base prompt hard-codes a regulated-SaaS domain), and it does not reopen the rejected paid or B2B route.

**agent-memory.** Every funded notetaker and a dozen local ones now ship an MCP server, so existence is worth nothing; what is thin is trust in both directions: can the owner prove what an agent was allowed to read, and can the agent's reader check what it was told. This project already holds the hard halves (a fail-closed per-meeting gate, anchors verified against the transcript, retained audio, a schema-fenced event log), but the five MCP tools drop the anchors, the log records only a tool name per call, the audit proved two readers leaking past the gate (F014, AUTO6), and an agent with file access can skip the gate entirely. My argument is an order of work: gate and ledger first, receipts second, then decisions and commitments as state that agents resolve with connectors the app refuses to hold (calendar, tickets, mail), and only after that OS surfaces or a kernel split. The lens has a kill switch the owner can read on day one, because the event log has emitted a row per MCP call since the server shipped on 2026-08-18; if agents barely read the library, most of this should not be built. Where it does hold, agent consumers let the project delete surface: the blocking local Ask generator, Facts as a human inbox, the workflow-keyed prep card and the six verb-only intents.

**radical-rearchitecture.** Language is the wrong unit of re-architecture here: the 2026 evidence says Rust buys cross-platform reach and costs the Neural Engine path, and the base loop is cloned weekly in both Swift and Rust, so no rewrite by itself makes this tool interesting. The moves that do are the ones that turn its identity claims into structural facts a funded cloud vendor cannot copy: zero egress enforced by the kernel instead of a patched HTTP client, an archive whose every artifact can be re-derived from retained audio when models improve, and a recorder small enough to be isolated and trusted apart from 70k lines of UI. The one true rewrite worth probing is dropping Python, because the audit shows every cross-tree defect sits where one rule exists twice, and the blocker recorded in July (no MLX-Swift candidate with constrained decoding) has since shipped upstream; the project's own local12 bench can settle it in days. Phone and watch belong as dumb satellites feeding the Mac rather than companions, and Rust belongs only in a portable library kernel gated on a real need to read the library off the Mac. Suggested order: the sandbox probe and the re-derivation probe first (cheap and identity-defining), then the Swift candidate, then decide the process split.

**beyond-meetings.** Leaving the call is not an edge in itself: Zoom, Google, Circleback, Jamie, Granola and Talat already capture in-person talk, and dictation apps have merged with notetakers, so the edge has to come from what this product knows that they do not. Channel separation dies in a room and far-field diarization runs at roughly 10 to 30% DER, but four assets survive: a mic channel that is physically the owner, a mute and call-state oracle (MeetingPipeCore), a voiceprint roster built from clean call audio, and retained audio to measure against. The lens therefore says follow the owner's own voice first, where consent burden is near zero and almost everything can be proven on the existing corpus before capture code is written: asides said on mute, a spoken debrief, notes that cite meetings, and at the radical end an own-voice-only ambient ledger. Two-channel conversations that are not meetings (phone and messenger calls) come second, and other people in rooms come last, only through imported files and only if cross-condition identity measures well. Every first probe below is owner-run, read-only and counts-only, and none requires changing or rebuilding the app, which matters because the installed binary is two months behind HEAD (F021).

**open-source-community.** The recorder itself has nothing to give that the field lacks: about 16 FluidAudio-based recorders and weekly clones already exist, and the market file shows that stars are not a return (small local recorders sit at 18 to 91). What this project has that they do not is instrumentation: a replayed, PII-gated detection corpus, cross-language golden fixtures, a single zero-egress predicate, and a paper trail of AI-written fixes with audit verdicts. So publish contracts and tests, never content, and judge each release by what comes back to one maintainer: traces and locale labels that cannot be produced on one Mac, a single owner for a file contract that now lives in two languages, early warning on FluidAudio and macOS regressions, and outside eyes on the 18 fixes that do not hold. In nearly every case the publishable artifact is the same one the audit says the project needs internally, which is what makes the cost defensible, and where a claim is not yet true (privacy, end-of-call detection) the honest first release is a failing conformance report. On Rust: not capture or inference, where the market file finds no accelerator path, but a clean-room second reader of the on-disk format is a defensible experiment and the form in which the Rust-majority open-source recorders could consume this work.

**personal-indispensability.** The capture loop is a commodity, so indispensability for one person has to come from things only a retained, channel-separated, single-owner archive can compound: knowing which words were the owner's own, recognising a small stable set of colleagues by voice, and being able to re-derive every transcript and summary when models improve. The project has already built or spiked every item in this lens, and its own measurements beat the market's enthusiasm: Facts closed 1.2% of its actions, the workflow-keyed recap 'makes no sense', the roster auto-names about a third of voices, the LoRA lost to its base. Those failures share two causes, identity that arrives late and by hand, and measurements that wait on owner labour and then never run. So the ideas are a dependency chain and not a menu: a yardstick that needs no labelling, then identity resolved from one naming pass, then memory keyed by the owner's own voice and by person instead of by series or a universal action list, then reprinting the archive, plus one cheap long-shot probe for a calendar-free meeting key. Each is framed as a probe with a registered bar whose failure closes the idea or carries out a deletion the project has already pencilled in.

**contrarian-delete.** Capture, on-device transcription, summaries, Obsidian export and an MCP server are now free from Google, Talat and a dozen clones (https://techcrunch.com/2026/10/08/google-releases-a-new-local-first-granola-competitor/), and the 2026-11-12 audit shows this codebase's 194 verified findings sit at the seams of what was added around a sound core: a second capture mode, a second summarizer, a rewrite path into Notion, state carried between meetings, and prose that outran the code (18 fixes that do not hold). So the interesting move for a one-person project is subtraction: five of the 17 critical and high findings (G001, F004, F014, F017, F088) disappear with the feature they live in, two more (F046, F019) go if the event log shows resume and merge are barely used, and none of the seven ideas below needs a new dependency. If everything else had to go I would keep exactly one thing, the capture-first recorder that writes channel-separated audio (mic left, system right) and its speaker-labelled transcript with embeddings as plain files: the audit found no path that silently loses captured audio, the best-funded rivals discard audio by design (https://www.granola.ai/pricing), and every other layer can be regenerated from it with whatever model is best later. It is also the least tested part (MeetingRecorder at 17% line coverage, no test that starts, writes and stops a real recording), which is where the attention freed by deletion should go. On Rust: the rewrite worth doing removes rules that exist twice, and a third language would add a seam and lose the Neural Engine path.

## The top ideas in detail

Every idea scoring 14 or more, or picked by at least one judge (21 of 47).

### I35. A yardstick that needs no labelling: channel energy, correction overlays and glossary firings as a standing speech scorecard

Lens personal-indispensability. Total 17.33 (differentiation 3.33, feasibility 4, fit 5, safety 5). Effort M. Picked by: market-skeptic, codebase-engineer, owner-advocate.

**Pitch.** Build the personal evaluation harness from ground truth the product already produces as a by-product, so it never waits on a hand-transcribed golden set. Candidate label-free scores per engine version: me-attribution error (segments labelled as the owner where only the system channel carries energy, and the reverse), term-miss rate (glossary rewrite firings per audio hour, counted before the rewrite), correction replay (does a re-run now produce the text the owner once typed as a fix), and evidence-anchor verification rate per summarizer. A fifth reading, transcription speed ratio from the event log, answers whether the menu-bar agent still reaches the Neural Engine on macOS 27.2. One command writes a scorecard file, Diagnostics shows the last result, and a FluidAudio bump, model swap or OS point release is accepted only on a scorecard diff.

**Value to the owner.** Upgrades stop being acts of faith. The owner sees, as numbers on their own English, Ukrainian and mixed audio, whether a new speech stack is better, and a silent regression (an OS update that blanks output, or the owner's name stamped on remote speech) shows up the day it happens with no labelling work.

**Why now.** The pin is FluidAudio 0.14.8 against upstream 0.18.3, and ASR6 was amended on 2026-11-12 to say a bump is ready to qualify, so a decision is waiting on a number that does not exist. The market digest (speech frontier segment, no URL in the digest) records that no trustworthy benchmark covers meeting audio end to end, that vendor figures conflict, and that a point OS release produced all-blank CTC output on M1 and M4 in a major SDK; the one public Parakeet versus Apple comparison is a vendor's own (https://www.argmaxinc.com/blog/apple-and-argmax). Accurate names, numbers and speaker identification are what surveyed users rank first (https://gladia.io/meeting-assistant-market-map), and nobody publishes a measured number for them.

**Builds on.** Stereo FLAC archive (mic left, system right) and the per-channel RMS code in pipeline/src/mp/diarize.py (soundfile and numpy are already in the lock); the mute timeline, so zero-filled muted spans are excluded; <stem>.transcript_corrections.json (index, original_text, edited_text) and the speaker-label overlay; glossary rules; scripts/valid1_check.py, which already reads a DER lower bound from corrections and ASR latency from transcription.engine events with audio_seconds; run sidecars with engine and model provenance; the mp finalize re-transcribe path; the Diagnostics view. Probe template: the repo root docs/spikes/feat17_roster_near_miss.py (other paths here are relative to that repo root).

**What the audit says.** Helps: the recorder core and pure logic held up and performance is rated 4.5, so a batch scorer is low risk. Bears directly: F110 (no test ever runs real ASR or a real recording), ASR6 and the build facts (pin four minor versions behind, model weights float on main with no hash, so a score must record the model revision it was taken on), and background Neural Engine access on 27.2 is unmeasured. Warnings: F151 (the coverage script printed 90.0% for a 51.0% daemon, so this instrument needs a planted-regression self-check before anyone trusts it), F062 (correction overlays are index-keyed with no drift check, so replay must match on time window and original_text), and Python tests do not isolate HOME, so the script takes its directories as explicit arguments.

**First probe.** Script only, owner-run, read-only, about three days of work, shaped like the FEAT17 probe: counts only, no text, no names, and it refuses to print a verdict on an empty scan. Step 0 prints coverage: stems with audio still present, stereo versus mono or merged, hours, and how many carry a correction overlay. Step 1, on up to 40 stereo meetings and reading the same redacted artifact the transcriber consumed, classifies half-second frames as mic-only, system-only, both or silent with a 12 dB dominance margin (muted spans excluded), joins them to transcript speaker labels and prints false-me and missed-me rates. Step 2 counts glossary firings per audio hour. Step 3 reads transcription speed ratio per week from events.jsonl across the macOS 27 install date. Registered bars: the channel hypothesis is USABLE if at least 70% of speech frames are single-channel on at least 80% of stereo meetings, a second run on a copy reproduces every rate within 1 point, and a planted regression (channels swapped on a copy) moves the false-me rate above 50%. It is CLOSED if under 50% of speech frames are single-channel (speaker bleed, no echo cancellation), in which case ASR4's hand-held golden set is the only route. A speed ratio that fell by more than half after the macOS 27 date is filed as its own defect. Comparing 0.14.8 with 0.18.3 is a second step that needs owner approval as a dependency change, a throwaway build that absorbs the ModelHub API break, and an isolated home and model cache so nothing live is touched.

**Risks.** The mic channel is the owner only when the owner wears headphones and nobody else speaks into that mic, which is why it is a hypothesis with a bar and not an assumption. Label-free scores cover attribution and known-term errors, not full word error rate, so a model can improve them and still be worse elsewhere; ASR4's small hand set stays as the second opinion. Correction overlays are few and biased toward errors the owner noticed. Shipped as a CLI only it breaks the de-CLI rule and joins the benches unrun since July, so it has to be wired to the upgrade path and Diagnostics to count as done.

**Considered before.** Yes, as ASR4 (new P2, filed 2026-10-24: an owner-held EN/UK/code-switched golden set with WER) and as the DER lower bound in valid1_check.py; neither has produced a number. What changed: this version removes the hand-labelling that has kept owner-owed benches (LOCAL13, LOCAL14, CAL3 leg 1) unrun since July; ASR6 was amended on 2026-11-12 to say a 0.18.3 bump is ready to qualify; and the audit found background Neural Engine access on macOS 27.2 unmeasured. It complements ASR4 and does not replace it.

- market-skeptic: An internal instrument, not a feature, yet it gates I16 and is the only benchmark design here that does not wait on owner labelling. Needs the planted-regression self-check given F151, and the channel hypothesis may fail on speaker bleed.
- codebase-engineer: Verified: channel helpers in diarize.py, and valid1_check.py already has attribution, DER lower bound and timing modes. It removes the hand labelling that stalled ASR4, carries a planted-regression self-check against the F151 failure, and matches corrections by time window because of F062; wire it to Diagnostics or it joins the unrun benches.
- owner-advocate: Removes the hand-labelling that has stalled every owner-owed bench since July and turns upgrades into a scorecard diff on the owner's own audio. Script-only and read-only with a planted-regression self-check; everything speech-related in the portfolio waits on it.

### I01. Kernel-enforced zero egress with a per-meeting egress receipt

Lens privacy-regulated. Total 16.34 (differentiation 4.33, feasibility 3.67, fit 4.67, safety 3.67). Effort M. Picked by: market-skeptic, codebase-engineer, owner-advocate.

**Pitch.** Under regulated or NDA policy, `mp` re-executes itself inside a macOS sandbox profile that denies every non-loopback connection in the kernel, so the Python guard becomes the readable error and the kernel becomes the enforcement, children and native extensions included. Each run writes `<stem>.egress.json` with four separate lines (pipeline network, daemon, storage location, sinks), and the meeting's top state is the weakest of the four, shown in the Library as Confined, Guarded or Left this Mac with the destinations named. Scope is stated, not implied: the kernel deny wraps only the Python child, while capture, ASR, diarization, the Apple Intelligence summarizer and FluidAudio's model fetch run in the unconfined daemon, and a network deny cannot see a library in an iCloud-synced folder, a LAN sink or a backup. A standing bypass harness in CI is the fence.

**Value to the owner.** The owner can say that the summarize and publish stage of this meeting could not reach the network, point at an enforcement the app's own 90K lines cannot bypass, and hand over a file that says so per meeting, with the unproven parts labelled as unproven. A client's security reviewer audits a ten-line profile and a harness instead of the codebase. The harness is tool-agnostic, so it can also live in the public repo as a runnable claims test for any local recorder, which sits inside the contributor-visibility carve-out.

**Why now.** The vendor suits turn on a third party receiving the audio: the Otter court let wiretap and BIPA claims proceed on 13 Aug 2026 (https://uctoday.com/productivity-automation/otter-ai-fails-to-dismiss-core-privacy-claims-in-u-s-court/). tl;dv exposed metadata for 181,874 meetings while marketed as SOC 2 compliant (https://tldv.io/blog/our-thoughts-on-the-darkreading-com-article/). The market file finds local claims in this category partial or uncheckable (Anarlog, Hedy, Superwhisper and Quill each keep a cloud path; a closed-source rival's no-outbound claim cannot be verified), and HN commenters already call the local category saturated (https://hn.algolia.com/api/v1/items/50454625). A dozen tools say it stays on your Mac; none prints proof per meeting. /usr/bin/sandbox-exec is present on this macOS 27.2 host, so the mechanism needs no Developer ID.

**Builds on.** `pipeline/src/mp/egress_guard.py` (arm, child_env), `entry.prepare` as the single ordering point, `config.zero_egress` as the single predicate, the self re-exec already used in `summarize_local.py`, `<stem>.run.json` provenance, `cloudsync.py` (synced-folder detection), `PrivacyReach.swift` (strings built from the predicates), the truth-fence CI.

**What the audit says.** Helps: the audit confirmed one fail-closed zero-egress predicate, argv-only spawns and a stdio-only MCP server. Motivates: the guard covers httpx only and authorises on URL host (SEC19), is kept valid by version caps, and 18 claimed fixes are not holding, so another in-process patch is the weak answer. Limits: enforcement engages only after classification, so SEC21 (NDA rule fails open), F005 and F046 (a workflow leaking into the next meeting) sit upstream and are untouched; F014 (a cloud-allowed run carrying a withheld meeting's content) is a policy-layer leak a kernel deny cannot see; SEC18 and STOR6 (iCloud-synced default library), F018 (doctor never checks the NDA publish folder) and SEC28 (a published note can fetch a remote image when opened) are file-mediated; FluidAudio 0.14.8 deletes its cache and re-downloads from huggingface.co on a load error, inside the daemon.

**First probe.** 5 days, synthetic only. Build a harness of 10 planted bypasses (httpx direct, httpx through an ambient proxy variable, urllib, requests, raw socket, asyncio stream, ctypes connect, child curl, child Python, DNS lookup of a unique name) aimed at a canary listener on this Mac's own LAN address, so nothing leaves the machine. Run each with the guard armed only, then under the sandbox profile; then run a local MLX summary of a golden fixture under the profile, and run the `cloudsync` check against a scratch library with a planted synced-folder marker. Pre-registered pass: 10 of 10 blocked under the profile, the golden summary completes with a schema-valid result, wall-time overhead under 5%, and the receipt's storage line reports the planted synced folder as not confined. Record the armed-only score as the baseline. Fail: sandbox-exec refuses on 27.2, or MLX and the loopback model server cannot run under the profile after 2 days of tuning; then stop and fall back to SEC19's in-process layer.

**Risks.** sandbox-exec is deprecated by Apple and may disappear, and its durable replacements (App Sandbox entitlements, a NetworkExtension filter) need a Developer ID, which is on the not-for-sale list. A receipt written by the code it attests can overclaim, so it must name the enforcement layer per line and default to the weaker word. A broad allow-default profile proves network confinement only. The daemon half stays argued rather than measured until EGRESS1 is actually run.

**Considered before.** Partly. The egress policy is adopted as the core identity; SEC19 (P2, new) proposes an in-process socket layer; EGRESS1 (P4, deferred) is a one-time firewall observation whose bar was waived, not measured; SEC18 covers file-mediated egress. A grep of docs and backlog finds no kernel confinement of the child and no per-meeting receipt. What changed: the 2026-11-12 audit found 18 claimed fixes not holding and three later features walking around the gate, which argues for enforcement outside the code that keeps changing; and EGRESS1's own promotion trigger (an NDA meeting that depends on the claim) is met, since the visibility module's docstring records NDA meetings in the library.

- market-skeptic: Per-meeting proof of non-egress is something no rival prints and a multi-tenant cloud cannot (local claims are partial or uncheckable per the market file; tl;dv leaked while SOC 2, https://tldv.io/blog/our-thoughts-on-the-darkreading-com-article/). It rests on a deprecated sandbox-exec that is unverified on 27.2 and covers only the Python child; the durable replacement needs a Developer ID, which would cross the not-for-sale list.
- codebase-engineer: Observed on this host: sandbox-exec runs on 27.2, a profile denying outbound network except localhost returned EPERM for a non-local address and children inherited it, and egress_guard.child_env, entry.prepare and config.zero_egress exist as claimed. With the filters I used, the same profile still admitted this Mac's own interface address and one extra allow clause made an earlier profile silently permissive, so the probe's LAN-address canary cannot discriminate and the harness, not the ten-line profile, is the piece to trust.
- owner-advocate: Best-shaped member of the egress cluster: a small profile plus a bypass harness sits outside the code that keeps changing. Run the 5-day probe and wrap only the mp child; defer the receipt UI, and say plainly that F014, F005 and F046 are policy leaks a kernel deny cannot see.

### I08. One read broker: narrowing tiers, client profiles, receipts and a read ledger

Lens agent-memory. Total 15.34 (differentiation 3.67, feasibility 4, fit 3.67, safety 4). Effort M. Picked by: codebase-engineer.

**Pitch.** Turn the binary visible or withheld gate into one read broker that every reader of meeting text passes through, with tiers that only narrow what a cloud client gets from a visible meeting (metadata, summary, full transcript) under a named profile set in each MCP client's launch entry; a withheld meeting stays withheld for every profile. Each payload leaves in one envelope: fenced text, the verified anchors already on disk (quote and second), a deep link from the existing `deeplink_url` helper, the speaker looked up from the transcript segment at that second, and a count of what was held back. Each read appends a row to the event log (profile, tool, stems, tier served, bytes) and Diagnostics gets a plain 'what agents read' view. A `verify_quote` tool, a thin wrapper over `evidence.resolve`, lets an agent check a quote before it repeats it.

**Value to the owner.** The owner can answer 'which meetings did a cloud assistant see last week' from a log instead of from faith, and agent answers arrive with a clickable second in the recording. Because the agent's own model does the reading, the measured local-latency NO-GO on interactive chat does not apply to this path, which makes the blocking local Ask generator a candidate to cut. It also gives the audit's recurring leak class one place to be fenced.

**Why now.** The market file found no personal tool with per-meeting sensitivity labels, tiered exposure, per-client rules or a readable log of agent reads; vendors offer an on or off connector (agent and MCP segment; the digest prints no URL for that paragraph). Binary withholding is not distinctive: a verified local connector already excludes restricted meetings by default (https://claude.com/connectors/minutes-conversation-memory). MCP is near universal and often plan-gated or limited to recent notes, so depth and control are the open ground (https://www.granola.ai/pricing, https://granola.ai/updates). The one comparative MCP test praised linked transcript lines and showed misheard identifiers and speaker confusion flowing into agent answers (digest, competitor-run, no URL printed).

**Builds on.** `pipeline/src/mp/visibility.py` (fail-closed gate with reasons), `mcp_tools.py` (five tools, `wrap_untrusted`), `MeetingSummary.evidence` (quote plus `t`, verified before write, currently dropped by `get_meeting` and `list_actions`), `evidence.resolve` and `segments_from_timestamped_markdown`, `markdown.deeplink_url`, `transcript_corrections.overlaid_markdown`, the event log (`mcp_tool_called` and `mcp_serve_started` exist since the 2026-08-18 commit but carry only tool name, withheld_hit and counts), truth-fence CI. Per-anchor attribution confidence (channel truth versus roster match) would be new work and is unverified.

**What the audit says.** Helps: the audit rates the single zero-egress predicate, fail-closed sidecar reads and stdio-only MCP as solid. Shapes the work: 'privacy state leaks across meetings' is its second-ranked problem. F014 (series continuity sends a withheld meeting's decisions and action owners to the cloud) and AUTO6 (global regulated mode ignored for older meetings, index built once per session) are the proven leaks; F005 and F046 carry a workflow across meetings; AUTO8 (titles and `list_actions` text unfenced) belongs inside this work. Per-meeting runs rely on `zero_egress` by design, so the fence, not a grep of who calls `visibility`, decides what else leaks. G004 (workflow rename orphans NDA membership) means a tier must live in the sidecar by workflow id. The 18 'claimed fixed, not holding' findings argue for a canary fence over a comment.

**First probe.** Ten days, owner-run, almost no product change. (1) Usage kill switch, day one: count `mcp_serve_started` sessions and `mcp_tool_called` rows in the pipeline event log since 2026-08-18 by week and tool, excluding test residue. Pre-registered: 100 or more tool calls across 10 or more distinct days is GO for the lens; fewer than 20 calls or fewer than 6 distinct days means the state, join, OS and kernel ideas wait. A low count is ambiguous because AUTO5 records a client reading files instead of calling tools, so read it together with the sealed-tier probe. (2) Canary fence: a fixture library with one meeting per stance (NDA, regulated, no sidecar, visible), each with a unique canary string in transcript, summary, decisions and actions; run every reader that can emit meeting text (five MCP tools, ask, digest, summarize with series continuity, each cloud sink, the paste bundle). A leak is a withheld canary reaching a cloud engine, a cloud sink or an MCP client, or one meeting's canary appearing in another meeting's run; an NDA meeting written to the local filesystem sink is correct. Pass is zero leaks; F014 and AUTO6 are expected to fail today, and the number of failing surfaces is the size of the broker work.

**Risks.** An agent with file tools can bypass any MCP-level gate: AUTO5 records a real session where the client left the tool and read a transcript off disk (a visible meeting in that case), so the broker binds MCP-only clients and is advisory for a coding agent until the sealed-tier idea or a deny rule covers the folder. The profile is set by launch entry, not authenticated. Tiers are one more setting in a product with too many. The ledger lists meeting stems and is itself sensitive. Speaker lookup per anchor inherits diarization errors.

**Considered before.** Partly. The binary gate shipped with AUTO3; on 2026-07-27 the owner chose fail-closed and declined fail-open-on-unknowns, expose-everything and per-workflow opt-in. This does not revisit that: tiers only narrow what a cloud profile receives from an already visible meeting and never reopen a withheld one. New here are per-client profiles, receipts in the payload and a per-read ledger. What changed: the 2026-11-12 audit showed later features leaking past the gate, and the 2026-11-12 security review filed AUTO8.

- market-skeptic: Tiers and a read ledger are the control the market file found missing in personal tools, but Minutes already excludes restricted meetings (https://claude.com/connectors/minutes-conversation-memory) and funded vendors have access controls on their roadmaps. The canary fence is the part to do now because it sizes F014 and AUTO6; the rest waits on an MCP usage count.
- codebase-engineer: Assets verified (visibility.py, five tools in mcp_tools.py, evidence.resolve, the mcp_tool_called row). Its first step, a fixture-only canary fence across every reader, sizes and then pins F014, AUTO6 and AUTO8, which is the audit's second-ranked problem; tiers and profiles should wait for the usage count and bind MCP-only clients at best (AUTO5).
- owner-advocate: The two probe legs are the valuable part: the day-one MCP usage count decides the whole agent lens, and the canary fence is the standing test for the audit's cross-meeting leak class. Tiers and client profiles are more settings in a product with too many; build them only if usage clears the bar.

### I29. Meeting bundle 0.1: write the per-meeting folder down as a spec with JSON Schemas and a validator, so the file contract has one owner

Lens open-source-community. Total 15.34 (differentiation 2.67, feasibility 4, fit 4, safety 4.67). Effort M. Picked by: codebase-engineer.

**Pitch.** Document the files that make up one meeting as a versioned format: stereo audio with the mic-left, system-right convention, transcript JSON, meta sidecar, speaker-label and text-correction overlays, markers, embeddings, summary with evidence anchors, run provenance and publish result. Ship JSON Schemas, a stdlib-only validate command that prints counts per rule, and the existing golden fixtures as the conformance set. Its distinctive clauses are ones other recorders' Markdown-plus-frontmatter dialects do not have: edits are reversible overlays that never rewrite the base, a summary claim can carry a quote and timestamp that was verified against the transcript, each artifact names the engine that wrote it, and a visibility label travels with the meeting.

**Value to the owner.** It is the typed owner for the per-meeting contract that ARCH10 and PIPE33 ask for, in a form that also works outside the repo: one schema both trees validate against, in place of about 18 mirrored rules. 'A library that outlives the app' becomes a checkable property (the whole library validates after every upgrade) instead of a README sentence, and the exit door is documented before it is needed.

**Why now.** 59% of surveyed users plan to switch notetakers within a year (https://gladia.io/meeting-assistant-market-map). In 2026 Granola changed and then encrypted its local cache, breaking readers and Obsidian plugins built on it (https://superframeworks.com/articles/granola-encrypted-local-notes); Rewind's capture was switched off after the Meta deal (https://9to5mac.com/2025/12/05/rewind-limitless-meta-acquisition/); Hyprnote was renamed twice (https://anarlog.so/blog/char-is-now-anarlog); Fathom's standalone future is unstated (https://blog.superhuman.com/superhuman-acquires-fathom/). Local tools each write their own dialect (Minutes uses Markdown with YAML frontmatter, https://github.com/silverstein/minutes), and the market file names an open on-disk format as a direction the field leaves open.

**Builds on.** ADR 0003 (files are the source of truth); CONVENTIONS.md sections for meta.json, markers, diarize_cleanup, publish.json, evidence anchors and speaker enrollment; the 10 cross-tree golden fixtures in daemon/Tests/MeetingPipeTests/Fixtures; scripts/gen_contract_fixtures.py; pipeline/src/mp/schemas.py; scripts/truth_fences.py as the place a schema fence would live.

**What the audit says.** Helps: the audit calls the two-tree seam the main architectural cost (about 26 shared file kinds, about 18 mirrored rules, 10 of roughly 30 contracts fixture-pinned, F129) and says every concrete defect it found sits where one rule exists twice. Blocks: the format is not coherent yet, and a spec has to fix or confess each of these: index-keyed correction overlays with no drift check (F062), publishers that ignore the overlays (F107), merge treating per-recording speaker labels as global (F019), a name-keyed glossary beside UUID-keyed workflows (G004), Swift as an unvalidated second writer of summary.json, and `try?` collapsing unreadable into absent at 37 read sites.

**First probe.** Five days. Write JSON Schemas for six file kinds (meta, transcript, speaker-label overlay, text-correction overlay, summary, publish result) from CONVENTIONS.md alone, without opening the writers. The owner runs a read-only validator over the real library that prints counts per file kind and per violated rule, never content. Pass: at least 95% of files of every kind validate; no more than 10 distinct violation classes in total; every file under Fixtures/meta-contract and the example payloads in the publish-result, speaker-overlay and transcript-corrections golden fixtures validate unchanged; and the schema author needed no more than 5 look-ups in source to resolve an ambiguity. Fail (under 80% on any kind, or more than 15 source look-ups): the documents are not the contract, so do ARCH10 internally first and publish nothing.

**Risks.** Nobody else adopts it, which is the likely outcome, so the return has to be internal to be worth the work. A published 0.1 freezes known defects unless the spec lists them as open issues. A 'standard' from a single-user tool has no authority; name it as this tool's documented format. Version migration becomes a permanent duty. Embeddings and voiceprints must be specified as never-exported fields, given the BIPA voiceprint claims in the market file.

**Considered before.** Partly. ADR 0003 adopted plain files and rejected a database of record; ARCH10 and PIPE33 (open) want a typed owner for the per-meeting contract; a public spec is not in the record. What changed: the 2026 lock-in events in the market file, and the 2026-11-12 audit putting numbers on the seam.

- market-skeptic: Hygiene with a durability story; nobody will adopt the format, so the return is ARCH10 delivered as executable schemas. Low risk, and it shrinks the 18 mirrored rules.
- codebase-engineer: ARCH10's typed owner in spec form: pydantic models, gen_contract_fixtures.py and truth_fences.py already exist, and the counts-only validator measures doc-versus-code drift directly. Nobody else will adopt the format, so judge it on the internal return and list F062, F107 and F019 as open issues, not frozen behaviour.
- owner-advocate: Worth it for the internal half: schemas and a counts-only validator are the typed owner ARCH10 asks for and a direct test of whether CONVENTIONS is really the contract. Skip the public '0.1 standard' framing and put the validator in truth_fences.

### I16. The archive as a build graph: every artifact re-derivable from retained audio, so the whole library improves when a model does

Lens radical-rearchitecture. Total 15.00 (differentiation 5, feasibility 3, fit 4, safety 3). Effort L. Picked by: market-skeptic, codebase-engineer, owner-advocate.

**Pitch.** Make the dual-channel WAV plus the owner's overlays the only sources of truth, and treat transcript, diarization, embeddings, speaker names and summary as cached derivations stamped with input hash, tool and model version, and parameters (the run sidecars already do this for summaries). A background re-derive pass can then replay the library through a new FluidAudio, a new diarizer or a newly named voice and offer a per-meeting diff, with overlays re-anchored by time span and voice embedding instead of by index. First consumer: qualify the FluidAudio 0.14.8 to 0.18.3 bump on the owner's own corrected meetings before it touches anything.

**Value to the owner.** Name a voice today and every past meeting picks it up. A model upgrade improves a meeting from last spring, not only the next one. Corrections stop being lost or misapplied on re-transcribe. And 'is the new model better on my meetings' becomes a number computed from the owner's own corrections, which unsticks a speech pin that is five months behind upstream.

**Why now.** Retained audio is the one asset the best-funded vendors chose not to keep: Granola deletes audio after transcription and is studying expiry of verbatim transcripts, Notion and ChatGPT discard audio (https://www.granola.ai/pricing, https://platformer.news/granola-chris-pedregal-interview, https://www.notion.com/help/ai-meeting-notes, https://www.sally.io/blog/chatgpt-record-mode-review). Users rank accurate names, numbers and speaker identity first (https://gladia.io/meeting-assistant-market-map), no trustworthy end-to-end meeting benchmark exists (https://soniqo.audio/benchmarks, https://www.pyannote.ai/blog/top-7-speaker-diarization-apis-and-models-in-2026), and point OS releases have already produced all-blank transcripts in the SDK this product depends on (https://github.com/FluidInference/FluidAudio/releases).

**Builds on.** Retained channel-separated WAVs, per-meeting 256-d speaker embeddings, the named roster, reversible overlays, run sidecars with engine provenance, the correction corpus, the existing per-meeting Re-transcribe and mp finalize path, SinkDispatcher (95.7% covered), cross-tree golden fixtures, and ADR 0003's rule that every view is a derived index over files.

**What the audit says.** Blocks until fixed: F062 (index-keyed overlays applied with no drift check and dropped when a re-transcribe fails) is the exact defect a whole-library re-derive would multiply; F107 (publishers ignore overlays), F019 (merge treats per-recording labels as global), G003 (undo naming deletes a whole voiceprint) and F001 (the originals reaper deletes sources) all break the 'sources are sacred' premise. F017 means a re-derive must never auto-republish to Notion. Helps: the audit's two installed-system facts, the stale FluidAudio pin and the unmeasured Neural Engine access for the background agent on macOS 27.2, are both answered by the probe. Model weights floating on main with no hash means derivation stamps must include a weights hash.

**First probe.** Under two weeks, owner-run, aggregates only, no content printed. A scratch SwiftPM package outside daemon/ links FluidAudio 0.18.3 (the app's pin is untouched) and re-derives 30 meetings that carry speaker or text overlays. Measure against the existing 0.14.8 artifacts: (1) speaker agreement on owner-corrected spans; (2) share of owner text corrections the new transcript already contains; (3) overlay re-anchor rate by time span within 0.5 s; (4) real-time factor when launched from a LaunchAgent versus a foreground Terminal; (5) median cosine between old-version and new-version embeddings of the same enrolled speaker. GO: neither 1 nor 2 regresses by more than 2 points and at least one improves by 5; 3 is at least 95%; 4 shows the background run no worse than 2x slower; 5 clears the roster's current match threshold. Any miss is a NO-GO that names the failing metric and keeps the pin.

**Risks.** Using existing corrections as ground truth only measures errors the owner noticed. Diff fatigue: nobody reviews 500 diffs, so auto-accept must be limited to meetings whose overlays are untouched and everything else keeps the old artifact. Published notes drift from re-derived local state. Summary re-derivation costs cloud tokens, so the default scope should be transcripts and speakers only. Storage for superseded versions.

**Considered before.** In pieces. Per-meeting Re-transcribe and re-finalize shipped; ASR4 (new P2) asks for a hand-built golden set, ASR6 (P3, amended 2026-11-12 to say a bump is now ready to qualify) asks for a qualified upgrade path, and FEAT19 has an apply-across-meetings leg. Nobody proposed a versioned, whole-archive derivation with time-keyed overlays, or using the corrections already in the library as the qualification set. What changed: the 2026-11-12 audit put numbers on the pin age, on F062, and on the open Neural Engine question.

- market-skeptic: Retained audio is the asset the market file calls distinctive in three segments because funded vendors discard it (https://www.granola.ai/pricing, https://www.notion.com/help/ai-meeting-notes). Bulk re-transcription alone is copyable; the 5 is for corrections carried through a re-derive and upgrades qualified on the owner's own corrections, and F062 blocks exactly that.
- codebase-engineer: Retained audio is the asset the funded leader chose not to keep (https://www.granola.ai/pricing), and the first step, re-anchoring overlays by time span, removes the F062 blocker while its first consumer qualifies the FluidAudio bump (ASR6). Keep scope to transcripts and speakers until F107, F019, G003 and F001 are fixed, and let I35 say when a new stack wins.
- owner-advocate: The one direction funded rivals have designed themselves out of: retained audio plus corrections as the negative, everything else a dated print. This quarter take only the derivation stamps, time-keyed overlay re-anchoring (the F062 fix) and the 0.18.3 qualification; no whole-archive run until the yardstick shows a win.

### I38. Reprint the archive: audio and corrections are the negative, every transcript and summary is a dated print

Lens personal-indispensability. Total 15.00 (differentiation 5, feasibility 3, fit 4, safety 3). Effort L. Picked by: none.

**Pitch.** Treat retained audio plus the owner's corrections as the only originals, and stamp every transcript, diarization, embedding set and summary with the engine, model revision and settings that produced it. When the yardstick says a new stack wins, an overnight job reprints the archive, keeps the previous print for one-step rollback, re-anchors human corrections by time and text, and leaves a change line per meeting (speakers moved, known terms recovered, opening seconds restored). Published notes are never touched automatically.

**Value to the owner.** A library that gets more accurate with age. In a year the spring meetings read as well as the autumn ones, the two thirds of meetings without voice embeddings gain them, and summaries from before July gain evidence anchors, without the owner redoing anything by hand.

**Why now.** Keeping the audio is the one thing the best-funded botless vendor chose not to do: Granola deletes audio after transcription and is studying expiry of verbatim transcripts (https://www.granola.ai/pricing, https://platformer.news/granola-chris-pedregal-interview), and Notion and ChatGPT discard it too (https://www.notion.com/help/ai-meeting-notes, https://www.sally.io/blog/chatgpt-record-mode-review). The speech stack is moving monthly (market digest, speech frontier segment: Nemotron 3 eight-speaker diarization, Parakeet fine-tunes, FluidAudio releases through 0.18.3 with long-form ASR fixes), so the value of a retained archive rises with every release, but only if reprocessing is safe.

**Builds on.** The lossless stereo FLAC archive, the per-meeting re-transcribe and re-finalize ratchet (mp finalize), run sidecars that already record engine and model, the overlay stores (speaker labels, transcript corrections), rebuildable FTS5 and embedding indexes, backup with a dry-run restore plan, the launchd scheduler, the event log as the audit trail of a batch, and the golden fixtures for contract checks.

**What the audit says.** Blockers, all in the weakest dimension (data handling, 2.5 of 5): F062 (index-keyed overlays applied with no drift check and dropped when a re-transcribe fails, so a bulk reprint would silently misplace or lose every hand correction), F001 and F020 (the reaper and the default uninstall delete only copies), F099 and F019 (merged meetings), and F017, F088 and F107 (republishing is destructive and ignores overlays, hence no automatic publish). Reproducibility: model weights float on main with no hash and the installed binary is two months behind the Python it drives (F021), so a print stamp is meaningless until both are pinned and recorded; 0.14.8 deletes its model cache and re-downloads on any load error, which a batch must not trigger. Background Neural Engine access is unmeasured, so overnight throughput in the agent is unknown. Helps: no path was found that silently loses captured audio, and writes are atomic where one writer owns a file.

**First probe.** Read-only inventory, then a reprint in a scratch copy; under two weeks, but the second half needs owner approval because a newer FluidAudio is a dependency change. Step 0 (one hour, script only): hours of archive that still have audio against total, split by workflow retention, and how many meetings carry a correction or speaker overlay. Step 1: copy 20 meetings spread across months and both languages to a scratch directory, re-transcribe with the current pin and with 0.18.3 in a throwaway foreground build under an isolated home and model cache, and score both with the yardstick. Step 2: on the copied meetings with overlays, re-anchor each correction by time window and original_text and count landed, missed and ambiguous. Registered bars: GO if at least 70% of archive hours still have audio, the newer stack improves at least one yardstick score by 20% relative with none worse by more than 1 point, at least 95% of overlays re-anchor with zero silent misapplications, and measured throughput puts the archive under three nights. NO-GO if under 40% of hours have audio (retention policy, not reprocessing, is then the real decision) or re-anchoring stays under 90%.

**Risks.** A reprint changes text under existing citations, Ask index entries and published notes, so the old print must be kept and the state shown as reprinted on a date, never swapped silently. A better model is still worse on some meetings, so rollback has to be per meeting and one action. Voice embeddings from different versions cannot be mixed: a reprint under a new runner rebuilds the roster in the same pass or leaves identity alone. Re-summarizing old meetings through the cloud engine costs money and re-sends old content, so the default reprint is speech only, with summaries on demand and each meeting's NDA or regulated flag read at reprint time. Without the yardstick it is churn.

**Considered before.** Partly. Per-meeting re-transcribe and re-finalize are wired (ASR3) and ASR6 says to qualify any bump on a golden set plus voiceprint compatibility. A whole-archive, versioned, reversible reprint is not in the record. A database of record was rejected (ADR 0003); this keeps files as the truth and only adds a print stamp to sidecars that already exist. What changed: upstream is four minor versions ahead with fixes aimed at long recordings, ASR6 was amended on 2026-11-12 to say a bump is ready to qualify, and the audit located the exact defect (F062) that makes bulk reprocessing unsafe today.

- market-skeptic: Duplicate of I16 with better sequencing (yardstick first, previous print kept for rollback). Same blockers: F062, floating model weights, and embedding compatibility across FluidAudio versions.
- codebase-engineer: Same thesis as I16 and the more carefully scoped of the two: speech only by default, published notes never touched, an inventory of surviving audio first. It shares the F062 blocker and the dependency on I35.
- owner-advocate: The same direction as I16 in the better shape: speech-only by default, previous print kept for rollback, published notes never touched, gated on the yardstick. Use this shape when I16 is built.

### I32. Upstream first: give FluidAudio the background-agent, long-form, bilingual regression case instead of extracting anything

Lens open-source-community. Total 14.67 (differentiation 1.67, feasibility 4, fit 5, safety 4). Effort S. Picked by: owner-advocate.

**Pitch.** The largest single-vendor exposure is FluidAudio, pinned five months back with no fallback engine. Contribute what this app has and most of the showcase apps do not: a measurement of Neural Engine dispatch from inside a menu-bar agent on macOS 27.2, timings and uncovered-speech counts for 60 to 120 minute stereo meetings, and Ukrainian plus code-switched counts. The deliverable is issues with numbers and one small tool (a compute-plan dump for the four models) that upstream maintains, not the owner.

**Value to the owner.** A safe path off the 0.14.8 pin, and an answer to the question the audit left open: does the agent still get the Neural Engine on this OS. After that, upstream's fixes are tested against a configuration that looks like this app, which is the cheapest regression protection a one-person project can get.

**Why now.** FluidAudio ships fast, and its recent releases carry macOS 27 fixes for the CTC and Kokoro paths, not the TDT path this app uses, plus long-form ASR fixes in v0.18.2 (https://github.com/FluidInference/FluidAudio/releases). It is the engine behind about 50 listed apps (https://github.com/FluidInference/FluidAudio), so a downstream that reports measured numbers is uncommon signal for its maintainers. Parakeet v3's code-switching weakness has no upstream workaround (https://github.com/NVIDIA-NeMo/Speech/discussions/14620), and the owner's meetings are that case.

**Builds on.** daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/FluidAudioRunner.swift; the audit's foreground MLComputePlan reading (encoder 1,381 ops on the Neural Engine and 4 on CPU, decoder and joint on CPU, wespeaker 1,149 ops on GPU); `valid1_check.py --timings` and `--attribution` (uncovered speech); pipeline_events.jsonl stage timings; ASR6's task file.

**What the audit says.** Helps: the audit lists the pin and the unmeasured agent dispatch among the two facts worth knowing today, notes upstream issue 738 quoting Apple on restricted background Neural Engine access, that 0.14.8 predates every upstream macOS 27 fix, that weights float on main unhashed, and that a model load error deletes the cache and re-downloads from huggingface.co. Blocks: FluidAudioRunner is at 44.7% coverage with no inference test, daemon/Package.resolved is untracked, and the installed app is a 2026-09-11 binary driving HEAD Python (F021), so a measurement means something only on a fresh build.

**First probe.** Three days, counts and seconds only. Add an env-gated debug path that runs MLComputePlan for the four models from inside the running menu-bar agent and logs op counts per compute device; transcribe the same 3 owner-chosen files (about 10, 60 and 120 minutes) in the foreground and in the agent, on 0.14.8 and on 0.18.3 in a scratch checkout. Pass: agent Neural Engine op share within 5 percentage points of the foreground reading, agent real-time factor within 20% of foreground, and 0.18.3 does not increase uncovered-speech seconds on any file. All three pass: bump the pin and close ASR6's open question. Any miss becomes one upstream issue with the table attached, and the pin stays with a dated reason. Either way the open question ends inside the window.

**Risks.** Upstream may not act. It makes public that the app runs an old pin. Three files are a small sample, and choosing them needs the owner. A debug path inside the agent is new code in a thinly covered area.

**Considered before.** ASR6 (open) tracks the single-vendor exposure and wants a qualified upgrade path; ADR 0007 rejected a second ASR engine and accepted having no fallback. Contributing upstream as the mitigation is not in the record. What changed: macOS 27's background accelerator restriction and four upstream minor releases since the pin.

- market-skeptic: Pure maintenance, but three days answer the audit's open Neural Engine question and unstick a pin four minor versions behind. Fold into I35 and run only on a fresh build.
- codebase-engineer: Three days, answers the audit's open question (does the menu-bar agent get the Neural Engine on 27.2) and either moves the pin or dates the reason it stays. It needs a fresh build, since the installed binary is from 2026-09-11 (F021), and a throwaway adaptation for the 0.18 API.
- owner-advocate: Three days to answer the audit's open question (does the menu-bar agent still get the Neural Engine on 27.2) and to qualify the 0.18.3 bump, with nothing new to own afterwards. If the agent has lost the accelerator every other plan re-orders, so run it first.

### I42. Flip the default on the secondary surfaces: deleted on a date unless a local witness shows use

Lens contrarian-delete. Total 14.67 (differentiation 1.67, feasibility 4, fit 5, safety 4). Effort M. Picked by: codebase-engineer, owner-advocate.

**Pitch.** FEAT18 has waited since 2026-10-24 for a per-surface opinion. Replace the opinion with a rule. App Intents, the LAN sink, the Facts rail, the prep card and series continuity are deleted now, on evidence already recorded. Every other secondary surface (digests, Meeting time, Tutorial, Guide reader, People rail, resume-after-end and merge, the CLI-only dev subcommands) gets 14 days to show use in the local event log and is deleted if it does not.

**Value to the owner.** Frees at least 5,000 source and 3,500 test lines by FEAT18's dedicated-file count (the architecture review's wider count is about 10K, 11% of production code), plus their Preferences rows, GUIDE recipes and tutorial anchors, and closes UX74, UX75, CAL3, DV5, AUTO1 and G035 as moot. It removes F014, a privacy leak, together with a feature the owner already said makes no sense. If resume and merge fail the witness, two more highs (F046, F019) and a fix that does not hold (F099) go with them. The owner stops carrying a decision table and gets a smaller app whose remaining rows are ones actually opened.

**Why now.** The second layer is where everyone else is piling in with teams behind them: Granola shipped Briefs, Recipes and a daily Agent brief in 2026 (https://granola.ai/updates) and a local connector already lists 34 tools (https://claude.com/connectors/minutes-conversation-memory). A solo tool's half-used versions of the same surfaces are cost without distinction, and the market file's own verdict is that more parity features will not make this product distinctive.

**Builds on.** the repo root docs/backlog/tasks/FEAT18.md (LOC and evidence per surface), DV5's measurement (16 of 1,374 actions ever resolved), the repo root pipeline/src/mp/summarize.py line 271 (`_prior_meeting_context`, the series-continuity block), the fenced event log and `mp logs`, existing witness rows (`digest_generated`, `mcp_tool_called`, `automation` command, `detail` toolbar_action, `recording_resumed_after_end`, `meetings_merged`), and TutorialFenceTests plus the config truth fence, which make a delete commit safe.

**What the audit says.** Helps: F014 (high, series continuity sends a withheld meeting's content to the cloud), G035 (tutorial step points at a control that is not rendered), G015, F047 and F075 (schedule toggles shelling out on main), and conditionally F046, F019, F099. Blocks: the event log has no row for opening a rail or window, it rotates at about 5 MiB with 3 backups, and it holds 7,279 test-residue rows, so the witness needs one new local row and every count must pass through `mp.events.drop_test_residue`.

**First probe.** Day 1: add one `library` action, `surface_opened`, with a `surface` attribute, emitted on a deliberate open of Digests, Meeting time, People, Guide and Tutorial; it is written to events.jsonl on this Mac and nowhere else. Days 2 to 15: normal use. Day 15, owner-run, counts only, residue-filtered: a surface with fewer than 3 opens in 14 days gets its delete commit; 3 to 9 goes behind the Experimental switch; 10 or more stays. Resume and merge are deleted if `recording_resumed_after_end` plus `meetings_merged` total fewer than 3 real rows across all retained generations. The idea fails as a whole if more than half of the witnessed surfaces score 10 or more.

**Risks.** Fourteen days can miss a monthly habit such as a digest, so a surface cut on a low count must stay one revert away. The witness row must never leave the Mac or it becomes the telemetry the project forbids. Self-voiceprint is load-bearing and is excluded. Deleting the Tutorial reverses a scope decision the owner made deliberately on 2026-08-18.

**Considered before.** Yes. FEAT18 (new P2, an open owner decision with nothing decided), DV5 (Facts, with Remove pre-registered and blocked on the owner), CAL3 and AI6 (series key found wrong, fix deferred P3), PIPE20 (dev tooling, delete or dated verdict). What changed: the 2026-11-12 audit found verified defects inside the undecided surfaces (F014 high, G035, G015, F047, F075, and F046, F019, F099 in resume and merge), so waiting now has a measured cost. The new content is the default flip and the witness, not the table.

- market-skeptic: No market value and high owner value: a dated rule in place of an open decision, and it removes F014 together with a feature the owner called senseless. Keep the witness row strictly local and exempt the People rail.
- codebase-engineer: Verified the series-continuity block at summarize.py line 271: deleting it removes F014 along with a feature the owner called senseless. Cut what the record already condemns now (Facts, prep card, App Intents, LAN sink), witness the rest with a local row, and leave the Tutorial out, since that was a deliberate owner decision.
- owner-advocate: Replaces a decision table the owner has carried since October with a rule and a local witness, removes F014 together with a feature the owner said makes no sense, and frees review time for everything else. Keep each cut one revert away and keep the witness row on this Mac.

### I06. Quote-only minutes under regulated mode: no generated sentence in the record

Lens privacy-regulated. Total 14.66 (differentiation 4, feasibility 3.33, fit 3.33, safety 4). Effort M. Picked by: market-skeptic.

**Pitch.** Under regulated or NDA policy, change what the local model may produce instead of waiting for it to improve: it only selects transcript spans and tags them from a closed vocabulary (decision, action, question, risk). The minute is assembled from verbatim quotes with speaker and timestamp, each passing the existing evidence verifier; anything unverifiable is dropped and the drop count is shown. The failure mode moves from fabrication, measured at 0.29 against a 0.05 gate for local prose, to omission, which a professional can defend. The header states the method in plain words: selected on this Mac, text is verbatim.

**Value to the owner.** A lawyer or clinician cannot file a small model's paraphrase but can file an indexed set of verbatim, playable quotes. For the owner, NDA meetings stop receiving a visibly weaker summary and receive a different, honest artifact. It also gives the unwired staged summarizer either a job or a deletion date.

**Why now.** Vendors of local tools admit small models hallucinate, miss action items and confuse speakers, and nobody publishes measured faithfulness (market file, local segment; no URL carried). The file's consent segment calls the bring-your-own-key path the weak point for privileged or clinical material and says a hard nothing-leaves switch is worth more to those users than any summary feature. The complaint that AI notes are treated as the record while wrong is documented (https://venture-lab.org/2026/copilot-meeting-notes-wrong/), and accurate names and numbers rank first among user priorities (https://gladia.io/meeting-assistant-market-map); a verbatim quote keeps the name and the number exactly as transcribed, one click from the audio.

**Builds on.** `evidence.py` (quotes re-checked against the transcript before writing), the extract stage of `summarize_staged.py` (about 1,000 lines, unwired, awaiting a PIPE20 verdict), the `MeetingSummary` schema's evidence fields, workflow extra sections, the dogfood harness with its held-out split and graded correction corpus, `effective_backend` as the place the mode switches, deep links to the second.

**What the audit says.** Helps: evidence verification and the fail-closed predicate held up under adversarial reading. Blocks: the Apple Intelligence summarizer is a second implementation that skips evidence verification (F108, F125) and must be refused in this mode; F107 means a corrected quote publishes uncorrected, which disqualifies a verbatim record until fixed; SEC28 matters more here because the quotes are other people's words written straight into Markdown sinks; F081 (language-check false positives trigger discarded re-summaries) and F079 (context ceiling) hit long meetings; G034 (regulated mode's sink behaviour contradicts its own copy). Transcript accuracy is unmeasured (ASR4 open), so a verbatim quote of a mis-hearing is still wrong, only checkable.

**First probe.** 7 days, owner-run on the existing dogfood held-out split with the staged extract stage and compose turned off; no new data. Pre-registered on at least 15 meetings: every published item passes the evidence check (fabrication 0 by construction, verified); decision recall and action recall against the graded cloud reference both 0.60 or higher; owner-graded relevance of a 100-quote sample 0.80 or higher; wall time at most twice the current local path. GO if all hold. Recall under 0.45: close it and delete `summarize_staged.py` under PIPE20. Between 0.45 and 0.60: one retry with the LOCAL13 model refresh, then close. If the split has fewer than 15 meetings, run on all graded meetings and treat the result as provisional with no build decision.

**Risks.** Quote lists read worse than prose and the owner may simply not want them, so try them on real NDA meetings before any interface work. Selection is harder on Ukrainian and code-switched transcripts. Verbatim records preserve speculation and jokes, which the market file flags as a liability for some users, so this pairs with retention classes. The recall bars lean on a provisional, LLM-graded baseline the project itself flagged for an owner spot-check.

**Considered before.** Adjacent decisions exist and this respects them. Making the local model the silent default was rejected (hallucination 0.29 against 0.05) and stays rejected; the staged extract, compose and verify pipeline is built but unmeasured since July (PIPE20, LOCAL14). This reuses only the extract half under a different output contract and makes no quality claim for local prose. Nothing changed in the model; the question changes from whether the local summary is good enough to whether the record can be limited to what was verifiably said.

- market-skeptic: Moves the local model's failure from fabrication to omission by reusing the evidence verifier, which answers the weak local summary every local vendor admits to and nobody measures. Cheap probe on existing data that also settles PIPE20; recall may well miss the 0.60 bar.
- codebase-engineer: evidence.py is sound, but the build stands on the extract stage of summarize_staged.py (994 lines behind a flag nothing sets, unmeasured since July) and its first step is an owner-run dogfood bench of the kind that has stalled since then. If it runs, give it one dated verdict that also settles PIPE20, and fix F107 before any quote is called verbatim.
- owner-advocate: An honest artifact for NDA meetings that moves the failure from fabrication to omission, and it gives summarize_staged a job or a deletion date. It depends on an owner-graded bench of the kind unrun since July, so give it one dated 7-day run and otherwise delete the staged file under I44.

### I37. What I said I would do: a ledger of the owner's own commitments, tested against the mic channel and playable in the owner's own voice

Lens personal-indispensability. Total 14.66 (differentiation 4, feasibility 3.33, fit 3.33, safety 4). Effort M. Picked by: market-skeptic.

**Pitch.** Offer the owner one candidate answer to DV5's open question instead of an inbox of everyone's actions: what did I say I would do, and can I hear myself say it. An action whose verified evidence quote sits in audio where the mic channel dominates is, on the hypothesis the yardstick tests, the owner's own, with no diarization label, roster name or owner field involved. The surface is a short list, each row the sentence, the date and a play button, with rows ageing out visibly unless kept and one key to close. Other people's commitments stay where they already are, on People.

**Value to the owner.** The only action list a person works from is their own promises. If the bar holds, about 1,500 open actions nobody owns become a handful a week that are the owner's, each checkable by ear in five seconds.

**Why now.** The market digest (agent and MCP segment, no URL in the digest) found commitment tracking to be a workflow and not a stateful feature in every tool read, and action items pinned on the wrong person are a leading complaint about platform notes (https://venture-lab.org/2026/copilot-meeting-notes-wrong/). Tools that discard audio cannot offer playback of the promise at all (https://www.granola.ai/pricing). Inside the project, DV5 is blocked on the owner with Remove as the registered default, so a decision is pending either way, and evidence anchors (shipped 2026-07-24) now give actions a timestamp that can be tested against the channel.

**Builds on.** ActionItem.evidence in pipeline/src/mp/schemas.py (quote and t, re-verified against the transcript before writing), the stereo archive and per-channel RMS code, the meetingpipe deep link that opens audio at a timestamp, the resolved field, the Library's ageing labels, the existing action clustering for restated items, the visibility gate, the weekly digest as the one push channel, and the People rail for everyone else's commitments.

**What the audit says.** F014 is the cautionary case: a cross-meeting aggregate that walked around the per-meeting gate and sent a withheld meeting's actions to the cloud; this projection has to go through visibility.py and stay local. DIAR6 is why the transcript's me label is not good enough and channel energy at the anchor is needed.
