G015 shows open actions get orphaned on rename, one more reason to key on channel and not on a name. F107 and F017 say not to write the ledger into published Notion pages. Tests: several existing tests pin a defect and not the intent, so the classification rule needs a real-audio fixture and not a source-text fence.

**First probe.** Script only, owner-run, read-only, two to three days, counts only, walked through DV5's registered thresholds in order. Step 0 measures what DV5 only inferred: the share of actions carrying an evidence anchor in summaries written since 2026-07-24 (it was 17.6% over the whole library on 08-19). Step 1, for anchored actions in stereo meetings, compares mic and system RMS in a six-second window around t in the consumed artifact and classes each as mine, theirs, both or unknown. Step 2 prints mine per meeting (median, p90), mine per week, and how many restate an earlier one. Step 3 is fifteen minutes of owner time: 30 random mine rows shown locally, each graded on whether the owner committed to it and would have wanted it back. Registered bars: T1 holds only if anchors cover at least 60% of post-July actions and at least 60% of those are classifiable; at least 85% of the 30 are real commitments and at least 40% wanted; the weekly count is at most 25. T2 is met by leaving others' commitments on People. T3 is tested by putting the same question to Ask for two sample weeks and counting what it misses. T5 still needs the owner to say this is a question they have. Any miss is NO-GO, and NO-GO carries out DV5's default: remove the Facts rail and close the ledger idea for this owner.

**Risks.** The summarizer may extract musings from the owner's speech as actions; the hand grade measures exactly that. Coverage starts in late July unless older meetings are re-summarized. Closure stays manual: detecting kept or slipped from later meetings is a silent-wrong inference and is left out on purpose. The mic channel carries someone else when a colleague leans in or the owner is on speakers, and muted asides are zero-filled, so the list is of things said to the call. Ask may turn out to answer it well enough, in which case T3 says build nothing.

**Considered before.** Yes. The Facts rail shipped (DV1) and is under review with Remove pre-registered (DV5): 16 of 1,374 actions resolved on 08-10, and on 08-19 named owners at 36.9%, due dates at 4.6% and evidence at 17.6%; FEAT21 counted 570 of 1,126 owned actions as diarization artifacts; the owner called it barely useful. What changed: this is not a reshaped inbox. It is the one slice DV5 did not measure, the owner's own commitments selected by channel instead of by a name, made testable by evidence anchors that postdate most of the library, and its failure delivers the evidence for Remove.

- market-skeptic: The owner's own commitments, selected by mic channel and playable, is something tools that discard audio cannot offer (https://www.granola.ai/pricing). Cheap probe that settles DV5 either way; the summarizer extracting musings as promises is the risk.
- codebase-engineer: Channel energy at the anchor is a sound selector, but anchors exist only since 2026-07-24 (17.6% of actions when DV5 measured, as cited) and extraction quality is unchanged. Its fail action is DV5's Remove, which I42 reaches without another owner-graded probe.
- owner-advocate: The narrowest possible retest of the commitments idea, selected by channel instead of by name, and a miss carries out DV5's Remove. The prior is poor (1.2% resolved) and it shares the yardstick's channel hypothesis, so run it as a rider on I35 and build no surface this quarter.

### I36. Voice census: name a voice once and the archive resolves

Lens personal-indispensability. Total 14.34 (differentiation 3.67, feasibility 3, fit 4.67, safety 3). Effort L. Picked by: market-skeptic, codebase-engineer, owner-advocate.

**Pitch.** Turn enrolment from a click per meeting into a short queue of recurring strangers. Cluster every unnamed per-meeting voice embedding across the library, rank the clusters by how many meetings each would resolve, and show one row per cluster: this voice is in 23 meetings, three short clips, one name field, applied everywhere as a reversible overlay. Because one person talks to a small stable set of colleagues, a later step can replace the open-set cosine gate with a closed-set scorer over that named set with an explicit unknown class, fed by per-segment embeddings taken from the system channel alone so the owner's own speech never contaminates a colleague's print.

**Value to the owner.** A quarter of an hour of naming, once, should put names on most recurring voices across the archive, and new meetings then arrive already named. That is the owner's stated pain, and it is the gate on everything person-keyed: People, action owners, Ask citations and the published attendee list stop reading THEM-A.

**Why now.** Speaker identification is among the top-ranked user priorities (https://gladia.io/meeting-assistant-market-map), users of local tools still call diarization and speaker names unreliable (https://hn.algolia.com/api/v1/items/50454625), and a cloud vendor's speaker recognition is the subject of surviving BIPA voiceprint claims (https://uctoday.com/productivity-automation/otter-ai-fails-to-dismiss-core-privacy-claims-in-u-s-court/), so funded players have a legal reason not to go deep on cross-meeting voiceprints of non-users. Inside the project, FEAT17 measured that enrolment, not the suggestion surface, is the constraint: 270 of 337 declined labels scored under the 0.50 suggestion floor, with roster people carrying one or two samples each.

**Builds on.** <stem>.embeddings.json (schema 2, one 256-d vector per final label), roster.json with k-means centroids and the two-gate rule (0.65 top, 0.07 margin) in pipeline/src/mp/roster.py, FEAT14 near misses, the reversible speaker-label overlay, the VoiceResolution cast strip, the People rail, audio playback at a timestamp for the clips, FEAT19's planned legs (picker, merge, play sample, apply across meetings), and docs/spikes/feat17_roster_near_miss.py as the probe template.

**What the audit says.** Must be fixed first, because one name now fans out to many meetings: G003 (undoing one name in one meeting deletes the person's whole roster voiceprint), G015 (rename everywhere leaves the person's open actions behind), F019 (merge-meetings concatenates per-meeting labels as if they were global). F107 and F017 limit the payoff downstream: publishers ignore speaker overlays and a Notion republish wipes the page, so resolved names reach published notes only after those are fixed. Helps: overlays are reversible by design and the matcher is biased toward unknown. Sensitivity: the capability map notes unencrypted backups and a default library in iCloud-synced Documents, and voiceprints are the most sensitive data in it.

**First probe.** Script only, owner-run, read-only, stdlib, two to three days: over every *.embeddings.json plus roster.json, printing counts and never a name. It prints its denominators first (FEAT17 found 83 sidecars for 249 summaries; the roster is 11 or 15 people depending on the source). Part A, separability: for each named person present in two or more meetings, same-person cross-meeting cosines against different-person cosines, reported as an equal error rate. Part B, census: average-link clustering of all non-owner vectors at that threshold, reporting clusters that span three or more meetings, purity against the named subset with names hidden, and the share of unnamed speaker appearances that fall into the 15 largest clusters. Registered bars: GO if the equal error rate is at most 10% and the top 15 clusters cover at least half of unnamed appearances at purity of at least 0.95. MARGINAL at 10 to 20%: the per-meeting mean is too noisy, and the next leg persists the per-segment embeddings the pinned runner already computes before it averages them. NO-GO above 20%: cross-meeting identity from these embeddings is closed for this audio, and names have to come from FEAT20's participant list. MATCH_MIN and SUGGEST_MIN are not tuned to move the verdict.

**Risks.** A wrong cluster merge spreads one wrong name over many meetings; nothing is named without the owner hearing clips and confirming, and the whole fan-out must undo in one action. Today's vectors are means over a mixdown of both channels (DIAR6), so they may be too contaminated to cluster, which is what the probe decides. Two thirds of meetings have no sidecar and need re-diarization from retained audio first. Embeddings from two FluidAudio versions must never be mixed: a bump means rebuilding the roster in the same pass, and whether the 0.18 span-embedding API yields compatible vectors is an a-priori check, as ASR6 already requires. A closed-set scorer can overfit a handful of samples, so the two-gate rule stays until the yardstick shows a win.

**Considered before.** Partly. The roster is adopted (33% of voices auto-named), FEAT19 (new P2) lists passive enrolment, picker, merge, play sample and apply across meetings, and FEAT20's participant-list spike is unrun. Not found in the record: unsupervised clustering of unnamed voices as the enrolment queue, a closed-set scorer, or system-channel-only voiceprints. It suggests and the owner confirms, so it stays inside the rule against silent inference. What changed: FEAT17's measurement that enrolment is the bottleneck, which turns the question from tuning the matcher to feeding it.

- market-skeptic: Speaker memory is no longer rare (Talat, Daisy, Transcripted) and platform notes get identity from the host, so this is catch-up done well, not a moat. It is the owner's stated pain and the probe is two days read-only; G003 must be fixed before one name fans out to many meetings.
- codebase-engineer: The owner's stated pain, with enrolment the measured bottleneck (FEAT17, as cited); step one is fixing G003, G015 and F019, already on the audit's fix-first list, because one name fans out to many meetings. Then a three-day stdlib probe decides whether today's vectors, duration-weighted means (verified in FluidAudioRunner.swift) over a mixdown (DIAR6), cluster at all.
- owner-advocate: Attacks the owner's stated pain at its measured bottleneck (enrolment, not matching) and compounds for one person with a stable set of colleagues. The 2 to 3 day separability probe decides it; G003, G015 and F019 must be fixed before one name fans out to many meetings.

### I45. Archive first, notes on request: stop summarizing and publishing every meeting by default

Lens contrarian-delete. Total 14.34 (differentiation 3.67, feasibility 3.67, fit 3, safety 4). Effort M. Picked by: market-skeptic. Radical.

**Pitch.** The automatic pipeline ends at a speaker-labelled transcript on disk. A summary is written when the owner asks for one (a click in the Library, a URL verb, an MCP call) or for the few workflows explicitly set to always. The product's promise changes from notes appear to nothing is lost, and anything can be asked later, with the speakers already named and the best model available at that moment.

**Value to the owner.** Frees the default cloud egress of every transcript, which is the largest gap in the privacy story (the default engine is cloud Anthropic), the cost and heat of summaries nobody opens, FEAT21 (a note cannot go out under diarization labels if it is written after naming), most Notion republishes and most long-meeting paste bundles. The owner gets fewer, better notes and a quieter app whose strongest claim, the kept audio, becomes the centre of the product.

**Why now.** The note is the commodity: it is bundled into Zoom, Slack, Notion and Google plans (https://www.zoom.com/en/products/ai-assistant/features/ai-note-taking). The funded leader deletes audio after transcription and is studying expiring verbatim transcripts (https://www.granola.ai/pricing, https://platformer.news/granola-chris-pedregal-interview), so it has to summarize eagerly; a tool that keeps the audio can afford to be lazy, and a vendor whose product is the note would not dare. Users' top complaints are wrong names and speakers in notes that get treated as the record (https://gladia.io/meeting-assistant-market-map, https://venture-lab.org/2026/copilot-meeting-notes-wrong/), which summarizing before anyone can name a voice produces by construction.

**Builds on.** The existing `byo` summary mode in the repo root daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/State.swift (it already stops the pipeline at the transcript), the pipeline lane's library-run jobs and Regenerate, the voiceprint roster and reversible overlays, evidence anchors, the MCP server and URL scheme as request surfaces, and per-workflow routing (an always flag is one more field in the Workflow contract). The channel-separated corpus with embeddings is what makes late summarization strictly better informed than early.

**What the audit says.** Helps: shrinks exposure to F014, F017, F088, F081 (re-summaries that are thrown away) and F079, and makes F107 less harmful because naming precedes publishing. Blocks: F023 (Regenerate and Retry always fail for Apple Intelligence meetings) and F009 (Library targets dropped on first open) sit on the on-request path and must hold first; digests, Ask context and the MCP action list get thinner when fewer summaries exist.

**First probe.** Fourteen days, a reversible setting, nothing deleted. Record every meeting in one or two chosen workflows in the existing `byo` mode; on day 1 verify that Regenerate runs on such a meeting, and if it does not, that gap is the probe's only code. Witness, owner-run, counts only: recordings in those workflows against `pipeline` run_started rows for the same stems within 3 working days. Pass (make on-request the default, keep always per workflow): summaries requested for under 50% of meetings. Fail (keep automatic, close the idea): 80% or more. In between: on-request by default, always for any workflow at 80% or more.

**Risks.** Hands-off notes are the stated core job and the owner may simply want them. Latency moves to the moment of need (about a minute on cloud, several on local). A meeting never summarized is invisible to digests and action lists. The habit of naming speakers first may not form, in which case the benefit to attribution does not arrive.

**Considered before.** Not as a default. The nearest records are FEAT21 (hold summarize and publish until speakers are named, opt-in per workflow, new P2) and the long-meeting gate, which already declines to summarize automatically. What changed since FEAT21 was filed: the 2026-11-12 audit shows the eager path is where the cross-meeting privacy leak and the destructive republish live (F014, F017, F088), and the market file dates the note's commoditization to 2026 bundling.

- market-skeptic: A stance a vendor selling notes cannot take: keep everything, summarize on request with speakers already named, and stop sending every transcript to a cloud engine by default. It contradicts the stated core job, so the 14-day reversible setting should decide.
- codebase-engineer: The mechanism exists (BYO mode and its URL verb verified) and the trial is reversible, but hands-off notes are the stated core job, the on-request path sits on F023 and F009, and search, Ask, digests and MCP all thin out without summaries. FEAT21's opt-in hold gets the naming benefit without changing the promise.
- owner-advocate: The cheapest radical experiment on the list: a reversible setting on one or two workflows and a 14-day count. It reverses the stated core job and depends on a naming habit and an Ask path that are weak today, so treat it as a trial, not a direction.

### I47. Shrink sensing to what the log has seen: Teams starts by itself, everything else is a hotkey

Lens contrarian-delete. Total 14.34 (differentiation 1.67, feasibility 4.67, fit 4, safety 4). Effort S. Picked by: none.

**Pitch.** Delete every detector path, title matcher and mute adapter with zero real firings in the retained event log, starting with browser-tab detection and any native app the owner has never recorded. The product then says plainly what is true: one app is detected and ended automatically, and anything else is recorded with the hotkey. MeetingPipeCore's observer bus, promotion engine and event log stay; only unmeasured catalogue entries and the code paths around them go.

**Value to the owner.** Frees DET6 and MIC7 (a probe and a browser extension for a path with zero recorded meetings), the browser admission gate, the one allow-listed skipped test (window_titles.json holds 21 seeded guesses and 0 captured rows), and part of the idle discovery cost (F038). The END band, where six fixes do not hold, narrows to one app and one teardown oracle, which is the only scope in which an event-log replay harness can make hands-off recording true. The owner gets a headline promise that is smaller and kept.

**Why now.** Automatic detection is still missing or paywalled in the open-source rivals (Meetily gates it to Pro and has several open requests, https://github.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meetily), so reliable detection for the one app that matters remains worth having, while generality across apps depends on scraping other vendors' interfaces, which the platform keeps moving (a macOS 27 change in October 2026 broke audio taps lacking a new permission, https://github.com/elien666/diarize/pull/3). A sensing layer that claims less and is right is a better asset than one that claims more.

**Builds on.** MeetingPipeCore (90% line coverage, strict concurrency, pure decide functions), events.jsonl with per-bundle `recording_started` rows and `lifecycle` ended rows carrying `confirmed_by`, `mp analyze-detection`, the repo root daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/Resources/meeting_apps.toml, and ADR 0017's tier table.

**What the audit says.** Helps: F038 directly; F002, F031, G006, G024 and G025 all sit in fusion across signal classes, which may get simpler with fewer classes but are not removed by this. The capability map already records that matchers are English-interface Teams only and that browser detection admits Google Meet alone. Blocks: the log rotates at about 5 MiB with 3 backups, so never seen means not in the retained window; the counts must be printed with the earliest row's date.

**First probe.** One day, owner-run, counts only. Group real `recording_started` rows by bundle id across all retained generations (CONVENTIONS records no test residue on that action; report the earliest row's date beside the totals) and count `browser_tab_rejected` rows by bundle. Pass: one bundle holds at least 90% of recordings and browser recordings are 0; delete browser detection and every catalogue entry with zero rows. Fail: any second source holds at least 10%, or there is at least 1 browser recording; keep that source and delete only the zero-row entries.

**Risks.** A job change or a client on another platform means re-adding detection later, from captured titles instead of guesses. It trims the story of MeetingPipeCore as a general presence layer for other products. Catalogue entries are cheap data; the saving is in the code paths and special cases around them, so the gain may be smaller than the line count suggests.

**Considered before.** Partly. DET6 (browser probe shipped, measurement owner-owed), MIC7 (extension feasibility GO, build deferred because the owner has zero recorded browser meetings) and DET15 (English-only matchers) all defer building; none proposes deleting the paths. What changed: the 2026-11-12 audit found the fixture behind them holds no real captures and found six END fixes not holding, so breadth is being carried while depth is not yet true.

- market-skeptic: Claims less and is right, for a one-day count. The gain is in special cases removed, not in lines, and never seen only means not in the retained log window.
- codebase-engineer: One count over the event log decides it, and the cut removes the browser admission path, the seeded-guess fixture with its skipped test, and part of F038, narrowing END work to the one app the owner records. Report the earliest retained row beside the counts, since never seen only means not in the window.
- owner-advocate: A one-day count that lets the product promise less and keep it, and narrows the END band to the one app a replay harness can cover. The saving may be smaller than the line count suggests, and a job change means re-adding detection from captured titles.

### I26. Listen-only shape: lectures and all-hands get an outline, not invented action items

Lens beyond-meetings. Total 14.33 (differentiation 3, feasibility 4, fit 3.33, safety 4). Effort S. Picked by: none.

**Pitch.** Measure, from the two channels, how much of a recording the owner actually spoke. When it is close to nothing, the recording is a lecture, webinar, all-hands or a talk playing on the Mac, and it is routed to an outline schema: topics with timestamps, claims, references, questions it raised for me. Attendees, decisions and owned actions are not asked for. The routing is shown on the meeting and reversible in one click, and it is a physical measurement of the owner's own channel, not an inferred meeting type.

**Value to the owner.** Lecture-shaped recordings get lecture notes with jump-to-second links, and the cross-meeting action list stops filling with items nobody owns (970 open actions was 'a dump, not a review').

**Why now.** Notes treated as the record while assigning actions to the wrong person is the leading complaint about platform notetakers (https://venture-lab.org/2026/copilot-meeting-notes-wrong/). File transcribers market to lectures but know nothing about the listener (https://apps.apple.com/au/app/aiko/id1672085276). No source in the digest shows a notetaker changing schema on who actually spoke.

**Builds on.** The channel-separated WAV and diarize.assign_speakers_by_channel; the rule in diarize.py that nobody is named 'me' when the owner sat silent; MeetingStats, which already derives the owner's talk share from the diarized transcript; per-workflow summary sections (WF7); evidence anchors and deep links; the manual hotkey for a talk playing on the Mac.

**What the audit says.** Helps: the modelled contracts (meta sidecar, summary field set) are the healthy part of the seam, and this is one routing key plus one prompt. Blocks: DIAR6 (a quiet mic merges 30 to 39% remote speech into 'me', and ASR runs on an unnormalised mixdown) makes the diarized talk share untrustworthy, so the measurement should be mic-channel voice activity counted only where the system channel is quiet or clearly lower, which also handles loudspeaker bleed. UX74 (durations derived from file mtime, P1 partial) means the denominator must come from the audio frame count. A second schema widens the mirrored-rule count (about 18, of which 10 are fixture-pinned), and the Apple Intelligence summarizer is already a drifted second copy (F108, F125).

**First probe.** 4 days, owner-run, counts only, no product code. A script over the library computes per recording the owner's talk share as defined above and prints the distribution in buckets, the number of recordings under 2%, and how many action items and decisions those recordings produced. Owner then reviews the actions from the 10 most recent listen-only recordings and marks each real-for-me or not. GO: at least 8% of recordings are under 2% owner talk share and at least 80% of their actions are not real for the owner. MARGINAL: the share holds but 50 to 80% are not real, so keep the meeting schema and only add a visible 'you did not speak' marker. NO-GO: under 3% of recordings, or more than half of those actions are real; close.

**Risks.** An all-hands can still carry a real action for the owner, so the outline must keep an 'asks of me' section and the reroute must be one click. On loudspeakers without headphones, bleed inflates the mic channel. A threshold is a cliff: recordings just above it get the full meeting schema. It is also a small step toward typed recordings, which the project has refused whenever the type was inferred.

**Considered before.** Two rejections sit close. AI5 (automatic meeting-type classification) was a measured NO-GO, and its own conclusion was that the gate is a better input, not a better classifier: it tested model-invented titles, workflow tags and attendee counts, and docs/spikes/ai5-meeting-type-classification.md never mentions channel talk share. Talk-time coaching was skipped as out of register, with one self-directed talk-time view shipped. What changed: this is a different input (measured on the owner's own channel) used to route a schema, with no chart and no score.

- market-skeptic: Small and honest: routing by measured owner talk share appears in no rival and avoids the inferred meeting types the project refused. Easy to copy and too small to be a direction, but cheap and it cuts action noise.
- codebase-engineer: Small and reversible: per-channel RMS helpers exist in diarize.py and per-workflow sections already ship. It must measure raw channel energy, not the diarized share (DIAR6), and its main payoff, fewer unowned actions, mostly disappears if the Facts rail is removed.
- owner-advocate: Small, physical and reversible, with a 4-day counts-only probe. Its main payoff (fewer invented actions) shrinks if the Facts rail is deleted and a second schema widens the mirrored-rule count, so run the count inside the yardstick script and decide then.

### I31. Egress receipts: publish a zero-egress conformance kit that this product currently fails

Lens open-source-community. Total 14.33 (differentiation 4, feasibility 3.67, fit 3.33, safety 3.33). Effort M. Picked by: none.

**Pitch.** Turn the local-first claim into a black-box test any recorder can be run under. One fixture meeting goes through summarize and publish inside a sandbox that denies all non-loopback network, with a socket-connect recorder attached, and the run leaves an egress receipt beside the meeting: stage, destination host, bytes, and the policy that allowed it. Publish the kit, the receipt format and this app's own results, red rows included. The reference architecture is then three plain parts: one policy predicate, one receipt per meeting, and one kill test with the network physically denied.

**Value to the owner.** Privacy is the project's identity, and the audit found it stronger in documents than in code. A receipt makes 'nothing left this Mac for this meeting' a derived, per-meeting fact the Library can show truthfully, and the kit catches the class an in-process guard cannot see: a later feature walking around the gate.

**Why now.** Local claims across the field are partial or uncheckable: open-core and hybrid tools keep summaries or analysis in the cloud by default, and a closed-source no-outbound claim cannot be verified (https://hedy.ai/post/best-local-ai-meeting-assistants-mac/, https://anarlog.so/pricing). tl;dv exposed metadata for 181,874 meetings while marketed as SOC 2 compliant (https://tldv.io/blog/our-thoughts-on-the-darkreading-com-article/). The Otter ruling turned on the vendor receiving and using conversations (https://uctoday.com/productivity-automation/otter-ai-fails-to-dismiss-core-privacy-claims-in-u-s-court/). A cloud vendor cannot publish a per-meeting receipt, because the honest content would be 'everything'.

**Builds on.** pipeline/src/mp/egress_guard.py (an httpx transport patch plus an environment scrub); the single zero-egress predicate (config.zero_egress, effective_backend); workflow.py's NDA and regulated modes; visibility.py; cloudsync.py; the schema-fenced event log as the receipt's transport; the audit's own passive socket-recording pytest run as prior art for the recorder.

**What the audit says.** Helps: the audit confirms a single fail-closed predicate, argv-only spawns and a stdio-only MCP server, so there is a real core to describe. Blocks: publishing the architecture as it stands would publish claims the audit found false: a withheld meeting's decisions sent to the cloud through series continuity (F014), a skipped workflow pick or a resume overriding an NDA match (F005, F046), doctor giving an all-clear without inspecting the NDA publish folder (F018), regulated mode's sink behaviour contradicting its own copy (G034), and README and SETUP lines that overstate (F041, G032). The guard covers httpx only and authorises by URL host (SEC18, SEC19), the Claude CLI provider runs with tools enabled (SEC20), and one pytest test makes a live network call. The honest first release is a failing report.

**First probe.** Five days, synthetic content only. Step one is a positive control: under `sandbox-exec` with a profile that denies non-loopback network (loopback stays open for the local model server) and with a `sys.addaudithook` socket-connect recorder attached, make one deliberate outbound connection; both the sandbox and the recorder must catch it, which also tests whether the sandbox tool still works on macOS 27.2 (unverified). Then take a 2-minute self-recorded clip, mark its workflow NDA, and run `mp` summarize and publish. Pass: 5 consecutive NDA runs complete with 0 non-loopback connect attempts, including one with the model cache removed, which must fail with a named error and not fetch; a cloud-workflow control run shows only the configured provider host and sink host; and a replay of the F014 shape (an earlier NDA meeting in the same workflow, flag switched off afterwards) puts 0 strings from the withheld meeting into the outbound request body. Any other host, silent fallback or leaked string is a fail, filed with its receipt. I expect the F014 replay to fail today; that is the kit doing its job.

**Risks.** `sandbox-exec` is deprecated and may stop working. The in-process hook sees neither subprocess providers nor the Swift side (FluidAudio's model download, Apple's Private Cloud Compute model), so the daemon needs its own check. It publishes privacy holes before they are fixed. The kit must stay a test and a file: a hosted trust page or a compliance write-up would cross the not-for-sale list. A cloud-synced library folder reintroduces a third party that no socket recorder sees.

**Considered before.** The per-workflow egress policy is adopted as the core identity with its holes filed (SEC17 to SEC31). An external conformance kit and a per-meeting receipt are not in the record. What changed: the 2026-11-12 audit found cross-meeting leaks (F014, F005, F046) that an in-process guard is structurally unable to notice.

- market-skeptic: Duplicate of I01 framed as a public kit. The F014 replay and the socket recorder belong in I01's harness; publishing red results before the fixes land adds risk for no owner gain.
- codebase-engineer: Same mechanism as I01, which I exercised on this host. Its distinct legs are worth folding in (the F014 replay that greps the outbound body for a withheld meeting's strings, and an audit-hook connect recorder), with results published only after the red rows are fixed.
- owner-advocate: The harness and receipt of I01 repackaged as a public kit; its F014 replay leg is worth adding to I01's probe. Publishing a failing privacy report before the fixes land is a cost with no reader.

### I10. Decision register scoped to a repository, for coding agents

Lens agent-memory. Total 14.00 (differentiation 3.33, feasibility 3.67, fit 3.33, safety 3.67). Effort M. Picked by: none.

**Pitch.** Project decisions and open questions (the two summary sections DV5 measured as well populated and not already projected) into a derived, read-only register in which each record keeps its verified quote, second and deep link, exposed through two MCP tools: `decisions(query, since, as_of)` and `open_questions(query)`. Scope is declared, never inferred: a small file in a repository lists the glossary terms, workflow ids and people that define 'meetings about this code', and a coding-agent skill uses it to answer 'what did we decide about X' and to draft an ADR in the owner's MADR template, with citations, for approval. Supersession is never auto-resolved; when two decisions match, the tool returns both with dates and the reader sees the conflict.

**Value to the owner.** Design decisions made in calls reach the place where the owner already works with agents, with provenance, instead of sitting in a published note nobody queries. ADR drafts stop being written from memory. It also replaces the decisions half of the Facts rail with something that has a reader.

**Why now.** Coding agents are a named audience in 2026 (Read AI's dev-tool pitch, Zoom's Codex and Claude Code plugins, a 23-skill Claude Code plugin from Minutes, a live plugin from Transcripted), so a plugin alone is not novel (agent segment; https://claude.com/connectors/minutes-conversation-memory for the Minutes listing). What the market file did not find anywhere is decisions with lifecycle or repository scoping with provenance. Discussion written up as decision is a documented failure of platform notes (https://venture-lab.org/2026/copilot-meeting-notes-wrong/), which is why receipts are the point.

**Builds on.** `MeetingSummary.decisions`, `questions` and the index-aligned `evidence` map (FEAT10), `evidence.verify`, the embedding index and FTS5 for query-time matching, workflows and the glossary as the scoping vocabulary, the meetingpipe open-at-timestamp link, the project's own ADR habit in docs/decisions.

**What the audit says.** Helps: the audit found the summary field set agreeing on both sides of the tree seam, and evidence verification is real. Blocks: Swift is an unvalidated second writer of summary.json; the Apple Intelligence summarizer skips evidence verification (F108, F125), so its meetings yield unanchored decisions; G004 and the name-keyed glossary beside UUID-keyed workflows mean scope must use workflow ids; F107 (publishers ignore the Library's overlays) is a warning that a new reader must go through the overlaid view or cite stale speaker labels.

**First probe.** Owner-run, read-only, one week. A script counts decisions and open questions over the last 90 days of visible meetings and the share carrying a verified anchor; the owner hand-labels a random 40 decisions as real decision, discussion written up as decision, or wrong. For one work repository the owner writes 10 questions of the form 'what did we decide about X' and checks whether query-time search over decisions returns the right record in the top 5. Pre-registered: GO if at least 80% of the 40 are real decisions, at least 60% carry an anchor and at least 7 of 10 questions hit. MARGINAL if precision passes but anchors are under 60% (then a re-summarize backfill is the true cost). NO-GO otherwise, and decisions stay summary text.

**Risks.** An agent will repeat a wrong decision with confidence; receipts reduce that and do not remove it. Most of the library predates evidence anchors (DV5 measured 17.6% of actions with evidence). Decisions about work code reach a cloud model when the coding agent reads them. A scoping file per repository is one more thing to keep true. Speaker on a decision is derived by segment lookup, not stored, and inherits diarization errors.

**Considered before.** Related. The Facts view (decisions plus open actions) shipped and is under review with Remove as the default; meeting-type classification was a measured NO-GO (45.6% agreement) and fuzzy grouping is a stated non-goal. This differs on each count: it keeps only the sections DV5 found populated, it is a query surface for an agent rather than a list for a person, and scope is declared by the owner rather than classified. Nothing in the record evaluated decisions as agent-queryable state.

- market-skeptic: Coding-agent plugins are common (Minutes, Transcripted, Zoom); the edge is declared repository scope plus verified anchors, and it suits an owner who writes ADRs with agents. Anchors cover only post-July meetings, so a backfill may be the real cost.
- codebase-engineer: Schema fields verified (decisions, questions, an index-aligned evidence map) and two read tools are a small build, but most of the library predates anchors (as cited), the Apple path writes unanchored decisions (F108), and it is a new cross-meeting reader of the F014 shape that must go through the gate and the overlaid view (F107). Its first step is 40 owner hand labels.
- owner-advocate: The best of the agent-memory builds: two read tools over the sections DV5 found populated, with declared scope and no inference. Gate it on I08's usage count and on anchor coverage, since most of the library predates evidence anchors and work decisions would reach a cloud coding agent.

### I33. Publish the experiment, not the recorder: a 'does the fix hold' dataset and a truth-fence kit for AI-written codebases

Lens open-source-community. Total 14.00 (differentiation 4, feasibility 3.33, fit 3.67, safety 3). Effort M. Picked by: none. Radical.

**Pitch.** The recorder is a commodity; the rare thing here is a roughly 90K-line, 1,093-commit codebase written entirely by AI with a paper trail no company would release: 234 task files with machine-readable headers, commit subjects that link to them by ID, spikes with pre-registered thresholds and honest negatives, ten dated audit reports, and a fresh finding that 18 fixes recorded as done do not hold. Package (a) a labelled set of task spec, fixing commit and audit verdict, usable to evaluate coding agents and reviewers, and (b) the process tooling as a small standalone kit: truth_fences.py, backlog.py, the worktree janitor with its pinned gates, and the spike and audit command shapes. The repository's subject becomes how to keep agent-written software honest, with a meeting recorder as the worked example.

**Value to the owner.** It turns the project's largest liability (comments, documents and instruments saying more than the code does) into its subject, and puts outside readers on exactly the class the owner distrusts. The immediate internal payoff is a verify-the-fix step for /tech-task, calibrated on real misses instead of guessed. It is also an answer to 'make it interesting' that does not compete with Granola, Talat or Google at all.

**Why now.** The local-recorder slot is saturated by the market file's own account: an open-source Granola alternative reached 271 points on Hacker News on 11 Nov 2026 with commenters calling the category saturated (https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50454625), GitHub's parakeet topic lists 603 repositories (https://github.com/topics/parakeet), and small local recorders sit at 18 to 91 stars. The October go-live assessment already advised against chasing community growth for the app. The market file does not cover the agent-evaluation space, so demand for such a dataset is my inference, not a sourced claim; what is sourced is that one more recorder will not be noticed.

**Builds on.** docs/backlog/tasks (234 files) and scripts/backlog.py; scripts/truth_fences.py; docs/audits; docs/spikes with their result JSON; docs/decisions; daemon/docs/gotchas.md (one incident per rule); scripts/prune-worktrees.sh with scripts/test-prune-worktrees.sh; the `<ID>: summary` commit convention; the truth-fence section of CONVENTIONS.md.

**What the audit says.** Helps: the audit's 'claimed fixed, not holding' table is the seed label set (18 rows, each with task, commit and file location), with five tests that pin the defect instead of the intent (G001, G006, G024, G025, G028) and one instrument that misreports (F151) as further positives. Blocks: about 90 Swift tests are source-text fences and several are vacuous on inspection, so the kit's centrepiece arrives partly discredited and has to say so; the antipattern pass read about 48 of 780 files, so fixes not on the list are unverified, not known-good, which caps label quality; the auditor re-read 9 of the 17 critical and high findings in source and the rest stand on verification agents alone.

**First probe.** Six days, local only. Assemble 54 items: the 18 not-holding rows plus 36 done tasks sampled at random from the same bands, each as task spec, fixing diff and the repo at that commit. Two blind reviewers (two different models, no access to the audit) answer one question per item: does this fix hold, and if not, name the failing path. Pass: at least 9 of the 18 known misses flagged with at most 9 false alarms among the 36, and on re-verification at least 3 of those false alarms turn out to be real new misses. That shows the set has signal and that a verify step would pay for itself. Fail (fewer than 6 of 18, or more than 18 false alarms): misses are not detectable from spec plus diff, the dataset is an anecdote, close it. In the same window a mechanical scan of git history for emails, URLs and a names list must return 0 hits outside an allowlist before anything is promoted.

**Risks.** It advertises a list of data-loss and privacy defects in the owner's own tool while they are open; the blast radius is one install, but the reputational cost is real. Eighteen positives is a small set, and labels produced by AI audits are fallible. The base prompt hard-codes one person's regulated-SaaS domain (PIPE26) and incident notes may carry meeting context, so a privacy read comes first. A launch post or a site to promote it would cross the not-for-sale list; a README section does not.

**Considered before.** No task or ADR records it (a grep of docs/backlog/tasks for 'open source' returns nothing). The nearest records are go-live option B (open source only in a light form, no community growth) and REPO1 (repo presence, done). This publishes process evidence and tooling, not the app, so that verdict does not decide it.

- market-skeptic: A real reframing that sidesteps the saturated recorder slot (https://hn.algolia.com/api/v1/items/50454625), but the market file has no evidence of demand and 18 positives is an anecdote. The verify-the-fix step is the useful core; publishing open data-loss and privacy defects is the risk.
- codebase-engineer: The paper trail is real (234 task files, truth_fences.py, the 18-row not-holding table) and the internal half, a verify-the-fix step calibrated on real misses, pays whether or not anything is published. Publishing waits on a privacy read of history (PIPE26, incident notes), and 18 positives from AI audits is a thin label set.
- owner-advocate: The most original answer to 'make it interesting', and its 6-day local probe would calibrate a verify-the-fix step on real misses. Publishing waits: the history needs a privacy read and it advertises open data-loss defects, so keep the blind-review probe as input to I46 and note that a launch post would cross the not-for-sale list.

### I43. Notion gets a note once: delete the rewrite path and the transcript upload, and drop the LAN sink

Lens contrarian-delete. Total 14.00 (differentiation 2, feasibility 4, fit 4, safety 4). Effort M. Picked by: none.

**Pitch.** Keep Notion as a destination but delete what makes it dangerous: the republish that deletes every block on the page and then appends without retry, the rewrite of Date and Status on every update, and the upload of the full transcript. A republish appends a dated revision under the existing page, the page carries the summary plus a deep link back to the exact second in the local library, and the LAN sink is removed outright.

**Value to the owner.** Frees two highs (F017, F088), shrinks F091's failure from a wiped page to a duplicate page, makes the SETUP sentence that only the summarizer sees the transcript true by removing its counterexample (G032), and cuts the default egress per meeting from the whole transcript to the note. For the owner: anything written by hand on a Notion page can no longer be destroyed by a re-summarize, and the transcript stays where the privacy story says it stays.

**Why now.** The market file's consent segment notes that publishing to a cloud workspace reintroduces a third party exactly as a cloud summarizer does, and that multi-tenant stores are an attack surface (tl;dv exposed metadata for 181,874 meetings, https://tldv.io/blog/our-thoughts-on-the-darkreading-com-article/). Notion rebuilt its MCP on 2026-10-19 so outside agents can write into it (https://www.notion.com/releases/2026-10-19), which makes a bespoke two-way sync less necessary than when it was written. FEAT21 also records that republish is today's routine repair for unnamed speakers, so the destructive path is exercised, not theoretical.

**Builds on.** the repo root pipeline/src/mp/publish_notion.py (774 lines, with 795 test lines beside it), the `<stem>.publish.json` result and exit 3 contract (unchanged), the meetingpipe URL scheme's open-at-timestamp verb, evidence anchors, and SinkDispatcher's per-sink failure isolation.

**What the audit says.** Helps: F017 and F088 (high), F091, G032, and G034 becomes simpler to state. It does not help F107 (all four publishers ignore the Library's overlays; that is a separate fix). Blocks: nothing structural, but the tests-python slice notes the Notion republish is checked by key names only, so the behaviour being replaced is weakly pinned and needs a real assertion first.

**First probe.** Owner-run, read-only, counts only, 3 days. From `.publish.json` files and `publisher` rows: R = share of Notion publishes that updated an existing page. One read-only Notion API query of the target database: H = pages last edited by a person after the integration created them. From the block fixtures or one sampled page: T = share of a page's blocks that are transcript. GO if H is at least 1 or R is at least 10%: replace wipe-and-append with an appended revision and stop sending Date and Status after creation; drop the transcript body if T is at least 70%. NO-GO if H is 0 across at least 50 pages: the wipe then has nothing of the owner's to destroy, and a retry on the append is the cheaper fix. The LAN sink needs no probe: FEAT18 records `[lan] host` empty on this Mac.

**Risks.** Notion is the default sink and on both earlier audits' keep lists, so this touches the daily path. The owner may read transcripts in Notion. Appended revisions make pages longer. The Notion query is a read of a remote system and must be owner-run.

**Considered before.** Partly. FEAT18 leans Cut on the LAN sink and explicitly keeps the Notion, Obsidian and filesystem sinks out of scope; this does not cut the sink, it cuts its rewrite path and transcript upload, which no task proposes. What changed: F017, F088, F091 and G032 in the 2026-11-12 audit, and Notion's 2026-10-19 MCP rebuild.

- market-skeptic: Cuts default egress from the whole transcript to the note and removes two highs (F017, F088). It touches the daily path, so ask the owner first whether Notion pages are hand-edited or read for transcripts.
- codebase-engineer: Verified the wipe in publish_notion.py: parallel block deletes, then an append that is deliberately not retried. Appending a dated revision and dropping the transcript body removes F017 and F088 and makes the SETUP claim true (G032); pin republish behaviour first, since today's tests check key names only.
- owner-advocate: Fixes two highs on the daily path by removing the wipe-and-rewrite, and cuts default egress from the whole transcript to the note. Run the 3-day count first, since the owner may read transcripts in Notion.

### I46. Stop producing claims faster than one person can check them: audit moratorium, a witness rule, and deletion of unfenced prose

Lens contrarian-delete. Total 14.00 (differentiation 1.33, feasibility 4, fit 4.67, safety 4). Effort M. Picked by: owner-advocate.

**Pitch.** Three process subtractions. First, no new audit and no new task filed until open critical and high findings are zero. Second, delete the partial and owner-verify limbo: a fix is closed by a test of intent that failed before it, or by an event-log replay, or it is open. Third, delete invariants no fence checks, starting with incident narrative in source comments and unfenced always, never and every sentences in README, ARCHITECTURE and CONVENTIONS.

**Value to the owner.** Frees the owner from being the only verifier of 41 partial tasks and of measurements unrun since July, shrinks about 950 KB of documentation carrying same-commit contracts (README 186 KB, ARCHITECTURE 217 KB, CONVENTIONS 130 KB, daemon gotchas 263 KB, by today's byte count), and removes the specific thing the audit says to distrust in AI-written code: text that says more than the code does. Every later coding session also reads less before it starts.

**Why now.** Mostly internal evidence. Eight audit documents are dated between 2026-10-21 and 2026-11-12, about 134 tasks were filed on or after 2026-10-21, 18 fixes are claimed and not holding (8 of them in the END and MIC bands), and four headline claims are false (F041, G032, F129 with 10 of about 30 contracts pinned, F151 printing 90.0% for a daemon at 51.0%). The market file adds one relevant fact: OS point releases silently break on-device pipelines (FluidAudio had to fix all-blank output under macOS 27.0.1), so only executable witnesses survive an upgrade, and prose does not.

**Builds on.** the repo root scripts/truth_fences.py (config, events, CLAUDE budget, links), the cross-tree golden fixtures, the DOC14 rule that already moved narrative out of CLAUDE.md, CONVENTIONS' own comments policy (default to no comments, no task references), the event log with analyze-detection for replay, and the 2026-06-26 done-task validation as precedent.

**What the audit says.** Helps: executive summary items 4 and 5, the 18 not-holding list, the tests that pin a defect instead of the intent (G001, G006, G024, G025, G028), the roughly 90 source-text fence tests in 43 files of which several are vacuous, and comments near half the length of the largest files (my count today: 702 comment lines of 1,980 in MeetingSessionController, 687 of 1,786 in MeetingRecorder). Blocks: coverage.sh must report the real figure first (F151), or the witness rule has no instrument.

**First probe.** Five days, read-only. Sample 60 claims at random: 20 comment lines asserting never, always, cannot or guarantee from the five largest Swift files (a keyword grep finds 1,366 such comment lines under daemon/Sources, which counts assertions and not errors), 20 invariant sentences from README and CONVENTIONS, and 20 tasks closed as done since September in the END, MIC, REC and SEC bands. A fresh session that did not write them marks each true, false or unverifiable against HEAD. Adopt the witness rule, the moratorium and the strip if false plus unverifiable reaches 15% (9 of 60). Drop the idea if it is under 5% (3 of 60).

**Risks.** Incident comments encode hard-won reasons and some would be lost; moving them instead of deleting them only relocates the bulk. A moratorium could let a real regression sit unfiled. The witness rule slows closing tasks, and for capture code a witness needs the headless seam the recorder does not yet have.

**Considered before.** Partly. DOC7 and DOC9 swept stale docs, DOC14 and DOC15 capped the CLAUDE.md files, CI5 fences three classes of drift, the go-live assessment flagged process weight, and a done-task validation ran on 2026-06-26. A moratorium, a filing cap and deletion of unfenced prose have not been proposed. What changed: after all of those sweeps the 2026-11-12 audit still measured 18 fixes not holding and four false headline claims, so sweeping is not converging.

- market-skeptic: Process, not product, aimed squarely at the audit's fourth finding (claims outrun code). The moratorium and witness rule cost little; delete prose only where a fence replaces it.
- codebase-engineer: The witness rule is the cheapest answer to the audit's items 4 and 5 and I would adopt it now, though it has no instrument until coverage.sh reports the real figure (F151) and the recorder has a headless seam. The comment strip is the risky leg, since those 1,366 lines also hold incident reasons, and a filing ban cuts against the owner's habit of filing the gap.
- owner-advocate: Aimed at the scarcest resource, one person's review time: eight audits and about 134 tasks in three weeks while 18 fixes do not hold. Adopt the moratorium and the witness rule now, move incident comments to gotchas instead of deleting the reasons, and fix coverage.sh first.

### I41. Regulated mode means zero egress, not zero recovery: delete the real-time gate and the destructive end cut

Lens contrarian-delete. Total 13.99 (differentiation 2, feasibility 4.33, fit 4.33, safety 3.33). Effort M. Picked by: codebase-engineer, owner-advocate.

**Pitch.** Delete `.regulatedGate` as a capture mode and, with it, the stop-time `truncated` cut. Regulated and NDA meetings resolve to the existing `.captureFirstRedact` path: lossless capture, offline redaction of the consumed artifact, the held tail kept as the same reversible `held_end_trim_sec` suggestion every other meeting already gets. The only thing still allowed to zero audio in real time is a deliberate act, the off-the-record hotkey. The mode's promise is restated honestly: nothing leaves this Mac, muted asides never reach transcript, notes or sinks, and the unredacted original expires on a short stated retention.

**Value to the owner.** Frees the audit's one critical (G001) and a high (F004) without fixing either, takes the teeth out of G026 and the two MIC8 fixes that do not hold (F036, G027), and collapses two capture paths into one so the single real start-write-stop recorder test the audit asks for (F110) covers every meeting. For the owner: a wrong end or a stale mute reading in the most sensitive meetings becomes one click to undo instead of a permanent hole in the recording.

**Why now.** The strictest mode is currently the lossiest, which is the product's best asset turned backwards. The market file names retained audio as the one thing funded botless vendors gave up (Granola deletes audio after transcription, https://www.granola.ai/pricing; Notion deletes the local copy after processing, https://www.notion.com/help/ai-meeting-notes) and lists lost or chopped recordings among the category's steadiest complaints. ADR 0016 itself records that those vendors manage privacy by retention, not by gating, and that an unreliable oracle cannot win a destructive real-time decision.

**Builds on.** the repo root daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/CaptureMode.swift (three cases, one resolver; the change is what `resolve` returns for regulated or NDA). the repo root daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/MeetingSessionController.swift lines 794 to 804 (`keepsHeldTail`, the only place the `truncated` cut is chosen). HeldEndTrim (END17's non-destructive trim, already shipped for lossless modes), MuteRedactor with its runaway-redaction guard, the kept original, per-workflow audio retention and the originals reaper, the off-the-record hotkey, and event rows `held_end_trimmed` (with `mode`), `lifecycle_end_held`, `micgate_attached`.

**What the audit says.** Helps: G001 (critical, the cut is pinned at the hold's first stamp and under this mode deletes in-call audio for good), F004 (high, a stale verdict zero-fills the mic), G026, F036, G027. Eight of the 18 claimed-fixed-not-holding findings are in the END and MIC bands. Blocks: F001 and F020 (the reaper and the default uninstall delete sole copies) must be fixed first because the kept original becomes the recovery copy; the default library sits in iCloud-synced Documents (SEC12), so audio at rest can mean audio at Apple until the library is moved.

**First probe.** Owner-run, read-only, counts only, 2 days. From events.jsonl: N = recordings that ran as regulated_gate. For each `held_end_trimmed` row with mode truncated, compute discarded seconds = stop time minus start time minus `limit_sec`, and join the matching `lifecycle_end_held` row for its dwell values. Then run ffmpeg silencedetect over the left channel of those N recordings and count M = recordings whose mic channel is exact digital silence for more than 50% of its length. GO (delete the mode) if any truncated row discarded more than its dwell plus 60 seconds, or M is at least 1. NO-GO if both are zero across at least 20 regulated recordings, or if the owner states that zero muted audio at rest is an external obligation and not a preference; in that case G001 and F004 are fixed in place and the mode stays.

**Risks.** Reverses a clause the owner approved in ADR 0016 (no audio at rest under regulated or NDA): muted asides and post-call room audio from the most sensitive meetings would sit on disk until retention expires. That is only acceptable if the library is off iCloud and the reaper never deletes a sole copy. The offline redactor has its own incident history (2026-06-08), so the runaway guard has to hold.

**Considered before.** Yes. ADR 0016 kept a real-time gate for regulated and NDA and accepted its residual data-loss risk; ADR 0017 rejected never-auto-end (not proposed here: the stop stays, only the cut goes). What changed: the 2026-11-12 audit found a critical (G001) and a high (F004) in exactly that accepted corner, plus G026 and two MIC8 fixes not holding, and END17 has since shipped the non-destructive trim for every other mode, so the alternative is already built and running.

- market-skeptic: Not a differentiator, but it turns the strictest mode from the lossiest into a recoverable one and retires the audit's one critical by deletion. It reverses an ADR 0016 clause, so it needs the library off iCloud and the reaper fixed first.
- codebase-engineer: Verified: one resolver (CaptureMode.resolve) and one cut site (keepsHeldTail, MeetingSessionController.swift line 794), with the lossless path and HeldEndTrim already shipping, so it removes the blast radius of G001 and F004 by deletion. It must land after F001 and F020 with the library off the synced folder, and it reverses an ADR 0016 clause, so it is the owner's decision.
- owner-advocate: Retires the audit's one critical and a high by deleting the path instead of repairing it, and leaves one capture path for a real recorder test to cover. It reverses a clause of ADR 0016, so it needs the owner's explicit decision, the reaper fix (F001) and the library off iCloud first.

### I21. Muted asides: what you say on mute becomes a private margin, not part of the meeting

Lens beyond-meetings. Total 12.66 (differentiation 4.33, feasibility 2.33, fit 3.33, safety 2.67). Effort M. Picked by: market-skeptic.

**Pitch.** In the default capture mode the full mic is the consumed artifact (CaptureMode.captureFirst) and the pipeline has no concept of mute, so anything the owner says while muted is transcribed, attributed, summarized and sent to sinks exactly like speech the call heard. Use the Teams client-log mute oracle plus the mic channel to split owner speech into heard-by-the-call and aside. Asides become an owner-only layer anchored at the second they were said: margin notes in the Library transcript, private steering context for the summarizer under the meeting's own egress policy, never written to any sink. Mute then doubles as a deliberate whisper-to-note channel during any call, with no new capture and no new UI.

**Value to the owner.** A truthful record first: published notes stop presenting as meeting content things nobody on the call heard. Second, a hands-free in-call note channel, the spoken equivalent of typing into a notepad beside the transcript. Third, owner intent reaches the summarizer, which the Facts numbers say is the missing input (16 of 1,374 actions ever resolved, 37% of owned actions name a human).

**Why now.** Granola's core mechanic is merging the user's own rough notes with the transcript, and device-capture tools separate speakers by device at best (per digest, tl;dv's competitor-run test, no URL in the digest). A spoken margin needs a mute timeline that is independent of audio plus a physically separate mic channel, which only a tool reading the meeting client's own state has. Accurate attribution is the top-ranked user priority (https://gladia.io/meeting-assistant-market-map), and side remarks landing in notes sent to everyone is a recorded complaint (per digest, legal segment, no URL).

**Builds on.** Paths are relative to the repo root the repo root . daemon/Sources/MeetingPipeCore/MicGate/SlimCoreMuteLog.swift (pure parser of Teams mute transitions, MIC8 GO, 594 transitions measured), MuteSourceFusion and MicGate, MuteTimeline and `<stem>.mute-timeline.json`, the channel-separated WAV, the existing recorder event mute_redaction_withheld (reason speech_in_muted_span) and MuteRedactor.speechFloorDb, `<stem>.markers.json` as the precedent for timestamp-anchored annotations, the overlay model for reversible edits, the self-voiceprint to drop a second voice in the room. MeetingPipeCore is an exported library product in daemon/Package.swift, so a probe can link it.

**What the audit says.** This is the constructive side of F041 (README says muted spans are redacted by default; the default never redacts) and G032 (SETUP says only the summarizer sees the transcript while the default Notion sink uploads it). Blocks: F036 and G027 (MIC8 fixes claimed, not holding: a suppressed AX mute reading is lost for good, producers dedupe before fusion) mean the fused oracle is not yet fit to classify speech, and the AX watcher deliberately treats sustained speech during a muted reading as a stale read (mute_state_cleared_vad_contradiction), so asides are classifiable only where an audio-independent oracle exists, which today is the Teams log; Zoom is unmeasured. F107 (all four publishers ignore overlays) and F014 (later features walk around the per-meeting gate) are exactly how a private layer would leak, so it needs a publisher-side fence and a cross-tree fixture. Default mode does not persist auto mute spans (MeetingRecorder.persistAfterMerge writes them only for captureFirstRedact), so the sidecar contract changes. Under the regulated gate muted audio is zero-filled at capture, so asides cannot exist there by design, and F004 says that gate has its own stale-verdict defect.

**First probe.** 5 working days, retroactive, no daemon change. A standalone Swift package (shape of daemon/scripts/diar4-offline-diarizer-probe) links the MeetingPipeCore library, parses the Teams log with SlimCoreMuteLog into muted spans, intersects them with recording intervals from events.jsonl, and for each default-mode recording that still holds audio prints counts only: muted seconds, and seconds inside muted spans where the mic channel is above -50 dBFS while the system channel is quiet or clearly lower (to exclude loudspeaker bleed). Step one is a coverage count: how many past recordings the retained Teams log generations still cover. Then for one week the owner uses mute as a note channel at least once per call and labels 20 sampled segments by ear: really muted, my voice, worth keeping (each yes or no). GO: at least 18 of 20 really muted, at least 10 deliberate asides in the week, at least 70% worth keeping. MARGINAL: 15 to 17 really muted, ship only a 'said while muted' transcript marker with no summarizer input. NO-GO: under 15, or fewer than 5 asides in the week; close, and file the truthfulness gap as a doc fix.

**Risks.** A wrong mute reading turns a real contribution into an aside, which is a silent error, so the layer must only mark and never remove, as a reversible overlay. The aside layer is the most sensitive data in the library: things said on mute because others should not hear them, possibly including another person in the room. Works only for native Teams today. The habit may not form. One more per-meeting file kind on a seam that already carries about 26.

**Considered before.** The project framed muted speech only as something to cut: ADR 0016 capture-first redaction, later made opt-in per workflow (MIC9), with never-redact as the default. Treating it as a private annotation layer is not in the considered list. What changed since those decisions: the Teams client-log oracle (MIC8, GO) gives mute timing that depends on neither audio nor Accessibility, which is the precondition for classifying rather than cutting.

- market-skeptic: The only idea that needs both the client mute oracle and the separate mic channel, and it repairs a truth defect (muted speech published as meeting content, F041). Teams only, on a fusion with two fixes not holding (F036, G027), and the aside layer becomes the most sensitive data in the library.
- codebase-engineer: The most original idea here, but it classifies speech with the fused mute oracle the audit found not holding (F036, G027), Teams only; default mode does not persist mute spans (verified in MeetingRecorder.swift), and a private layer must survive four publishers that ignore overlays (F107). Fix the F041 truth gap first, since muted speech is transcribed and published today.
- owner-advocate: Novel, and it fixes a truth gap (muted speech is published as meeting content today), but it rests on a mute oracle whose fixes are not holding and creates the most sensitive layer in a codebase where private layers leak. Run the retroactive 5-day count and ship at most the 'said while muted' marker until F036, G027 and F107 are fixed.

## What the judges would do

### market-skeptic

Top picks: I16 (The archive as a build graph: every artifact re-derivable from retained audio, so the whole library improves when a model does), I01 (Kernel-enforced zero egress with a per-meeting egress receipt), I21 (Muted asides: what you say on mute becomes a private margin, not part of the meeting), I06 (Quote-only minutes under regulated mode: no generated sentence in the record), I35 (A yardstick that needs no labelling: channel energy, correction overlays and glossary firings as a standing speech scorecard), I37 (What I said I would do: a ledger of the owner's own commitments, tested against the mic channel and playable in the owner's own voice), I36 (Voice census: name a voice once and the archive resolves), I45 (Archive first, notes on request: stop summarizing and publishing every meeting by default).

Direction: stop competing on the note and make the product the checkable record. Three segments of the market file call detect, capture, transcribe, summarize, export and read-only MCP a commodity (Talat ships the same stack, https://techcrunch.com/2026/03/24/talats-ai-meeting-notes-stay-on-your-machine-not-in-the-cloud/; Google gives one away, https://techcrunch.com/2026/10/08/google-releases-a-new-local-first-granola-competitor/). Spend only on what funded vendors gave up by design: retained channel-separated audio (Granola deletes it, https://www.granola.ai/pricing), the client mute oracle, the single egress chokepoint and the evidence verifier.

Step 0, decisions before fixes (week 1). Three deletions are alternative fixes for items on the audit list, and paying for both is waste. One combined counts script over the event log settles I41 (fix G001 and F004 in place, or retire them with the real-time gate) and I47. One question to the owner settles I43 (are Notion pages hand-edited or read for transcripts; if so the rewrite path goes, if not F017 gets a retry). I42 deletes workflow-keyed series continuity and with it F014. I42's witness rule exempts the People rail, because I36 feeds it and I37 leaves other people's commitments on it.

Step 1 (weeks 1 to 5). The rest of the audit fix list (F005, F046, F018, G003, F001 with F020, F016, F012, G004) plus F062, because I16 sits on it, and the assessor's direction-neutral seam legs. Hold deletion of summarize_staged.py and dogfood.py until I06 reports, since its probe uses both.

Step 2, owner-run measurements, capped at five this quarter and ordered by owner time, because owner-owed benches have stalled since July (LOCAL13, LOCAL14, CAL3 leg 1, PIPE20). No labelling: I35 yardstick with its planted-regression self-check, I36 census separability, I01 bypass harness (synthetic). About 15 minutes: I37. One bench: mlx-swift-lm against LOCAL12's four bars, to write ARCH11 with numbers, and no port work. The Neural Engine reading from I32 rides on I35 but only after a fresh build, since the installed binary is from September (F021); comparing FluidAudio 0.18.3 is a dependency change that needs owner approval. Waiting until one of the five closes: I06 (100 quotes to grade) and I21's habit week (20 labels). I21's retroactive mute-span count needs no labelling and can run with the step 0 script.

Step 3 (weeks 9 to 12). Build at most two. First, I16's first leg: time-keyed overlays, then the FluidAudio bump qualified on the yardstick. Second, I01 if its harness passes, otherwise I36's naming queue. I45 runs as a reversible setting on one or two workflows outside the I37 and I06 samples.

What changed for items the project already decided. I37: Facts is under review with Remove as the default (DV5); evidence anchors shipped on 2026-07-24 give each action a timestamp that can be tested against the mic channel, a slice DV5 never measured, and a miss executes Remove. I41 and I21: ADR 0016 kept a real-time gate and treated muted speech only as something to cut; since then the audit found a critical (G001) and a high (F004) in that corner, END17 shipped the non-destructive trim, and MIC8 gave a mute oracle that does not depend on audio. I06: the local model as silent default stays rejected (hallucination 0.29 against 0.05); the model is unchanged, the output contract is, to verbatim verified quotes only. The I17 bench: LOCAL12 said NO-GO because no MLX-Swift candidate existed; the market file now reports mlx-swift-lm 3.32.3 with guided generation, so the measured A/B that the non-goal names can run.

Rust. Closed in-tree (I07, I14, I20). The only acceptable shape is I34, an out-of-tree clean-room reader of the on-disk format, and only if the owner wants Rust for its own sake.

Refuse. A Swift port before the bench (I17). In-person, phone and watch capture (I19, I25, I24), which is where funded players are expanding. Spotlight and Siri parity (I13). Pre-meeting briefs (I12). Always-on capture (I27). Encryption at rest (I09). Community packaging of the sensing layer (I28) while 7 of the 18 unheld fixes sit in it. The process split in I18; take the target split only. Person pages before the census reports (I39). Legal hold, deletion certificates and voiceprint clocks (I03, I04) beyond the footprint enumerator and SEC22.

**Where this judge expects to be outvoted.** 1. Deletion and hygiene ideas (I41, I42, I46, I47, I32, I29). Feasibility and fit judges will put these on top. I score them 1 or 2 on differentiation and keep them out of the top picks while placing them first in the sequence. Deleting makes the product true, not interesting, and the owner asked for interesting.

2. I16 in first place. Others will say F062 and a 2.5 data-handling score make re-derivation dangerous, and that few people re-read old meetings. I hold because it is the one property the market file calls distinctive in three segments and funded vendors cannot follow, and because its first leg (time-keyed overlays, a qualified FluidAudio bump) is needed anyway.

3. I21. Expect lower marks on safety (most sensitive layer, F036 and G027 not holding) and on Teams-only reach, and no market evidence of demand. I hold at 4 on differentiation because nothing else uses both the mute oracle and the mic channel, and it repairs F041.

4. I36 capped at 3 on differentiation. Others will rate it highest as the owner's stated pain. Speaker memory already ships in Talat, Daisy and Transcripted, and platform notes get identity from the host for free, so it is catch-up. I still pick it, on fit.

5. Tie-breaks. I08, I10 and I26 equal or beat I36 and I45 on my own numbers. I picked I36 because it is the stated pain and gates I39, I35 because it gates I16, and I45 because it is the cheapest way to make default behaviour match the privacy identity. I08 and I10 wait on an unmeasured MCP usage count, and Minutes already excludes restricted meetings and lists commitment and relationship tools. I26 is worth doing but too small to be a direction. Agent-lens judges will disagree.

6. I17. Architecture judges may want all-Swift as a pick because it removes a measured cost class. Talat is a 20 MB all-Swift app; language is not where distinction lives. Bench only.

7. I02. I give it 4 on differentiation and still leave it out of the quarter, since it crosses the not-for-sale list and adds a key to hands-off recording. Others may either drop it lower or pull it in on legal grounds.

8. I27 and I33. Radical-lens judges may pick them. I27 is unique but I score safety 1. I33 has no demand evidence in the market file and 18 positives is an anecdote.

9. I45. Fit judges will say hands-off notes are the core job. I hold that the note is the commodity and a 14-day reversible setting is a cheap way to find out.

10. I01 over I15 and I31. Some will prefer the wider daemon confinement or the public kit. I keep the narrow child-only shape as canonical because sandbox-exec is deprecated and unverified on 27.2, so the smallest claim that can be proven comes first.

### codebase-engineer

Top picks: I01 (Kernel-enforced zero egress with a per-meeting egress receipt), I35 (A yardstick that needs no labelling: channel energy, correction overlays and glossary firings as a standing speech scorecard), I41 (Regulated mode means zero egress, not zero recovery: delete the real-time gate and the destructive end cut), I42 (Flip the default on the secondary surfaces: deleted on a date unless a local witness shows use), I08 (One read broker: narrowing tiers, client profiles, receipts and a read ledger), I29 (Meeting bundle 0.1: write the per-meeting folder down as a spec with JSON Schemas and a validator, so the file contract has one owner), I36 (Voice census: name a voice once and the archive resolves), I16 (The archive as a build graph: every artifact re-derivable from retained audio, so the whole library improves when a model does).

Direction: a recorder whose claims can be checked, on an archive that gets better with age. The base loop is a commodity (https://hn.algolia.com/api/v1/items/50454625), so the interest has to come from what rivals structurally lack: privacy that is enforced and printed per meeting (local claims elsewhere are partial or uncheckable, https://anarlog.so/pricing), retained audio that can be re-derived (the funded leader deletes it, https://www.granola.ai/pricing), and names that stick (the top-ranked user priorities, https://gladia.io/meeting-assistant-market-map). All of it comes after the audit's fix-first list, not instead of it.

Order.

1. Fix-first and subtraction together, because several fixes are deletions: I41 (after F001 and F020, library off the synced folder, owner signs off the ADR 0016 reversal), the evidence-backed half of I42 (series continuity with F014, Facts, prep card, App Intents, LAN sink; not the Tutorial), I43, I47, and only the staged-summarizer and trainer leg of I44. Adopt I46's witness rule as process, without the comment strip.

2. Agent-run, fixture-only work that costs no owner time: I01's bypass harness, child-process profile and per-meeting receipt, folding in I31's F014 replay; I08's canary fence as the standing cross-meeting privacy test; I29's schemas as ARCH10's typed owner, with I03's canary delete test as a second fence.

3. Owner-run measurement, capped at three sittings because LOCAL13, LOCAL14, PIPE20 and T8 item 4 have sat unrun since July: (a) one event-log count script that answers I41, I47, I08's usage question and I35's speed ratio in a single run; (b) I35's channel scorecard and I36's census counts in one read-only pass; (c) I32, which is the assessor's second measurement (Neural Engine in the agent, plus the FluidAudio bump) and sits inside the cap. The assessor's first measurement, the mlx-swift-lm bench (https://github.com/ml-explore/mlx-swift-lm/releases), sits outside the cap: run it only when the three are done, since nothing in this portfolio waits on it, and let it decide ARCH11 when it runs.

4. Build only on what passed: the I36 enrolment queue once G003, G015 and F019 are fixed; I16 limited to transcripts and speakers with time-keyed overlays, its first job the FluidAudio bump (https://github.com/FluidInference/FluidAudio/releases).

5. If time remains: the internal verify-the-fix step from I33.

Refused this quarter: Rust inside the app (I07, I14, I20; the accelerator gap is why it stays out of the engine, https://moklabs-research.pages.dev/reports/technology/2026-03-20-parakeet-rs-tauri-integration-patterns), the all-Swift port beyond its bench (I17), the capture process split (I18), always-on capture (I27), encryption at rest (I09 and I04's encryption leg), new capture surfaces (I19, I23, I24, I25), any new per-meeting file kind before ARCH10 has a single writer (I02's sidecar state, I05, I21, I22), a write tool on the MCP server (I11), the signing step for Spotlight and Siri (I13), the person-page home (I39), and on-request summaries as a default (I45). I34 is acceptable only as an out-of-tree experiment after I29, if the owner wants Rust for its own sake.

Provenance: at commit 4949c9c0 in the repo root  I ran local-only sandbox checks, greps and line counts, and read daemon/Sources/MeetingPipe/CaptureMode.swift, the series-continuity function in pipeline/src/mp/summarize.py, the republish block in pipeline/src/mp/publish_notion.py and daemon/Package.swift. Observed on this host with the filters I used: /usr/bin/sandbox-exec enforces on macOS 27.2 (EPERM for a non-local address, inherited by child processes), a loopback-only profile still admitted this Mac's own interface address, and one extra allow clause made an earlier profile silently permissive. Every library-derived figure and every market or audit claim is taken from the two digests as cited; I built nothing and read no meeting data.

**Where this judge expects to be outvoted.** Where I expect to be outvoted, and why I hold.
1. The deletions. I41, I42, I43 and I47 score 1 or 2 on differentiation, and a market-minded judge will call them no answer to "make it interesting". I rank them high because each removes a verified defect at a site I read (the gate resolver and cut site, the series-continuity block, the Notion wipe), and every build idea below them is safer once they land.
2. The most original ideas. I21 (muted asides), I22 (debrief), I27 (own-voice ledger) and I39 (person pages) will win on novelty. Each builds on a part the audit found not holding (mute fusion F036 and G027, the session controller around F005 and F046, roster undo G003) and none fixes it, so I scored the build, not the probe.
3. Consent. A legal-minded judge will put I02 near the top on the Granola complaint (https://btlaw.com/en/insights/alerts/2026/what-the-granola-class-action-means-for-companies-building-and-deploying-conversation-capture-tools). I agree the duty is the owner's; I scored feasibility 2 because the evidence leg leans on the prompt and the mute timeline, and I would take only the static per-workflow declaration and the paste text.
4. The rewrite. Radical judges will want I17 or I14 as the headline, and I44 would close the question by decree. I fund only the bench: the port is XL against a local baseline the digest records as below the project's own ship gate, and deleting the Apple summarizer before the number exists throws away the provider a Swift path would need.
5. I41 itself. A privacy-first judge can fairly object that it puts NDA audio at rest against ADR 0016.
