**Trends.**

- The on-device stack is a commodity. FluidAudio (Swift, Apache-2.0, about 3.3k stars) wraps Parakeet ASR, Pyannote, Sortformer and Nemotron diarization and VAD for the Neural Engine; its showcase lists 51 apps of which roughly 16 are meeting recorders or notetakers (Talat, Summit, OpenOats, Muesli, Hedy and others), and GitHub's parakeet topic lists 603 repositories. Talat is itself built on FluidAudio with Parakeet. <https://github.com/FluidInference/FluidAudio> <https://github.com/topics/parakeet> <https://techcrunch.com/2026/03/24/talats-ai-meeting-notes-stay-on-your-machine-not-in-the-cloud/>
- Dictation apps converged on meetings in 2026: Wispr Flow shipped Notetaker (5 Aug; bot-free, MCP, pre-meeting briefs), MacWhisper 15.2 added live meeting transcripts (23 Sep), Superwhisper shipped Meetings (15 Oct), and newer apps such as Muesli and Talat bundle dictation and meeting capture in one app. <https://9to5mac.com/2026/08/05/wispr-flow-takes-on-ai-meeting-assistants-with-notetaker-its-first-product-beyond-dictation/> <https://9to5mac.com/2026/09/23/macwhisper-update-brings-real-time-meeting-transcripts-improved-speaker-recognition-more/> <https://betakit.com/?p=410935> <https://github.com/Muesli-HQ/muesli>
- MCP access to meeting history went from novelty to table stakes. Granola (Feb 2026; free plan limited to the last 30 days), Zoom (expanded May 2026), Read AI, Avoma, Wispr Flow, screenpipe, Talat, Daisy and Ghosty Notes all expose it, while Meetily and Granola gate it by plan. Vendors now argue the value lies in write-back and per-agent access control rather than read access. <https://www.granola.ai/pricing> <https://www.granola.ai/blog/what-is-mcp-plain-english> <https://www.spinach.ai/blog/mcp-server-meeting-notes-ai-agents> <https://meetily.ai/pricing>
- Rename, pivot and relicensing churn is high. Hyprnote became Char and then Anarlog within about three months while the team's main effort moved to another product; screenpipe moved from lifetime licences to a source-available licence and subscriptions; Talat moved from a $49 one-time price to subscription and lifetime tiers; Rewind was switched off two weeks after its acquisition was announced. <https://anarlog.so/blog/char-is-now-anarlog> <https://github.com/screenpipe/screenpipe> <https://screenpipe.com/pricing> <https://talat.app/>
- Open-core and freemium gating concentrates on the features power users ask for most. Meetily puts diarization, auto-detection, MCP, templates and exports in Pro; Anarlog puts better speaker identification, sync, sharing and hosted AI in a $14 plan; Ghosty Notes puts calendar sync and the fast cross-meeting model in Pro; Granola limits free history and MCP to 30 days. <https://meetily.ai/pricing> <https://anarlog.so/pricing> <https://ghostynotes.app/> <https://www.granola.ai/pricing>
- The cloud incumbent is moving upmarket and into legal exposure. Granola raised $125M at a $1.5B valuation (25 Mar 2026), launched Spaces plus personal and enterprise APIs, broke users' local agent workflows by changing its local cache, trains on anonymised data by default on Basic and Business plans, and was sued on 30 Jul 2026 (Chamberlain v. Granola, N.D. Cal.) over botless capture with participant notice off by default. Lawyers note that a ruling in the Otter.ai bot case may not protect tools that are never visible to other participants. <https://techcrunch.com/2026/03/25/granola-raises-125m-hits-1-5b-valuation-as-it-expands-from-meeting-notetaker-to-enterprise-ai-app> <https://www.granola.ai/pricing> <https://btlaw.com/en/insights/alerts/2026/what-the-granola-class-action-means-for-companies-building-and-deploying-conversation-capture-tools>
- On-device diarization and speaker memory improved in 2026. NVIDIA's Nemotron 3 Diarization (up to 8 speakers) shipped in MacWhisper, Hedy and meeting-transcriber, and Talat and meeting-transcriber keep voice prints or embeddings on device to recognise speakers across meetings. <https://9to5mac.com/2026/09/23/macwhisper-update-brings-real-time-meeting-transcripts-improved-speaker-recognition-more/> <https://hedy.ai/post/best-local-ai-meeting-assistants-mac/> <https://github.com/pasrom/meeting-transcriber> <https://talat.app/>
- Platform owners bundle botless capture. ChatGPT Record mode runs in the macOS app on paid plans, Notion AI Meeting Notes captures system audio plus mic on desktop, and Apple Notes on macOS 27 records, transcribes and offers an Apple Intelligence summary. MacWhisper already exposes transcripts to Siri and Shortcuts on macOS 27, and vendor benchmarks put Apple's SpeechAnalyzer in the same accuracy range as Whisper and Parakeet, with conflicting results. <https://help.openai.com/en/articles/11487532> <https://www.notion.com/product/ai-meeting-notes> <https://support.apple.com/en-au/guide/notes/apdb5106e334/4.13/mac/27> <https://9to5mac.com/2026/09/23/macwhisper-update-brings-real-time-meeting-transcripts-improved-speaker-recognition-more/>
- Local summarisation is the acknowledged weak link, and vendors respond with a small bundled model plus an escape hatch to cloud. Talat defaults to a Qwen 4B model, Hyprnote/Anarlog shipped a 1.7B default, Meetily recommends cloud models for accuracy while keeping transcription local, and Liquid AI markets a 2.6B transcript-specialised model with unverified claims of cloud-level quality. <https://talat.app/> <https://kompozy.io/reviews/hyprnote> <https://meetily.ai/blog/our-quest-for-meeting-summary-accuracy> <https://www.liquid.ai/blog/the-future-of-meeting-summarization-local-fast-private-and-fully-secure>
- Differentiation is shifting from the post-meeting note to before and during the meeting, and to more devices: pre-meeting briefs and catch-up recaps (Wispr Flow), live talking points from your own notes (OpenOats), in-session suggestions (Hedy), live minutes (Quill), plus Windows, iPhone and Watch companions (Talat) and Windows, Linux and mobile betas (Anarlog). <https://9to5mac.com/2026/08/05/wispr-flow-takes-on-ai-meeting-assistants-with-notetaker-its-first-product-beyond-dictation/> <https://github.com/yazinsai/OpenOats> <https://hedy.ai/post/best-local-ai-meeting-assistants-mac/> <https://quillmeetings.com/pricing>

**Complaints and gaps.**

- Local summaries are unreliable. Meetily's team reports hallucinations, missed action items, omitted decisions and speaker or topic confusion with models under 7B parameters, slow or failing runs with a 7B model on a 24 GB Mac, and recommends cloud models for accuracy. A 2026 review scored Hyprnote's default 1.7B summariser 3.6 out of 5. Hedy says local summaries take tens of seconds or more. No independent, measured comparison of local versus cloud summary accuracy was found. <https://meetily.ai/blog/our-quest-for-meeting-summary-accuracy> <https://kompozy.io/reviews/hyprnote> <https://hedy.ai/post/best-local-ai-meeting-assistants-mac/>
- Speaker diarization and identification is the most requested capability and is often paywalled or imprecise. The most-discussed open Meetily issue is speaker diarization (#230), and Meetily and Anarlog reserve it or its better version for paid plans. Commenters on the Hyprnote launch thread asked for better speaker identification for non-native English speakers, and a Talat listing notes weaker diarization early in a recording. <https://github.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meeting-minutes/issues> <https://meetily.ai/pricing> <https://anarlog.so/pricing> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=44725306>
- Automatic meeting detection and calendar-driven start and stop are missing or gated in open-source tools. Meetily has several open requests (#387, #218, #449), auto-detect is Pro only and calendar integration is still marked as coming soon; Hyprnote commenters asked for MacWhisper-style automatic detection; a competitor notes Notion's notetaker has no calendar-triggered recording. <https://github.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meeting-minutes/issues> <https://meetily.ai/pricing> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=44725306> <https://fireflies.ai/blog/notion-ai-meeting-notes>
- Audio capture is fragile. Open Meetily issues cover the mic not being captured with Bluetooth headphones on Mac (#450), choppy audio with noise and echo (#220), recording failing on Windows 11 (#135) and missing system audio on Linux (#273, #383). Talat's founder describes Apple's Core Audio Taps API as poorly documented, and a Hyprnote launch commenter reported the app never opening on an M4 Mac. <https://github.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meetily/issues/450> <https://github.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meetily/issues/220> <https://github.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meeting-minutes/issues> <https://www.weekinvoiceai.com/p/how-talat-built-a-local-first-stack>
- Longevity and lock-in worries are justified by 2026 events. Rewind users got two weeks before capture stopped and, in several regions, a deletion deadline; Granola's change to its local cache broke users' agent workflows; a reviewer marks Anarlog down for stability after two renames; screenpipe stopped selling lifetime licences and changed licence. <https://9to5mac.com/2025/12/05/rewind-limitless-meta-acquisition/> <https://techcrunch.com/2026/03/25/granola-raises-125m-hits-1-5b-valuation-as-it-expands-from-meeting-notetaker-to-enterprise-ai-app> <https://kompozy.io/reviews/hyprnote> <https://github.com/screenpipe/screenpipe>
- Consent and legality of invisible capture is an open problem for the whole botless category. The Granola class action targets capture with no bot and notice off by default; Hyprnote launch commenters raised jurisdiction-specific disclosure and compliance-team resistance; OpenOats makes users acknowledge consent obligations before first use, and a 2026 overview of local copilots defers the question rather than resolving it. <https://btlaw.com/en/insights/alerts/2026/what-the-granola-class-action-means-for-companies-building-and-deploying-conversation-capture-tools> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=44725306> <https://github.com/yazinsai/OpenOats> <https://themenonlab.blog/blog/ai-meeting-copilots-local-first-privacy>
- Local claims are often partial or unverifiable. Anarlog states that local speech recognition does not make summaries local and its chat web search sends queries out; Hedy's analysis is cloud by default; Superwhisper uses cloud models unless switched; Quill's AI notes use a hosted model unless reconfigured; a closed-source rival's no-outbound-requests claim cannot be checked; sources conflict on whether Granola transcribes on device. <https://anarlog.so/pricing> <https://hedy.ai/post/best-local-ai-meeting-assistants-mac/> <https://metawhisp.com/blog/granola-alternatives-on-device-mac/> <https://www.getvoibe.com/resources/quill-meetings-alternatives/>
- Language coverage and code-switching are weak on the default on-device engine. Parakeet v3 covers 25 European languages; NVIDIA's model card lists code-switching as a failure mode; a NeMo discussion asks for a way to force the output language with no workaround; a bilingual benchmark ranks Parakeet v3 mid-pack. One vendor test shows Whisper large ahead of Parakeet and Apple Speech on English accuracy, so engine choice is unsettled. <https://github.com/NVIDIA-NeMo/Speech/discussions/14620> <https://huggingface.co/blog/ServiceNow-AI/code-switching> <https://ghostynotes.app/> <https://whispernotes.app/de-AT/blog/apple-speech-vs-whisper>
- Hardware and setup burden remains. Observers describe local tools as rougher and more DIY than SaaS; local AI needs Apple Silicon with 16 GB or more (Summit also about 10 GB of disk; Hedy's largest models realistically 36 GB); Anarlog users must set up Ollama or LM Studio themselves; Meetily's community Linux build is source-only. <https://themenonlab.blog/blog/ai-meeting-copilots-local-first-privacy> <https://www.gappsy.com/tools/summit-ai-notes/> <https://hedy.ai/post/best-local-ai-meeting-assistants-mac/> <https://github.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meeting-minutes>
- What the open-source options still lack relative to Granola: shared folders, hosted search and integrations for teams; configurable summary templates and prompt control (Meetily #243, #235); domain vocabulary hints (#474); inline transcript editing with re-summarisation (#377); and independent evidence, since review counts are tiny (Meetily has 1 G2 review, Quill 7). <https://github.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meeting-minutes/issues> <https://metawhisp.com/blog/granola-alternatives-on-device-mac/> <https://www.speechmark.co/articles/granola-alternatives> <https://www.g2.com/products/meetily/reviews>

**Not verified.**

- Search budget: the shared web search limit for this run was exhausted after about 33 of my searches, so a planned search on echo and duplicate transcripts when recording without headphones, and further searches for Reddit threads, could not be run. Remaining checks were done by fetching primary pages directly.
- Unsourced belief from prior knowledge: speaker bleed (the mic re-capturing system audio when not on headphones, producing echo or duplicated lines) is a common complaint for dual-stream botless recorders. I could only source a generic Meetily echo issue (#220), not this specific failure.
- No Reddit threads were reachable; user sentiment here comes from Hacker News, GitHub issues, Product Hunt and vendor or competitor pages. Many comparison pages are written by vendors (Hedy, Speechmark, MetaWhisp, Voibe, Anarlog, screenpipe) and should be read as marketing.
- GitHub pages did not render release or last-commit dates, so project activity is inferred from commit counts, changelog mentions and third-party trackers. Star counts are the rounded figures GitHub showed on 2026-11-12.
- Amurex: maintenance status in 2026 is unverified; the May 2025 last-push date comes from a directory, not the repo.
- Quill: the $6.5M seed, the local MCP server and the bring-your-own-model option come from a competitor's blog; Quill's own release notes page rendered empty and its pricing page mentions BYO LLM only for Enterprise.
- Talat: conflict between March 2026 press ($49 one-time, $99 at 1.0) and the current vendor site ($89 per year, $189 lifetime). Whether 1.0 has shipped is not stated. The periodic licence check claim comes from a competitor.
- MacWhisper: price (EUR 59, 64 or 65) and diarization model (Nemotron 3 per 9to5Mac, Pyannote 4 per another review) conflict across sources; the vendor page was not fetched.
- Meetily: the Pro 1.8.2 date for diarization (22 July 2026) came from a search summary of the vendor FAQ, not a page I fetched. The GitHub README mentions speaker diarization as on-device while pricing lists it as Pro.
- Granola: sources conflict on whether transcription happens on device or in the cloud, and on total funding ($176M versus $192M). The status of the Otter.ai litigation (claims surviving dismissal versus still awaiting a ruling) also conflicts across sources.
- The Hyprnote launch thread used for complaint themes dates from 2025, before the renames; I read it through a mirror page of the Hacker News item.
- Apple: I found documentation only for Notes recording, transcription and summary on macOS 27; no confirmed native feature that captures both sides of a video call. Treat any stronger claim as unverified.
- Absence of evidence, not evidence of absence: I did not find any local or open-source tool advertising evidence-linked summary verification, commitment tracking across meetings, or jurisdiction-aware consent workflows, but I did not audit every repository.

### Platform-native meeting intelligence and what the OS gives away for free

By November 2026 capture, transcript and summary are bundled into nearly every platform at little or no marginal price. Zoom includes summaries in paid Workplace plans and sells My Notes standalone, now covering Teams, Meet, Webex and in-person talk without a bot. Google put Gemini notes into Workspace Standard and up, and in June 2026 into consumer AI Pro and Ultra. Slack ships huddle notes from Pro; Notion and ChatGPT record system audio plus microphone from desktop apps. Microsoft splits recap across Teams Premium and Copilot, with Facilitator as a visible agent. Anthropic launched Claude Meetings (beta, Google Meet only, Team and Enterprise) on 10 November 2026; a desktop recorder codenamed Parka is reported but unreleased. The frontier has moved from notes to agents that act on the meeting (ZoomMate, Facilitator, Claude Meetings), billed by credits. All process in the vendor cloud and keep output in the vendor's store. Apple is the exception and the gap: macOS 26 and 27 give on-device building blocks (SpeechAnalyzer, Foundation Models with a pluggable LanguageModel protocol, Siri AI tool calling through App Intents, a Spotlight semantic index) but no system meeting-notes feature was found. Notes transcribes recordings, and call recording covers Phone and one-to-one FaceTime audio, with no documented speaker labels. What platforms do not offer: fully local processing, capture that does not depend on the host's licence, retained audio, vendor-neutral files, and one memory across every tool. Legal pressure is rising, including a July 2026 class action against botless capture.

**What it means for this product.**

- Crowded, do not compete here: 'detect a call, capture without a bot, transcribe, summarise' is now shipped by Notion, ChatGPT and Zoom My Notes from the desktop and bundled into Zoom, Google, Slack and Microsoft plans. Summary quality and botless capture alone make the tool a commodity (https://www.notion.com/help/ai-meeting-notes, https://www.zoom.com/en/products/ai-assistant/features/ai-note-taking).
- Thin, and structural: every platform offering processes audio in the vendor's cloud. Apple is the only on-device player and it stops at microphone notes and one-to-one Phone or FaceTime audio with no speaker labels. A fully local chain with diarization for any call app, any language mix, and off-platform conversations remains something only an independent tool does (https://support.apple.com/en-au/guide/notes/apdb5106e334/4.13/mac/27, https://huggingface.co/blog/anydecisionmodel).
- Thin, and structural: host-independent capture. Platform notes hinge on the organiser's licence, organisation and admin policy, and the newest agents refuse external meetings (Facilitator) or leave when an outsider joins (Claude Meetings). A local recorder gives the owner the same record as guest, host or in a tool with no AI at all. This is worth treating as a headline property, not an accident (https://learn.microsoft.com/microsoftteams/facilitator-teams, https://techcrunch.com/2026/11/10/anthropic-launches-claude-meetings-to-turn-workplace-discussions-into-plans-and-tasks/).
- Distinctive: a durable archive that keeps the audio. Platforms discard audio and keep notes in their own store, so nobody can re-run an old meeting through a better ASR, diarizer or summariser. Retained audio plus plain files plus re-processing on model upgrades (a library that improves with age) is something the cloud products are designed not to offer (https://www.notion.com/help/ai-meeting-notes, https://www.sally.io/blog/chatgpt-record-mode-review).
- An MCP server over the library is the right direction but not unique: vendor connectors (Otter, Granola, Fireflies, Read AI, Fathom) and a verified local connector with 34 tools already exist. The interesting part is depth of memory, not the protocol: commitments tracked across meetings, people and decision graphs, links into the owner's Notion or Obsidian, and per-meeting sensitivity rules that keep restricted meetings away from cloud agents (https://claude.com/connectors/minutes-conversation-memory, https://www.read.ai/resources/best-ai-meeting-notetakers-for-claude-2026-connectors-compared).
- Ride the OS instead of waiting to be absorbed by it: since 3 November 2026 an app can mark App Intents as Siri tools and index its entities into the Spotlight semantic index, testable today on development-signed builds, which is exactly what a personal build is. Meetings, people and action items exposed this way would make the local library answerable from Spotlight and Siri AI on device, a surface Zoom, Microsoft and Google cannot reach on the Mac. Adopting the OS 27 LanguageModel protocol would also make local MLX, Apple on-device and bring-your-own-key models interchangeable behind one API (https://ppc.land/apple-opens-siri-ai-to-all-developers-but-apps-cant-ship-it-until-spring/, https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2026/343/, https://developer.apple.com/documentation/updates/foundationmodels.md).
- Treat the speech stack as maintained commodity, and harden capture: Parakeet-class ASR still beat Apple's SpeechTranscriber in the one public benchmark found and Apple has no diarization, so keeping the current stack is justified, but open packages now ship the same combination, so it is not a moat. The practical risk is capture: verify the app declares the audio-capture usage description, holds the 'System Audio Recording Only' permission on macOS 27, and detects a silent system channel rather than trusting a fallback (https://www.argmaxinc.com/blog/apple-and-argmax, https://huggingface.co/blog/anydecisionmodel, https://github.com/elien666/diarize/pull/3).
- Where it could be more interesting than a notes archive: the platforms' direction is meeting to agent work, but always inside their cloud and their tenant. A local equivalent, where action items become tasks for the owner's own agents through MCP with human confirmation of owner and wording, addresses the top complaint (wrong action items treated as truth) and the top trend at once. Add a simple consent notice and log even for personal use, since the Granola suit attacks invisibility specifically (https://venture-lab.org/2026/copilot-meeting-notes-wrong/, https://www.reworked.co/digital-workplace/anthropic-explores-meeting-recorder-that-assigns-tasks-to-claude-agents/, https://btlaw.com/en/insights/alerts/2026/what-the-granola-class-action-means-for-companies-building-and-deploying-conversation-capture-tools).

**Trends.**

- Basic AI notes are now a bundled feature rather than a product: included in paid Zoom Workplace plans, Slack Pro, Google Workspace Standard and Notion Business, so the standalone price of 'transcript plus summary' trends to zero. <https://www.zoom.com/en/blog/zoom-ai-companion-zoommate/> <https://slack.com/pricing> <https://workspace.google.com/pricing> <https://www.notion.com/pricing>
- Platforms are leaving their own walls: Zoom My Notes covers Teams, Meet, Webex and in-person; Google's notes work in person and on calls hosted on Zoom or Teams; Notion and ChatGPT capture any call from the desktop. Cross-platform capture is no longer exclusive to independents. <https://www.zoom.com/en/products/ai-assistant/features/ai-note-taking> <https://www.zoom.com/en/blog/hidden-cost-fragmented-meeting-intelligence/> <https://www.uctoday.com/unified-communications/google-meet-ai-notes-in-person-meetings> <https://www.notion.com/help/ai-meeting-notes>
- Botless capture of system audio plus microphone has become the default desktop pattern for general-purpose apps (Notion, ChatGPT, Zoom My Notes, and reportedly Claude's unreleased Parka), so 'no bot in the call' is no longer a differentiator. <https://www.notion.com/help/ai-meeting-notes> <https://www.sally.io/blog/chatgpt-record-mode-review> <https://www.zoom.com/en/products/ai-assistant/features/ai-note-taking> <https://www.reworked.co/digital-workplace/anthropic-explores-meeting-recorder-that-assigns-tasks-to-claude-agents/>
- The competitive frontier moved from notes to agents that do the follow-up work: ZoomMate (June 2026), Teams Facilitator, Claude Meetings (10 November 2026), and Parka's reported routing of action items to Claude Code and Cowork. <https://nojitter.com/digital-workplace/how-zoommate-succeeds-zoom-s-custom-ai-companion> <https://learn.microsoft.com/microsoftteams/facilitator-teams> <https://techcrunch.com/2026/11/10/anthropic-launches-claude-meetings-to-turn-workplace-discussions-into-plans-and-tasks/> <https://www.reworked.co/digital-workplace/anthropic-explores-meeting-recorder-that-assigns-tasks-to-claude-agents/>
- Monetisation is shifting from the note itself to metered agent work: ZoomMate seats include 2,200 pooled credits, Notion sells credits for custom agents, Slack caps Slackbot messages, and Apple applies daily quotas to server-side intelligence. <https://nojitter.com/digital-workplace/how-zoommate-succeeds-zoom-s-custom-ai-companion> <https://www.notion.com/pricing> <https://slack.com/pricing> <https://wwdcnotes.com/documentation/wwdc26-319-build-with-the-new-apple-foundation-model-on-private-cloud-compute/>
- Meeting notes reached consumer tiers in 2026: Google extended 'Take notes for me' to personal AI Pro and Ultra subscribers on 29 June 2026, and ChatGPT's recorder is bundled into paid ChatGPT plans. <https://blog.google/products-and-platforms/products/workspace/take-notes-for-me/> <https://help.openai.com/en/articles/11487532-chatgpt-record>
- Apple is shipping on-device building blocks instead of a meeting product: SpeechAnalyzer for transcription, a Foundation Models framework that from OS 27 can drive any model through one protocol, and Siri AI that calls app tools through App Intents and reads app content from a Spotlight semantic index. macOS 27 feature roundups show no system meeting-notes feature. <https://developer.apple.com/documentation/updates/foundationmodels.md> <https://ppc.land/apple-opens-siri-ai-to-all-developers-but-apps-cant-ship-it-until-spring/> <https://www.macworld.com/article/3139330/macos-27-mac-features-siri-apple-intelligence-release-date-compatibility.html> <https://www.macrumors.com/roundup/macos-27/>
- Meeting archives are becoming agent-readable memory through connectors: Fireflies, Otter, Read AI, Fathom, Fellow and Granola publish Claude connectors, Microsoft is exposing real-time Teams transcripts to agents through Work IQ, and a local-only MCP connector is verified in Claude's directory. An analyst notes vendors such as Zoom now charge for MCP access. <https://www.read.ai/resources/best-ai-meeting-notetakers-for-claude-2026-connectors-compared> <https://mc.merill.net/message/RM570651> <https://claude.com/connectors/minutes-conversation-memory> <https://www.computerworld.com/article/4247466/anthropic-targets-workplace-collaboration-with-claude-meetings.html>
- Litigation has widened from bots to botless capture: the consolidated Otter case awaits a motion-to-dismiss ruling, Fireflies faces BIPA and California suits, and Chamberlain v. Granola (filed 30 July 2026) targets invisible system-audio capture and default-off notices. Platforms are responding with visible notices, enforced consent playback, admin approval for external guests and transcript-free recaps. <https://btlaw.com/en/insights/alerts/2026/what-the-granola-class-action-means-for-companies-building-and-deploying-conversation-capture-tools> <https://www.layer3labs.io/guides/ai-note-taker-lawsuits> <https://www.notion.com/help/ai-meeting-notes> <https://www.computerworld.com/article/4247466/anthropic-targets-workplace-collaboration-with-claude-meetings.html>
- On-device speech is commoditising while the macOS capture surface tightens: open packages now bundle Parakeet and Sortformer diarization behind Apple's own API shape, and developers report that since mid-October 2026 Core Audio process taps on macOS 27 require the separate 'System Audio Recording Only' permission, with the ScreenCaptureKit fallback delivering silence. <https://huggingface.co/blog/anydecisionmodel> <https://github.com/elien666/diarize/pull/3> <https://github.com/madduck-tech/backchannel/issues/275> <https://github.com/hab56ur9/transcript/pull/25>

**Complaints and gaps.**

- AI notes get treated as the official record while being wrong: action items assigned to the wrong person, discussion written up as decisions, product and client names garbled. A journalist's Gemini notes assigned her stories she never agreed to write, and a Slack reviewer reports mis-assigned action items when people talk over each other. <https://venture-lab.org/2026/copilot-meeting-notes-wrong/> <https://www.yahoo.com/news/legal-security-experts-ai-notetakers-110013440.html> <https://sagnikbhattacharya.com/blog/slack-ai-small-teams-catch-ups-search-costs>
- Platform notes depend on who hosts and who is licensed. Google requires the organiser to hold an eligible edition and only people in the host's organisation can start notes; Zoom summaries cover hosted meetings; Facilitator is not supported in external meetings and only licensed users can start it; Claude Meetings leaves when an external guest joins unless an admin allows it. A guest often gets nothing. <https://support.google.com/meet/answer/14754931?hl=en> <https://www.zoom.com/en/products/ai-assistant/features/ai-note-taking> <https://learn.microsoft.com/microsoftteams/facilitator-teams> <https://techcrunch.com/2026/11/10/anthropic-launches-claude-meetings-to-turn-workplace-discussions-into-plans-and-tasks/>
- Teams recap is tied to a stored transcript: without transcription there is no Recap tab afterwards, and the transcript-free Copilot mode keeps nothing after the meeting. <https://kurtsh.com/2026/08/28/info-options-for-teams-meeting-summaries-with-or-without-transcriptions/>
- Multilingual meetings are poorly served: Google and Facilitator handle one language per meeting, Notion's speaker identification is English only, and ChatGPT's recorder is described as working best in English. <https://support.google.com/meet/answer/14754931?hl=en> <https://learn.microsoft.com/microsoftteams/facilitator-teams> <https://www.notion.com/help/ai-meeting-notes> <https://www.sally.io/blog/chatgpt-record-mode-review>
- Speaker attribution is weak or absent in the general-purpose recorders: ChatGPT Record is reported to produce one unlabelled block, Notion labels reliably only in English one-to-one desktop calls, and Apple's Speech framework has no diarization module. <https://www.sally.io/blog/chatgpt-record-mode-review> <https://www.notion.com/help/ai-meeting-notes> <https://huggingface.co/blog/anydecisionmodel>
- Audio is usually discarded, so a meeting cannot be replayed or re-transcribed later with a better model: ChatGPT deletes audio after transcription, and Notion deletes the local copy after processing unless local storage is switched on (10 most recent recordings). <https://www.sally.io/blog/chatgpt-record-mode-review> <https://www.notion.com/help/ai-meeting-notes>
- Notes end up scattered across vendor stores with different retention and permissions: a Loop file in the initiator's OneDrive, a Google Doc in the organiser's Drive, Zoom Hub, claude.ai, a ChatGPT canvas. Zoom itself markets against this fragmentation while proposing its own hub as the answer. <https://learn.microsoft.com/microsoftteams/facilitator-teams> <https://support.google.com/meet/answer/14754931?hl=en> <https://www.zoom.com/en/blog/hidden-cost-fragmented-meeting-intelligence/> <https://www.computerworld.com/article/4247466/anthropic-targets-workplace-collaboration-with-claude-meetings.html>
- Apple's built-ins stop short of meetings: no capture of Zoom, Meet, Teams or Slack calls, no speaker labels, no automatic start, FaceTime recording limited to one-to-one audio, an audible notice to both parties, and regional unavailability of call recording (source is a vendor blog reading Apple's guides, plus Apple's own Notes guide which documents none of these capabilities). <https://saymurmur.app/blog/apple-intelligence-meeting-notes> <https://support.apple.com/en-au/guide/notes/apdb5106e334/4.13/mac/27>
- Apple's free models are constrained for transcript work: the on-device context is 4,096 tokens (8K reported on newer hardware), and the 32K Private Cloud Compute model is limited to small App Store developers with a managed entitlement and per-user daily quotas, with no paid tier. <https://developer.apple.com/documentation/updates/foundationmodels.md> <https://wwdcnotes.com/documentation/wwdc26-319-build-with-the-new-apple-foundation-model-on-private-cloud-compute/> <https://daringfireball.net/linked/2026/06/13/pcc-severely-limited-third-party-developers>
- System audio capture on macOS fails silently: developers report no public API to check the audio-tap permission, silence instead of an error when it is declined, and a macOS 27 change in mid-October 2026 that broke taps lacking the NSAudioCaptureUsageDescription permission. <https://github.com/elien666/diarize/pull/3> <https://github.com/madduck-tech/backchannel/issues/275> <https://github.com/martinjsklorz/mixdown/pull/9>

**Not verified.**

- Method limits: about 34 web searches were run before the shared search budget for this run was exhausted; later verification used direct page fetches only. Follow-ups not completed: Zoom's own MCP or connector pricing, first-hand Reddit or forum threads (complaints here come from blogs, vendor reviews and Microsoft and Google documentation), Cisco Webex, and Windows-side OS features.
- OpenAI's help page for ChatGPT Record returned HTTP 403 on every attempt, so plan eligibility (Plus included or not), the session cap (240 versus 120 minutes) and the Google Meet calendar auto-join detail come from search-engine excerpts of that page and from a competitor's April 2026 review. These conflict and are unresolved. ChatGPT plan prices were not captured.
- Teams Premium at $10/user/month and enterprise Microsoft 365 Copilot at $30/user/month come from third-party guides; Microsoft's Teams Premium page timed out. The exact split of recap features between Teams Premium and Copilot in late 2026 was not confirmed on a Microsoft page.
- No evidence was found of a system-level meeting-notes feature in macOS 27, and no credible rumour of one for a later release. This is absence of evidence from two feature roundups and Apple's Notes guide, not a statement from Apple.
- Whether Notes on the Mac can capture system or app audio is not stated on Apple's guide; the claim that it cannot record third-party call apps rests on a vendor blog. Apple's dedicated call-recording support page could not be located by direct fetch, so the Phone and FaceTime audio scope comes from a search excerpt of the Mac User Guide and the same vendor blog.
- Apple's Speech framework 'updates' page did not render, so whether SpeechAnalyzer or SpeechTranscriber changed in macOS 27 is unknown. The 'no diarization' claim rests on a 29 October 2026 third-party write-up and a 2025 vendor feature table.
- On-device Foundation Models context size conflicts across sources (4,096 documented, 8K reported on newer devices). Whether iCloud+ raises Private Cloud Compute quotas for third-party apps also conflicts (session notes say yes, Gruber says no).
- Inference, not sourced: a personal app that is not on the App Store probably cannot obtain the Private Cloud Compute entitlement, since eligibility is framed around the App Store Small Business Program. Likewise, the suggestion that Siri AI tool and entity integration will work for a personally signed build is inferred from the preview's 'development-signed builds' rule and was not tested.
- Whether Siri AI uses Gemini at inference is disputed: press coverage says Siri is powered by Apple Foundation Models and Google Gemini, while a beta teardown found no Gemini assets on device and reads the collaboration as training and infrastructure. Claims in some blogs that MCP became a system-wide Siri feature are contradicted by the same teardown and were not confirmed in Apple material.
- The macOS 27 audio-tap permission change is known only from open-source pull requests and issues (one fetched in full, others seen as search excerpts); no Apple release note was found, and the author of the main report could not establish why behaviour changed in mid-October 2026.
- Zoom branding is unsettled: some third-party sites say the AI Companion name was retired in June 2026, while Zoom's own pages describe features under functional names and still reference AI Companion. Whether Teams and Meet capture in My Notes needs any add-on, and how the audio is captured technically, is not stated by Zoom.
- Google AI Pro and Ultra prices in USD and USD Workspace list prices were not captured from a primary page (the pricing pages were served in another locale). Notion's separate AI add-on being closed to new users in May 2026, and Slack moving huddle notes to all paid plans in June 2026, are single third-party claims.
- Project Parka details (audio sources, transcript, routing to Cowork and Claude Code, macOS 13 minimum) come from one outlet's analysis of a desktop build, relayed by a second outlet; Anthropic has not confirmed it and the original article is partly paywalled. Claude Meetings pricing beyond plan eligibility is not published in the sources read.

### On-device speech and LLM technology frontier, and the native-language question (Rust core vs all-Swift vs Python sidecar)

Late 2026 on-device speech on Apple silicon is a solved commodity at the pipeline level and unsolved at the edges. Parakeet TDT v3 on the Neural Engine (about 2.4 to 2.5% LibriSpeech WER at 110 to 155x real time) is the default baseline, shipped through FluidAudio or Argmax; LLM-based ASR (Qwen3-ASR on MLX) and multi-billion-parameter leaderboard leaders beat it by roughly a point at much higher cost. Apple's SpeechAnalyzer is a credible free fallback but not the accuracy leader and still has no diarization in OS 27. Diarization moved most: NVIDIA's Nemotron 3 Diarization (Sept 2026) lifts the four-speaker streaming cap to eight, while pyannote Community-1 remains the open offline default; real-meeting DER is still roughly 10 to 30%. For LLMs, MLX became the Mac runtime of record (Ollama previewed an MLX engine; mlx-swift-lm 3.x added guided generation), and Apple turned Foundation Models into a provider-agnostic Swift API with Core AI succeeding Core ML. Local summaries are usable from 9B-class models on 16 GB, better at 27 to 35B on 32 GB, but slow and weaker on long multi-speaker meetings. On the language question: Rust dominates cross-platform recorders (Meetily, Anarlog, Handy, Cap, Screenpipe), yet Apple accelerator access from Rust is weak (ONNX Runtime CoreML is reported to fail on Parakeet, mlx-rs is immature), and teams that need reliable capture move it into Swift. Python is leaving the hot path everywhere. The all-Swift recipe is now cloned by many small apps.

**What it means for this product.**

- Crowded: the core recipe (Swift menu-bar app, taps or ScreenCaptureKit, FluidAudio Parakeet on the Neural Engine, pyannote diarization, local or BYOK summary, Markdown/Obsidian/Notion output, MCP server) is now replicated by at least five small native apps (Talat, Muesli, Meeting Transcriber, Transcripted, HushScribe) and by larger Rust/Tauri projects (Meetily at 32.7k stars, Anarlog). Having this pipeline is no longer interesting in itself; what is built on top of it is.
- A Rust core is hard to justify for a macOS-only, single-user tool. Every notable Rust recorder chose Rust for cross-platform reach, and on Apple silicon the Rust inference path today means CPU ONNX Runtime (CoreML provider reported broken for Parakeet) or an immature mlx-rs, so a rewrite would trade away Neural Engine Parakeet or reintroduce a Swift bridge anyway. If the appeal is the experiment, scope Rust to a self-contained, accelerator-free component (index and search, the MCP server, a transcript store) rather than capture or inference, where the evidence favours Swift.
- Dropping the Python sidecar for all-Swift is now well supported and matches where the ecosystem is going: mlx-swift-lm 3.x has guided generation and tool calling, Apple's LanguageModel protocol lets on-device, MLX, Core AI, Claude and Gemini models sit behind one Swift session API, and Meetily, oMLX, HushScribe and Muesli are precedents. The real costs are mlx-swift-lm API churn (pin versions), the Metal shader library needing an Xcode build rather than plain swift build, and rewriting publish sinks. A staged move (summarisation behind the LanguageModel protocol first, publishing last) limits risk.
- Thin and valuable: speaker identity. Upgrading to an eight-speaker streaming diarizer (Nemotron 3 class, already in CoreML ports) and adding persistent voiceprints with calendar-attendee priors, confidence tiers, retroactive relabeling and a correction loop is something only a couple of small projects attempt and none benchmark. For a single user with a stable set of colleagues this compounds over time in a way commodity tools cannot match.
- Thin and cheap to own: a personal evaluation harness. No independent benchmark covers meeting audio end to end, vendor numbers conflict, and a point OS release already produced silent all-blank transcripts in a major SDK. A small regression suite over the owner's own recordings (WER on personal vocabulary, DER, summary faithfulness and schema validity per model) would make model swaps and OS upgrades safe and turn the monthly model churn into an advantage.
- Thin: live intelligence during the meeting. Streaming ASR (Parakeet EOU, Nemotron Unified at 0.56 s) and streaming diarization (0.32 to 1.04 s) are available on device, and purpose-built 2 to 3B summarisers fit in under 3 GB, yet shipping local tools still summarise at the end. Rolling summaries, running decision and action lists, or a live feed into an agent session (one project already pipes the in-progress meeting to a coding agent) are open territory, with battery and heat as the constraint to design around.
- Capture robustness is an underrated differentiator. The failure modes that generate support tickets elsewhere (zero-filled tap buffers, silent permission loss, clock drift and mislabeled sample rates, echo from speakers, mute gaps) are documented and mostly unaddressed in hobby-scale apps. Detecting silent buffers, measuring real buffer cadence, crash-safe fragmented writing and on-device echo cancellation (FluidAudio's LocalVQE is in beta) would make the recorder trustworthy in a way feature lists do not show.
- Agent access is table stakes, so depth is the differentiator: a speaker-resolved, timestamp-cited memory of decisions, commitments and open questions across meetings, exposed through MCP and also through Apple's new surfaces (Foundation Models tools, Spotlight-backed local retrieval, App Intents). Apple's on-device model is a useful zero-download default for short extraction tasks, but its small context and memory floor mean an MLX or BYOK model is still needed for full-length transcripts.

**Trends.**

- Parakeet-class models on the Neural Engine are the on-device ASR default, and accuracy leadership has moved to LLM-based ASR at several times the compute. FluidAudio reports Parakeet TDT v3 at 2.5% LibriSpeech test-clean WER and 155.6x real time on an M4 Pro (v2: 2.1%); a vendor benchmark on an M5 Pro has Parakeet v3 INT8 at 2.37% and 117x versus Qwen3-ASR 1.7B on MLX at 1.32% and 36x and WhisperKit large-v3-turbo at 1.71% and 12x. Published speed figures for the same model differ by source (about 110x on the model card vs 155.6x in the docs). <https://docs.fluidinference.com/reference/benchmarks.md> <https://soniqo.audio/benchmarks> <https://huggingface.co/FluidInference/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v3-coreml>
- The Open ASR leaderboard lead changed hands repeatedly in 2026 (Cohere Transcribe at 5.42% in March, IBM Granite Speech 4.1 at 5.33%, NVIDIA Nemotron 3.5 Transcribe at 5.33% in late October, per secondary snapshots) while Parakeet TDT v3 sits near 6.3%. The gap to a 0.6B model is about one WER point, and drop-in fine-tunes of the same architecture (Moondream Parakeet Ultra: 5.80 vs 6.26) keep narrowing it. <https://select.keenable.ai/r/speech-recognition-accuracy-leaders-2022-2026/JbURQjlVooLQ6J10NkbRVw> <https://codesota.com/speech/stt-leaderboard> <https://huggingface.co/moondream/parakeet-ultra>
- Apple's SpeechAnalyzer is now a real free baseline (2.12% / 4.56% on LibriSpeech clean/other, about 3x faster than Whisper Small) but trails Parakeet and Whisper large-v3-turbo on FLEURS in every core language tested under macOS 27, covers about 30 locales, and gained no diarization at WWDC26. <https://lyonesse.app/blog/apple-speech-api-benchmark.html> <https://whispernotes.app/fr-BE/blog/apple-speech-vs-whisper> <https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/829751>
- Streaming diarization broke its four-speaker ceiling. NVIDIA's Nemotron 3 Diarization (23 Sep 2026) handles eight speakers at 0.32 to 1.04 s buffers and beats streaming Sortformer v2.1 on every reported dataset; within weeks it appeared as CoreML and MLX ports (about 11% overall DER on a 132-recording, eight-language set), in parakeet-rs, and as a mode in at least one open-source Swift recorder. <https://huggingface.co/nvidia/Nemotron-3-Diarization> <https://soniqo.audio/benchmarks/diarization> <https://lib.rs/crates/parakeet-rs> <https://github.com/pasrom/meeting-transcriber>
- Apple turned Foundation Models into a provider-agnostic LLM API at WWDC26: a LanguageModel protocol with open-source MLX and Core AI conformers and Claude and Gemini packages, a larger on-device context (reported 8,192 tokens, 32K on Private Cloud Compute), image input, a Spotlight-backed local retrieval tool, and Core AI as the successor to Core ML for transformer models. <https://dev.to/hariharanjagan/whats-new-in-apples-foundation-models-framework-at-wwdc-2026-5227> <https://infoq.com/news/2026/06/apple-core-ai-wwdc> <https://macrumors.com/2026/06/08/apple-unveils-xcode-and-models-improvements>
- MLX is becoming the default LLM runtime on Macs while llama.cpp stays the cross-platform choice. Ollama's 0.19 preview moved its Apple silicon engine to MLX with roughly 2x decode speed; mlx-swift-lm reached a 3.x line with guided generation and tool calling in Swift; products that must also run on Windows (Hedy) keep llama.cpp with Metal and Vulkan. <https://ollama.com/blog/mlx> <https://github.com/ml-explore/mlx-swift-lm/releases> <https://hedy.ai/post/local-ai-engineering-deep-dive-hedy-3-2/>
- Local summarisation has settled into RAM tiers and is still almost always batch. Hedy's shipping matrix: 2 to 4B models on 8 GB, Gemma 4 E4B or Qwen 3.5 9B on 16 GB, 27 to 31B on 32 GB; local summaries take about 30 seconds on a standard model and up to five minutes. Purpose-built small summarisers exist (Liquid's LFM2-2.6B-Transcript claims a 60-minute, 10K-token transcript in 16 s under 3 GB). Shipping local tools stream the transcript live but summarise when the meeting ends; rolling in-meeting summaries are mostly tutorials. <https://hedy.ai/post/local-ai-engineering-deep-dive-hedy-3-2/> <https://www.liquid.ai/blog/the-future-of-meeting-summarization-local-fast-private-and-fully-secure> <https://dev.to/andrew-ooo/meetily-review-2026-privacy-first-ai-meeting-assistant-29h3> <https://picovoice.ai/blog/build-real-time-meeting-summarization-tool/>
- Python is leaving the hot path of local AI desktop apps. Meetily archived its FastAPI backend in favour of a single Rust process; oMLX retired its PyObjC menu-bar app for a native Swift app (its server stays Python); OpenWhispr chose a native sherpa-onnx binary explicitly to avoid Python and PyTorch; all-Swift speech stacks (FluidAudio, speech-swift on MLX Swift plus CoreML) and in-process mlx-swift-lm summarisation (HushScribe, Muesli) are now normal. <https://instagit.com/Zackriya-Solutions/meetily/meetily-tauri-app-vs-fastapi-backend/> <https://github.com/jundot/omlx/releases/tag/v0.4.0> <https://feedback.openwhispr.com/blog/local-speaker-diarization> <https://forums.swift.org/t/speech-swift-on-device-speech-processing-for-apple-silicon-asr-tts-diarization-speech-to-speech/85182>
- Capture is moving to native Swift even in non-native apps, while Rust capture bindings have matured without closing the accelerator gap. Circleback rebuilt its Electron recording engine in Swift in two months (ScreenCaptureKit, frame-indexed mixing across three clocks, fragmented MP4) and says recording went from a top support-ticket source to boring; Recall's reference build keeps Electron for UI and a Swift helper for media. In Rust, cpal added macOS loopback in 0.17.0 (Dec 2025) with silence and device-collision fixes in 0.18.0 (Jun 2026) and the screencapturekit crate is at 11.1.2 (Nov 2026), yet Screenpipe and Anarlog still lean on MLX and Apple Speech for acceleration. <https://circleback.ai/blog/how-we-rebuilt-our-electron-recording-engine-in-swift> <https://www.recall.ai/blog/how-i-built-a-botless-meeting-recorder-from-scratch> <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/RustAudio/cpal/master/CHANGELOG.md> <https://docs.rs/crate/screencapturekit/latest>
- Agent-facing access is now table stakes and the Rust-built recorders are diverging on business model and UI. MCP servers ship in Talat, Anarlog, Transcripted and Screenpipe; Screenpipe (YC S26) moved from MIT to a source-available commercial license; Anarlog went the other way from GPL to MIT; Cap now describes its desktop app as native Rust with GPUI rather than a web view (its repo topics still mention Tauri). <https://techcrunch.com/2026/03/24/talats-ai-meeting-notes-stay-on-your-machine-not-in-the-cloud/> <https://anarlog.so/about/> <https://github.com/r3dbars/transcripted> <https://hn.nuxt.dev/item/49024620>

**Complaints and gaps.**

- No native diarization from Apple. SpeechAnalyzer has none in OS 27, the WWDC26 catalog did not mention it, and a developer question about it sits unanswered; the working pattern is to bolt on FluidAudio and assign speakers to transcript segments by time overlap. <https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/829751> <https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/819555> <https://www.argmaxinc.com/blog/apple-and-argmax>
- On-device diarization is still error-prone on real meeting audio and vendor numbers conflict. pyannote Community-1 is stated at 19.9% DER on AMI SDM; FluidAudio's own docs show 26.2% (streaming) and 31.7% (Sortformer) on AMI SDM and say streaming is 10 to 15% worse than offline; Nemotron 3 rises to about 27 to 29% DER with 5 to 9 speakers; pyannote ranks itself first while an independent set ranks Community-1 last on DER in several languages. <https://www.pyannote.ai/blog/top-7-speaker-diarization-apis-and-models-in-2026> <https://docs.fluidinference.com/reference/benchmarks.md> <https://huggingface.co/nvidia/Nemotron-3-Diarization> <https://soniqo.audio/benchmarks/diarization>
- Models output anonymous labels only; persistent speaker identity is do-it-yourself and brittle. One team that built it documents the failure modes: utterances under about 0.8 s give unreliable embeddings, overlapping speech cannot be represented by one label, new speakers are weak until several samples exist, clustering thresholds split or merge people, far-field audio degrades results. Vendor blogs also report Granola falling back to generic or Me/Them labels. <https://feedback.openwhispr.com/blog/local-speaker-diarization> <https://the-decoder.com/nvidia-drops-a-free-100m-parameter-model-that-identifies-up-to-eight-speakers-in-real-time/> <https://anarlog.so/blog/granola-ai-complaints/>
- System-audio capture on macOS remains fragile. Developers report Core Audio process taps delivering all-zero buffers while audio is audible, silent permission denials, and the need for an aggregate device; no capture API applies echo cancellation, so speaker bleed duplicates speech in transcripts; device clocks drift and some virtual drivers report 48 kHz while delivering 44.1 kHz; mic mute produces gaps. cpal's loopback path shipped bugs that returned silence until 0.18.0. <https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/825780> <https://dev.to/nickdelv/2000-buffers-of-nothing-3i8> <https://www.recall.ai/blog/core-audio-taps> <https://circleback.ai/blog/how-we-rebuilt-our-electron-recording-engine-in-swift>
- OS updates silently break on-device pipelines. FluidAudio had to move a mel spectrogram to CPU to fix all-blank CTC output on M1/M4 under macOS 27.0.1; macOS 27 betas changed CGWindowList behaviour and added a separate Remote Desktop permission entry; Apple updates its own speech model at its discretion with fixes only in the next OS release; Core AI models load slowly the first time because of per-device specialization. <https://github.com/FluidInference/FluidAudio/releases> <https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/841530> <https://www.argmaxinc.com/blog/apple-and-argmax> <https://infoq.com/news/2026/06/apple-core-ai-wwdc>
- Small local LLMs remain the weak link for long meetings. Hedy reports its smaller models are weaker on long multi-speaker meetings and that phone-class models lose the thread, with local summaries ranging from five seconds to five minutes plus heat and battery drain; Apple's on-device model is capped at a reported 8,192 tokens (4,096 in community reports before) while a one-hour transcript is about 10K tokens, and its newest version reportedly needs 12 GB of unified memory; Anarlog warns that running speech and summary models together strains the machine. <https://hedy.ai/post/local-ai-engineering-deep-dive-hedy-3-2/> <https://dev.to/hariharanjagan/whats-new-in-apples-foundation-models-framework-at-wwdc-2026-5227> <https://www.liquid.ai/blog/the-future-of-meeting-summarization-local-fast-private-and-fully-secure> <https://mjtsai.com/blog/?p=52279>
- Rust has no first-class path to Apple's accelerators. A March 2026 integration report says ONNX Runtime's CoreML provider loads Parakeet but crashes at inference (issue #26355) and calls CPU the safe default with WebGPU experimental; mlx-rs describes itself as in active development, has about 390 stars, warns of segfaults with captured arrays in closures and needs Xcode-specific build setup; the same report falls back to a Swift XPC service running FluidAudio for more than four speakers. <https://moklabs-research.pages.dev/reports/technology/2026-03-20-parakeet-rs-tauri-integration-patterns> <https://github.com/oxiglade/mlx-rs> <https://lib.rs/crates/parakeet-rs>
- Webview shells carry their own bill. A six-month Tauri retrospective lists WKWebView quirks (smart-quote substitution breaking parsing), IPC serialization overhead that forced paging, and most breakage coming from the Linux webview stack; Tauri developers asking for system audio are told to use native ScreenCaptureKit in Swift; Circleback concluded a render process is the wrong place for realtime capture. <https://hackernoon.com/six-months-with-tauri-the-benefits-and-the-bill> <https://www.github.com/tauri-apps/tauri/discussions/9581> <https://circleback.ai/blog/how-we-rebuilt-our-electron-recording-engine-in-swift>
- There is no trustworthy benchmark for the whole meeting pipeline. Published tests are read speech (LibriSpeech, FLEURS), run by vendors, on different hardware and OS versions; SpeechAnalyzer looks best on LibriSpeech and worst on FLEURS; authors themselves say meeting audio, accents and far-field were not tested; no source measures summary faithfulness or schema adherence on real transcripts. <https://lyonesse.app/blog/apple-speech-api-benchmark.html> <https://whispernotes.app/fr-BE/blog/apple-speech-vs-whisper> <https://soniqo.audio/benchmarks> <https://www.pyannote.ai/blog/top-7-speaker-diarization-apis-and-models-in-2026>
- The best capabilities keep landing behind licenses or paywalls. Argmax's Parakeet builds are Pro-only; Voxtral's diarization exists only in the closed API model; pyannote's streaming model (Live-1) is paid; Nemotron 3 Diarization ships under OpenMDW 1.1 with a separately gated evaluation-only preview and targets NVIDIA GPUs on Linux first; Screenpipe moved from MIT to a source-available commercial license; mlx-swift-lm broke its API at 3.0 and flags instability on main. <https://huggingface.co/argmaxinc/parakeetkit-pro> <https://mistral.ai/news/voxtral-transcribe-2> <https://www.pyannote.ai/blog/top-7-speaker-diarization-apis-and-models-in-2026> <https://huggingface.co/nvidia/Nemotron-3-Diarization>

**Not verified.**

- Process note: the shared web search budget for this run was exhausted after roughly 49 searches, so several follow-ups were done by fetching primary pages only. Not verified by search: late-2026 maturity of candle and Burn Metal backends for speech, upstream maintenance status of whisper-rs, and head-to-head Qwen3-ASR vs Parakeet results beyond the single vendor benchmark cited. The advisor review tool was rate-limited and could not be consulted.
- Apple's official documentation for the on-device context window could not be read (the developer docs page returned no body). The 8,192-token on-device and 32K PCC figures, the free PCC tier threshold and the LanguageModel protocol details come from one third-party WWDC26 write-up; treat as likely but unconfirmed against Apple primary sources.
- Open ASR leaderboard figures for Cohere Transcribe, Granite Speech 4.1 and Nemotron 3.5 Transcribe, and the Nemotron 3.5 Transcribe size and release date (about 34B, 28 Oct 2026), come from search-result snippets of secondary blogs, not from the live leaderboard or NVIDIA's model card.
- FluidAudio's exact current model roster is approximate: one fetch of the repository page listed many new models (Parakeet Ultra, Phonon-2, Bark 3.0 and others) while a fetch of the raw README returned older text with a 0.12.4 install line. The releases page (v0.17.5 to v0.18.3) is the more reliable source and is what the entries above rely on.
- The Argmax open-source SDK rename and v1.0.0 date (1 May 2026) come from a search snippet; the fetched registry page confirmed only v1.1.0 (6 Aug 2026) and the three-module layout. Argmax Pro pricing is from the July 2025 GA post and may have changed; the Pro SDK changelog is behind a login.
- Cap's move to GPUI is stated on its own site and README, but no migration post was found and an earlier search snippet still described a Tauri v2 plus SolidStart app; the timing and reasons for the change are unknown.
- The statement that reviewers find Talat's local summaries hit or miss on longer meetings appeared only in a search snippet (the source PDF did not parse) and is not used as a sourced claim above.
- Date inconsistencies noticed in sources: the InfoQ summary dates the Core AI announcement to 20 June 2026 while other coverage says 8 June (probably article date vs keynote date); one benchmark page lists its OS as macOS 25.5, which looks like a typo for 26.5; some SEO guides cite model names such as Qwen 3.8 that could not be verified.
- Inference, not sourced: that a Rust core for this specific app would forfeit Neural Engine Parakeet unless it called back into Swift, and that a staged Swift migration (summarisation first, publishing last) is the lowest-risk order. These follow from the cited evidence but no source states them for this use case.
- No first-hand retrospective was found from a team that rewrote a meeting recorder from Swift into Rust, or from Rust back into Swift. The evidence on rewrites is Electron to Swift (Circleback), Python to Rust (Meetily), PyObjC to Swift (oMLX), and apparently Tauri to GPUI (Cap).

### Agent and MCP ecosystem: meetings as memory for AI agents and personal knowledge tools

By November 2026, exposing meetings to agents is table stakes. Every large cloud notetaker reviewed (Granola, Fireflies, Otter, Fathom, Read AI, tl;dv, Zoom) runs a hosted, OAuth-gated MCP server, several on free plans, and they surface as connectors in Claude, ChatGPT, Cursor and Codex. Incumbents are repositioning from note-taking to a context layer for agents: Granola raised at a 1.5 billion dollar valuation and shipped Spaces and APIs, Otter launched a knowledge-graph pitch with MCP in both directions, Superhuman bought Fathom to feed its agents, and Notion lets a finished meeting note trigger Custom Agents. Meta's purchase of Limitless ended a prominent lifelog product. On the local side there is a long tail of local-first macOS recorders with local MCP servers (Minutes, Daisy, Transcripted, Talat and others), mostly open source and mostly on the same Parakeet or Whisper plus FluidAudio stack, some built in reaction to Granola's 30-day free limit and its March 2026 cache change. That slot is crowded by count but small by traction. What the pages read did not show is depth: commitments and decisions tracked as stateful objects across meetings, durable per-person memory, fine-grained control and audit of what an agent may read, and independent evidence of what people actually do with meeting MCP. Most usage claims come from vendor blogs, and the one cross-vendor benchmark was run by a competitor. MCP itself matured with the 2026-07-28 specification, which still leaves security decisions to implementers.

**What it means for this product.**

- Crowded: record, transcribe on device, summarise, and offer an MCP server that searches past meetings. Cloud vendors give this away on free tiers and several open-source Mac apps ship the same Parakeet or Whisper, FluidAudio, Markdown and local MCP shape. More read tools will not make the product distinctive (Minutes already lists 34), and matching incumbents on breadth of agentic follow-up (Notion triggers, Otter connectors, Tana in-call agents) is a losing race for one person.
- Thinnest area in the pages read: a commitment and decision ledger with state. Model commitments (who, to whom, what, by when, under what condition) and decisions (proposed, confirmed, superseded) as first-class records linked to transcript spans, and resolve them from later meetings (open, kept, slipped). Others store action items per meeting; none of the pages read showed the lifecycle. Exposed over MCP this answers questions like what do I owe a given person and what changed since a decision.
- People memory is partly taken, so aim past naming speakers. Daisy and Transcripted already remember named voices and Omi exposes people tools. What was not found is a per-person timeline across meetings (topics, open commitments both ways, last decisions) and a pre-meeting brief generated from it. Tana's critique that notetakers do not prepare you or carry context forward points at the same gap. On-device diarization with persistent enrolment is the prerequisite you already have.
- Make agent privacy a product surface. No page read showed per-meeting sensitivity labels, tiered exposure (metadata only, summary only, full transcript), per-client rules (local model versus cloud assistant), or a readable log of every agent read for a personal tool; vendors mostly offer an on or off connector. A single-user local library can do this cheaply, and it is the control that cloud tools structurally struggle to offer.
- Treat transcripts as untrusted input. Other people's speech reaches the agent through your server, and Microsoft's review describes exactly that injection path plus local stdio risks. Keep read tools separate from anything that writes or publishes, keep actions opt-in (Daisy's pattern), and check the server against the 2026-07-28 deprecations (Roots, Sampling, Logging, HTTP plus SSE) if it relies on any of them.
- Evidence-first answers are a cheap differentiator. The competitor-run benchmark shows misheard identifiers and speaker confusion flowing into agent output, and the behaviour it praised was Fathom linking every transcript line. Returning speaker, timestamp and an audio link with every claim, plus a custom vocabulary for names and ticket IDs, and telling clients to verify against source records (as Omi's server does), makes local answers more trustworthy than cloud ones.
- Coding-agent memory scoped to a repository suits a solo developer and is only loosely served. Vendors pitch it over per-user cloud corpora, and Minutes and Transcripted ship Claude Code plugins, so a plugin alone is not novel. The distinctive version maps meetings to projects, exposes decisions affecting this repository with provenance, and drafts decision records from design discussions for approval.
- Durability is a feature, and the radical option is to invert the product. 2026 showed a vendor changing local storage under its users, a lifelog product switched off after acquisition, and a notetaker absorbed into a suite. Plain files plus an owned index with a documented schema is a real advantage. Taken further: treat capture as a commodity input and make the ledger, people memory and policy layer the product, able to ingest Markdown from other recorders as well as your own.

| Daisy (open source) | local | Apache-2.0 local-first Mac recorder: Whisper on device, bring-your-own or local summariser, localhost MCP server with read tools plus opt-in actions. | Launched on Product Hunt in 2026 (about five months before Nov 2026). MCP binds to 127.0.0.1 with a bearer token; tools include list, get and search sessions, rename_speaker, and opt-in resummarize and routing actions; no tool can delete. Named speakers are remembered through a local voice fingerprint. Small traction: 18 stars, 2 reviews. | Free during beta, no account. | <https://www.producthunt.com/posts/daisy-4> <https://github.com/addicted-studio/daisy-app> |
| Transcripted (open source) | local | MIT native Swift Mac app: Parakeet V3 by default, Markdown notes in a user-chosen folder, read-only MCP server, and a live Claude Code plugin that feeds in-meeting context to the coding agent. | Requires macOS 26 on Apple silicon. 91 stars, 7,070 commits. Suggests a remembered name when a known speaker talks again; audio can auto-delete after 7 or 30 days. Sentry and PostHog are on by default and can be disabled. | Free, no account or subscription. | <https://github.com/r3dbars/transcripted> |
| Talat | local | One-time-purchase offline notetaker for Mac and Windows: Core Audio taps, FluidAudio on CoreML with Parakeet-class models, local LLM summaries, exports and MCP connectors. | Started after Granola limited free users to 30 days of history; the builder frames privacy and offline capability as the main features and leaves workflows to exports and MCP. | $49 one time, no account (reported May 14, 2026). | <https://www.weekinvoiceai.com/p/how-talat-built-a-local-first-stack> |

An MCP server and CLI are mentioned only by a third-party blog (Oct 30, 2026); the vendor pages read do not mention MCP. | Free for local and own-key use; Pro $14 per month or $140 per year (vendor post dated Oct 12, 2026). | <https://anarlog.so/blog/local-ai-meeting-notes> <https://anarlog.so/blog/char-is-now-anarlog> <https://char.com/blog/hyprnote-is-now-char> |
