MCP tool names are not listed there. | Not stated on the page read. | <https://pieces.app/features/mcp> |

**Trends.**

- Official hosted MCP servers are now standard for cloud notetakers and have reached free tiers. tl;dv's November 2026 comparison covered six official servers (Fathom, Fireflies, tl;dv, Otter, Read AI, Granola); Fireflies and Granola both state MCP is on every plan, with Granola's free tier limited to 30 days of personal notes. Caveat: tl;dv is one of the six and ran the test itself. <https://tldv.io/blog/meeting-mcp-servers/> <https://fireflies.ai/blog/fireflies-mcp-server/> <https://www.granola.ai/blog/granola-mcp> <https://www.granola.ai/pricing>
- Vendors are repositioning from notetaker to context or knowledge layer for agents. TechCrunch notes meeting notes are commoditising and Granola is focusing on follow-on actions; Otter now sells a knowledge graph of conversations, decisions and people; Superhuman bought Fathom to make meeting data available to its agents; Circleback argues meetings are the richest context source for AI. <https://techcrunch.com/2026/03/25/granola-raises-125m-hits-1-5b-valuation-as-it-expands-from-meeting-notetaker-to-enterprise-ai-app/> <https://otter.ai/blog/otter-ai-evolves-from-ai-notetaker-to-create-100b-enterprise-conversational-knowledge-engine-market> <https://blog.superhuman.com/superhuman-acquires-fathom/> <https://circleback.ai/blog/meeting-data-as-ai-context>
- Consolidation and platform absorption. Superhuman acquired Fathom (Sep 14, 2026). Meta acquired Limitless (formerly Rewind) in December 2025: pendant sales stopped, and a vendor blog reports recording features in the Rewind and Limitless apps were disabled on Dec 19, 2025. ChatGPT captures meetings natively on Mac. Google extended Take notes for me to in-person meetings and reported over 110 million attendees using it in a month. <https://blog.superhuman.com/superhuman-acquires-fathom/> <https://www.uctoday.com/devices-workspace-tech/meta-acquires-limitless-ai-wearables/> <https://www.usecarly.com/blog/meta-limitless-acquisition/> <https://smartscope.blog/en/blog/chatgpt-meetings-plugin-plans-macos-record-2026/>
- Agentic follow-up is moving from extracting action items to triggering agents, usually with a human approval step. Notion starts a Custom Agent when a meeting note is summarised; Otter pushes summaries to a Notion page or a Gmail draft when a call ends; Tana says every agent output is a proposal the user approves; Superhuman's example is an agent drafting status updates for a project manager to approve. <https://www.notion.com/releases/2026-07-31> <https://otter.ai/blog/otter-ai-evolves-from-ai-notetaker-to-create-100b-enterprise-conversational-knowledge-engine-market> <https://tana.inc/blog/why-ai-notetakers-fail-to-drive-action-2026> <https://blog.superhuman.com/superhuman-acquires-fathom/>
- Coding agents are a named audience. Read AI pitched its MCP server (Feb 25, 2026) as a dev tool, with the example of generating an endpoint from a sprint-planning transcript; Zoom shipped a Codex plugin and a Claude Code plugin; Minutes ships a Claude Code plugin with 23 skills; Transcripted ships a live plugin that puts in-meeting context above the Claude Code prompt. All of these descriptions are vendor or project authored. <https://read.ai/post/read-ai-mcp-your-meetings-just-became-your-most-powerful-dev-tool> <https://news.zoom.com/zoom-mcp-expanded-capabilities/> <https://github.com/silverstein/minutes> <https://github.com/r3dbars/transcripted>
- A long tail of local-first Mac recorders with local MCP has formed on a shared stack (Parakeet or Whisper, FluidAudio or CoreML, Markdown files): Minutes, Daisy, Transcripted, Talat, plus OpenOats and others. Part of the impetus was Granola's 30-day free limit and its March 2026 cache change. Traction is small: Daisy 18 stars, Transcripted 91, Minutes about 1.6k. <https://github.com/silverstein/minutes> <https://github.com/addicted-studio/daisy-app> <https://github.com/r3dbars/transcripted> <https://www.weekinvoiceai.com/p/how-talat-built-a-local-first-stack>
- Meeting MCP surfaces are moving from read-only to selective write. In March 2026 Circleback described current servers as primarily read-only. By autumn, tl;dv's test found Granola creating folders and moving notes, Fireflies sharing and moving meetings, and Read AI sharing reports; Omi's server creates, edits and deletes memories and action items; Daisy gates its write tools behind opt-in. <https://circleback.ai/blog/meeting-data-as-ai-context> <https://tldv.io/blog/meeting-mcp-servers/> <https://docs.omi.me/doc/developer/mcp/introduction.md> <https://github.com/addicted-studio/daisy-app>
- The protocol matured while security stayed the implementer's job. The 2026-07-28 MCP specification made the core stateless, hardened authorization, moved Tasks and MCP Apps into extensions, replaced server-initiated elicitation with multi round-trip requests, and deprecated Roots, Sampling, Logging and the HTTP plus SSE transport with roughly a year of support. Microsoft's June 2026 review says the protocol does not enforce security and that local stdio servers bring file-system and subprocess risk. <https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/2026-07-28/> <https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/microsoft-security-blog/the-state-of-mcp-security-in-2026/4531327>
- Knowledge tools are becoming agent memory and absorbing capture. Notion rebuilt its MCP so outside agents can store and read context, with memory sync live in October 2026. Tana and Mem both record without a bot (Mem's desktop recording runs on a third-party SDK from Recall.ai). Obsidian has a crowd of community capture plugins, some fully local (Echostrata, Scuttlebutt) and one that syncs from a separate local recorder. Basic Memory exposes a Markdown and SQLite knowledge graph over MCP that opens in Obsidian. The Mem, Obsidian and Basic Memory points are search-listing level. <https://www.notion.com/releases/2026-10-19> <https://tana.inc/agentic-meetings> <https://get.mem.ai/blog/your-meetings-on-autopilot> <https://www.recall.ai/customers/mem-ai>
- Data ownership and pricing model became flashpoints. When Granola changed its local cache, users who had pointed agents at it objected; reported reactions stressed that the value is in the raw transcript and that some users do not want a subscription attached. One-time-purchase and free open-source tools are positioned against that. <https://www.weekinvoiceai.com/p/week-in-voice-ai12-granola-scramble> <https://www.weekinvoiceai.com/p/how-talat-built-a-local-first-stack> <https://techcrunch.com/2026/03/25/granola-raises-125m-hits-1-5b-valuation-as-it-expands-from-meeting-notetaker-to-enterprise-ai-app/>

**Complaints and gaps.**

- Extraction is not execution. Tana (a vendor) argues capture is solved and that an extracted action item only describes work someone still has to do; Granola's own guide says owners and deadlines are not assigned automatically and need a manual post-call review. <https://tana.inc/blog/why-ai-notetakers-fail-to-drive-action-2026> <https://www.granola.ai/blog/stakeholder-meetings-ai-notetakers-track-commitments-follow-ups>
- Commitment tracking is a workflow, not a stateful feature, in the pages read. Granola builds it from templates, chat queries and folders, has no native Jira or Asana integration, and warns that conditions attached to a commitment can be dropped. Minutes stores action items and decisions in frontmatter and Omi exposes action-item tools, but no page read showed a commitment's lifecycle across meetings (kept, slipped, superseded) or conditional commitments as first-class records. <https://www.granola.ai/blog/stakeholder-meetings-ai-notetakers-track-commitments-follow-ups> <https://github.com/silverstein/minutes> <https://docs.omi.me/doc/developer/mcp/introduction.md>
- Decisions blur with discussion, and cross-meeting recall is mostly search. Granola's own post argues meeting search alone is not enough; Tana (vendor) says each meeting becomes an isolated record so decisions go stale and get re-discussed; practitioner guides recommend separating proposed, confirmed and conditional decisions and having a participant verify against the transcript. <https://www.granola.ai/blog/second-brain-meetings-organizational-memory> <https://tana.inc/blog/why-ai-notetakers-fail-to-drive-action-2026> <https://www.digitalapplied.com/blog/ai-meeting-notes-decision-record> <https://anarlog.so/blog/meeting-decision-log>
- Speaker identity and proper nouns are weak links that flow straight into agent answers. In tl;dv's competitor-run test Granola labelled speakers by device rather than person, Fathom merged two speakers and mislabelled guests, and several tools misheard ticket identifiers and names. The developer of an on-device diarization app on Hacker News concedes his speaker system tends to split one speaker into several. <https://tldv.io/blog/meeting-mcp-servers/> <https://hn.svelte.dev/item/48415709>
- Control over what an agent may see is coarse. Granola's connector is toggled per user, with advanced access controls listed as planned for Enterprise; Zoom's and Otter's announcements give no scope detail; Notion's new controls cover who connected which tool and revocation. A competitor (Spinach) lists no admin control over which meetings are exposed and no PII redaction as Granola gaps. No page read described per-meeting or per-field scoping of agent reads, or an audit log of agent reads, for a personal tool. Partial counter-examples: Daisy separates read tools from opt-in actions; Pieces excludes capture per app. <https://www.granola.ai/blog/granola-mcp> <https://news.zoom.com/zoom-mcp-expanded-capabilities/> <https://otter.ai/blog/otter-ai-evolves-from-ai-notetaker-to-create-100b-enterprise-conversational-knowledge-engine-market> <https://www.notion.com/releases/2026-10-13>
- Local MCP servers for personal data carry real security risk. Microsoft's 2026 review says many servers run locally over stdio with file-system and subprocess access, that command injection is among the largest vulnerability classes reported this year, and that agents can be steered by instructions planted in data a tool returns. The protocol itself does not enforce the defences. <https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/microsoft-security-blog/the-state-of-mcp-security-in-2026/4531327> <https://blog.modelcontextprotocol.io/posts/2026-07-28/>
- Lock-in and access cliffs. Granola's free plan reaches 30 days of history and API access needs a paid plan; its March cache change broke user-built agent workflows. Limitless users in several regions lost service after the Meta deal with a short export window (vendor-blog report). Superhuman's post leaves the future of standalone Fathom unstated. <https://www.granola.ai/pricing> <https://www.weekinvoiceai.com/p/week-in-voice-ai12-granola-scramble> <https://www.usecarly.com/blog/meta-limitless-acquisition/> <https://blog.superhuman.com/superhuman-acquires-fathom/>
- Consent and legal exposure are unresolved, and voiceprints are the sharp edge. An AP report (July 2026) says Illinois law treats voiceprints as biometric identifiers needing written notice, informed consent and a retention and destruction policy, and quotes lawyers worried about privilege once conversations reach a third party. ChatGPT's Meetings beta reminds only the operator and does not notify other participants. <https://jamaica-gleaner.com/article/business/20260710/ai-note-takers-promise-easy-meeting-recaps-becomes-data-someone-else> <https://smartscope.blog/en/blog/chatgpt-meetings-plugin-plans-macos-record-2026/>
- Meeting MCP is rough in practice, per the one comparative test found (run by a competitor): partial-result warnings on most Fireflies transcript fetches, searches that failed until retried, caps such as three transcript reads per question or 50 meetings per query, duplicate action items, and a permission prompt before every Granola tool call. <https://tldv.io/blog/meeting-mcp-servers/>
- Independent evidence of real usage is thin. Nearly every description of what people do with meeting MCP is vendor-authored; the one Reddit thread located (r/AI_Agents, May 19, 2026) asks whether anyone treats meeting data as agent memory rather than just notes, and the retrieved copy carried no replies. Circleback itself concedes the technology is not fully mature. On the local side, one developer reported a local Qwen model struggling with long JSON-heavy extraction prompts. <https://bittide.aicompass.dev/article/a55464ea-65b2-4941-9585-6678a5fb98a7> <https://circleback.ai/blog/meeting-data-as-ai-context> <https://hn.svelte.dev/item/48415709>

**Not verified.**

- Search budget: the shared web search limit was exhausted after roughly 33 searches. Not searched or not resolved: Reflect and Logseq meeting capture, any Microsoft Work IQ or Copilot MCP exposure of Teams transcripts, Tana's MCP documentation, the Claude connector directory listing for meeting tools, and any 2026 litigation against notetaker vendors. Treat absence of these as not checked, not as absence in the market.
- No Reddit or Hacker News thread with substantive replies about meeting MCP usage was retrieved. Statements about what users do with these servers are therefore vendor claims; the gap list reflects that rather than user testimony.
- Architecture labels for Granola and Tana (hybrid) are my reading of the fetched pages: capture runs on the user's machine while the MCP endpoint, sharing and workspace features are hosted. From prior knowledge, not re-verified here: Granola's transcription and summarisation run in the cloud.
- Snippet-only local projects, not verified on primary pages (several returned 403): Mnemos, Ghosty Notes, Speechmark, Meet2Notes and Meetey are described in search listings as on-device Mac recorders with local MCP servers, several on Parakeet. Their activity, licences and adoption are unknown.
- Fathom: a launch date of April 2026 for its official MCP server and the tool count (7 or 9) appeared only in search summaries of tl;dv posts. Fireflies: a beta launch date of June 25, 2025 comes from a Substack listing in search results. Tana: a Local API and MCP launch date of Jan 30, 2026 comes from a single directory site. None confirmed on vendor pages.
- Minutes: figures conflict across captures (31 or 34 tools, 1.3k or 1.6k stars, Whisper or Parakeet as the engine). A search summary described a local relationship graph of people, commitments and topics; the repo page fetched did not show it.
- Screenpipe: its current about page pitches enterprise workflow discovery with a source-available capture engine and lists no prices or MCP details, which conflicts with directory listings that describe an open-source personal screen and audio memory with an MCP server and a one-time price. Status as a personal tool is unclear.
- Google: the Workspace MCP Server announced at Cloud Next 2026 names Drive, Gmail, Calendar and Chat; whether Meet transcripts are exposed was not established. A competitor blog claims there is no official Google Meet MCP server. Apple: reports of MCP support inside App Intents date from 2025 beta code and were not confirmed for shipping macOS.
- Zoom: a Tactiq page claims the AI Companion brand was retired in June 2026, which conflicts with Zoom's own May 2026 release using that name. Unresolved.
- Mem0 reportedly removed its local OpenMemory server on July 29, 2026 (single secondary source in a search summary). Bee has only an unofficial community MCP server per search listings. Neither verified.
- The AP story on notetaker privacy was read via a syndicated copy; two other syndications returned 404. It names no lawsuit against a notetaker vendor.
- Limitless: the support floor of at least a year from December 2025 implies a possible wind-down around December 2026; that timing is inference from a vendor blog, not an announcement.

### Consent and legal landscape, pricing, and what users complain about

As of November 2026 the AI notetaker field is crowded, consolidating and under legal fire. Granola raised $125M at $1.5B and added MCP, an API and mobile apps; Superhuman bought Fathom; Google shipped an on-device Mac notetaker; Wispr Flow and Notion ship botless capture. Botless capture, on-device Parakeet or Whisper, Markdown export and MCP are now baseline, not differentiators.

Law: In re Otter.AI survived dismissal on 13 August 2026 (federal wiretap, CIPA and BIPA voiceprint claims). Granola was sued on 30 July 2026 for silent botless capture with participant notice off by default, plus default training. Fireflies and health systems face parallel suits. Universities and Microsoft Teams block third-party bots but mostly do not address desktop capture. In the EU, high-risk AI Act duties slipped to December 2027; GDPR and German criminal law still apply, and the household exemption excludes work meetings. No case or regulator source was found on fully local recorders; by inference they avoid the vendor-receipt and training theories but not the user's own all-party consent duty or voiceprint sensitivity.

Pricing clusters at $14 to $20 per user per month; free tiers are capped by history, minutes or meeting count; local tools are free, one-time or about $10 per month.

Users complain most about speaker attribution, non-English and mixed-language audio, reliability, lock-in and consent, more than about summary quality. Thin areas: persistent speaker identity, code-switching, consent tooling.

**What it means for this product.**

- Crowded: botless capture, on-device Parakeet or Whisper, diarization, local LLM summaries, Markdown or Obsidian export and a local MCP server are now shipped by a dozen 2026 projects and by Google. The described stack matches the common pattern almost exactly (Trace uses Parakeet through FluidAudio; Yogurt, Anarlog and Meetily ship local MCP or CLI). None of these features is distinctive by itself any more.
- Thin and most valued: persistent speaker identity. It is the top user complaint, cloud vendors are being sued over exactly this feature, and local developers call it hard. A single-user tool can do what vendors now find legally risky: enrol voices locally, combine them with the calendar roster and remembered corrections, and keep names stable across meetings. Same-room capture on one microphone is the weakest case and is where incumbents are expanding (watch, phone, in-person notes). Treat the voice embeddings as the most sensitive data in the library: local only, never synced or sent to a bring-your-own-key model, deletable per person.
- Thin: two-language and code-switched meetings. Commercial tools mis-detect language and local launches are English-first. Per-segment language identification, routing between models, a bilingual LLM repair pass and a small personal evaluation set of real mixed-language recordings would be unusual in this field. Published accuracy figures here are vendor claims, so the only trustworthy number is one measured on the owner's own audio.
- Local processing changes the legal picture less than marketing suggests, and this part is inference because no case or regulator source on fully local recorders was found. The vendor suits turn on a third party receiving the audio and using it for its own purposes or training, plus notice being off by default; a local tool avoids the first two and sidesteps bot bans. It does not remove the user's own duty where all parties must consent (one vendor guide lists California, Florida, Illinois, Maryland, Massachusetts, Montana, New Hampshire, Pennsylvania and Washington, with several hybrid states), Germany's criminal rule on recording non-public speech, or GDPR for work meetings.
- Consent as a product feature is open ground: a per-meeting consent state, a ready chat or spoken notice, a re-prompt when someone joins late, a record of who was told kept beside the recording, and a mode that transcribes without retaining audio for sensitive calls. Commenters ask for exactly this, and the funded vendors only surfaced notices under legal pressure.
- The bring-your-own-key path is the weak point for privileged, clinical or source-sensitive material. Privilege and confidentiality analyses turn on vendor terms and third-party access, so a hard per-meeting or per-workflow switch that guarantees nothing leaves the machine (ASR, diarization, summary and publish target alike) is worth more to lawyer, clinician, journalist or researcher style use than any summary feature. Publishing to a cloud workspace or keeping the library in a cloud-synced folder reintroduces a third party in the same way.
- Agent access is commodity; depth and openness are not. Vendors gate MCP by plan and history and have closed their local stores. A personal library can expose everything (full transcripts, stable speakers, decisions, commitments, flagged moments) and support longitudinal questions per person or project, pre-meeting briefs and commitment tracking. Granola's briefs and agent brief show where funded players are heading, so the edge is depth over one's own history, not the existence of an MCP server.
- Dependability and shape beat novelty. Users ask for a headless daemon with a CLI, never losing audio, separate tracks, echo handling, sane file naming and mid-call flagging, and they resent Electron memory use and subscriptions. A native menu-bar daemon already sits on the right side of those complaints, so nothing in the user evidence points to a language or runtime rewrite as a source of value; summary quality and price are also poor places to compete.

Trace reached 205 points in June with Parakeet through FluidAudio and mid-call moment flagging. | Yogurt free; Trace about GBP 9.99 one-time; MimicScribe free on-device with BYO key for cloud; Muesli open source | <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50454625> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48521236> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48415709> |

**Trends.**

- Litigation has moved from visible bots to botless capture. Chamberlain v. Granola (N.D. Cal., No. 3:26-cv-07926, filed 30 July 2026) pleads federal wiretap, CIPA sections 631 and 632, CDAFA, UCL, intrusion upon seclusion and unjust enrichment on the theory that no bot joins, chat and watermark notices exist but default to off, and anonymised data trains models by default on free and business plans. The complaint quotes Granola's own site: "Other people in the room won't know it's there." These are allegations with no merits ruling. A law-firm article written the day before the Otter ruling argued the federal count may fail under one-party consent; that is pre-ruling commentary, since the same district then let a federal wiretap claim proceed against Otter. <https://natlawreview.com/article/ai-note-taking-tools-silent-recording-and-consent-problem> <https://www.layer3labs.io/guides/ai-note-taker-lawsuits> <https://idtechwire.com/otter-ai-must-face-voiceprint-claims-under-illinois-biometric-law/>
- In re Otter.AI Privacy Litigation (N.D. Cal., No. 5:25-cv-06911): on 13 August 2026 Judge Eumi K. Lee granted dismissal only in part. Federal Wiretap Act, CIPA and Illinois BIPA voiceprint claims proceed; CFAA, CDAFA and Washington Privacy Act counts were dismissed and six counts withdrawn. At the pleading stage the court would not treat the vendor as a mere tool of the host where it allegedly kept and used conversations for its own purposes, and it accepted that a speaker profile able to recognise the same person in later meetings is plausibly a voiceprint. No liability finding. <https://idtechwire.com/otter-ai-must-face-voiceprint-claims-under-illinois-biometric-law/> <https://www.recordinglaw.com/news/otter-ai-wiretap-lawsuit-explained/> <https://www.bairdholm.com/blog/core-privacy-claims-against-ai-note-taking-platform-survive-dismissal/>
- Institutions block third-party bots and steer people to built-in assistants. Virginia Tech (11 March 2026) lists Otter, Read, Fireflies and others as unauthorised, approves the Zoom and Microsoft assistants and tells staff to uninstall unauthorised software. Waterloo bans third-party note-taking bots in hosted meetings. UW-Madison has blocked six such services from Zoom since late 2024. Microsoft Teams now detects external bots, labels them as unverified in the lobby and lets admins auto-block (notice of 13 March 2026, general availability planned for June). None of the policies read addresses desktop or botless audio capture explicitly, though Virginia Tech's catch-all wording could reach it. <https://news.vt.edu/notices/it/2026/03/it-ai-meeting-assistants.html> <https://uwaterloo.ca/information-systems-technology/about/policies-standards-and-guidelines/artificial-intelligence-ai/meeting-bots-ai-transcription-tools-guidelines> <https://it.wisc.edu/news/uw-madison-blocks-3rd-party-ai-services-for-enhanced-security> <https://uctoday.com/ai-meeting-bots-controls-microsoft-zoom-google>
- EU: the AI Act omnibus was politically agreed on 7 May 2026 and, per one data-protection digest, published in the Official Journal on 24 July 2026. Stand-alone high-risk obligations move to 2 December 2027 and product-embedded ones to 2 August 2028; transparency duties applied from 2 August 2026 and cover emotion recognition and biometric categorisation. A German law-firm note says pure speech-to-text is generally not expected to be high-risk, that this can change when combined with evaluation of people, and that recording non-public speech without consent can be criminal under Section 201 of the German Criminal Code. GDPR Recital 18 limits the household exemption to activity with no professional link, so work meetings fall outside it. <https://www.lw.com/en/insights/ai-act-update-eu-resolves-to-change-rules-and-extend-deadlines> <https://techgdpr.com/blog/data-protection-digest-01082026-eu-ai-act-becomes-applicable-with-high-risk-ai-temporarily-excluded/> <https://www.heuking.de/en/news-events/newsletter-articles/detail/legally-compliant-use-of-ai-transcription-tools-in-businesses.html> <https://gdpr-info.eu/recitals/no-18/>
- Voice biometrics is the sharpest legal edge of speaker identification. The US suits against Otter and Fireflies plead that speaker recognition creates voiceprints under Illinois BIPA. A dictation vendor's compliance guide (not a regulator) draws the GDPR line this way: raw meeting audio is personal data, while an extracted voiceprint or a transcript labelling speakers by identity is Article 9 biometric data, and it argues on-device processing avoids most regulated categories. That last point is a vendor's view; none of the legal commentary read analyses on-device tools. <https://idtechwire.com/otter-ai-must-face-voiceprint-claims-under-illinois-biometric-law/> <https://natlawreview.com/article/illinois-bipa-suit-targets-ai-note-takers-practical-lessons-meeting-transcription> <https://weesperneonflow.ai/en/blog/2026-05-16-eu-ai-act-voice-dictation-compliance-guide-europe-2026/>
- Regulated professions are getting specific warnings. A proposed class action against Sharp HealthCare (filed 26 November 2025) and another against Sutter Health and MemorialCare allege an ambient AI scribe recorded visits without all-party consent and sent audio to a vendor cloud; advisers now recommend affirmative per-patient consent and close reading of vendor training and deletion terms. For lawyers, NYC Bar Formal Opinion 2025-6 (December 2025) requires client notice and consent before AI recording, accuracy review and vendor diligence, and US v. Heppner (S.D.N.Y., 17 February 2026) denied privilege where a consumer AI platform's terms allowed training and third-party disclosure. No court has squarely ruled on notetaker transcripts. <https://www.fisherphillips.com/en/news-insights/new-class-action-targets-healthcare-ai-recordings.html> <https://www.medicaleconomics.com/view/what-ai-vendor-contracts-and-ai-scribe-consent-mean-for-physicians-legal-risk> <https://www.implicator.ai/ai-note-takers-face-privilege-warnings-after-new-york-bar-opinion/> <https://clsbluesky.law.columbia.edu/2026/06/16/cahill-discusses-a-i-note-takers-in-corporate-meetings/>
- Consolidation and bundling. Granola raised $125M at $1.5B (25 March 2026) and itself says basic meeting notes are becoming a commodity, so it is moving to acting on notes. Superhuman acquired Fathom (14 September 2026). Wispr Flow launched a botless notetaker for Mac (5 August 2026). Google released an on-device Mac notetaker (8 October 2026). Notion bundles botless notes into its $20 Business plan and Zoom's assistant page claims in-person and third-party platform coverage. TechCrunch describes the market as crowded and names Calendly as another entrant. <https://techcrunch.com/2026/03/25/granola-raises-125m-hits-1-5b-valuation-as-it-expands-from-meeting-notetaker-to-enterprise-ai-app/> <https://techcrunch.com/2026/09/14/superhuman-acquires-yc-backed-notetaker-fathom-as-productivity-platforms-push-for-agentic-work/> <https://techcrunch.com/2026/08/05/wispr-flow-is-preparing-to-launch-a-meeting-notetaker-updated-terms-suggest/> <https://techcrunch.com/2026/10/08/google-releases-a-new-local-first-granola-competitor/>
- A wave of local-first Mac recorders shares one stack: Parakeet or Whisper through FluidAudio or WhisperKit, pyannote and WeSpeaker diarization, llama.cpp or Ollama for summaries. 2026 Hacker News launches include Yogurt (271 points, 11 November), Trace (205 points, June), Muesli, MimicScribe, Ghost Pepper Meet, Ownscribe, Loofah, Tome, HushScribe, Biscotti and MeetCap. Commenters themselves call the space crowded and say LLM-assisted coding makes a do-it-yourself recorder easy; a March newsletter ties the surge to Granola closing its local cache. <https://hn.algolia.com/api/v1/search?query=meeting%20transcription%20local&tags=story> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50454625> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48521236> <https://www.weekinvoiceai.com/p/week-in-voice-ai12-granola-scramble>
- MCP and agent access became standard during 2026. Granola launched MCP on 4 February (free tier limited to the last 30 days), added a paid API and on 11 November an agent brief written for other AI agents. Otter lists an MCP server even on its free tier. Jamie puts MCP and API on Pro. Meetily Pro, Anarlog and Yogurt ship local MCP or CLI servers. <https://www.granola.ai/updates> <https://www.granola.ai/pricing> <https://otter.ai/pricing> <https://www.meetjamie.ai/pricing>
- Pricing. Mainstream paid tiers sit at roughly $14 to $20 per user per month on monthly billing (Granola Business $14, Krisp Core about $16, Otter Pro $16.99, Read AI Pro $19.75, Fathom Premium $20, Notion Business $20 as a bundle), euro-priced tools run higher (tl;dv Pro EUR 29, Jamie Pro EUR 39 billed annually), and enterprise tiers land at $30 to $40. Free tiers are limited by history (Granola 30 days), minutes (Otter 300 per month and 30 per conversation) or count (Read AI 5 transcripts, Jamie 10 notes, Zoom 3 uses per month); Fathom's unlimited free recording is the outlier. Local tools are free open source, one-time (MacWhisper Pro EUR 64, Trace about GBP 9.99) or a low subscription (Meetily Pro $10 per month billed yearly). <https://www.granola.ai/pricing> <https://krisp.ai/pricing/> <https://otter.ai/pricing> <https://www.read.ai/plans-pricing>

**Complaints and gaps.**

- Speaker attribution is the most consistent complaint. In a competitor-authored tally of 35 Reddit threads about Notion AI Meeting Notes (137 people with a verdict), wrong or missing speaker labels drew the most complaints (24), including swapped host and guest on one-to-one calls. On Hacker News, users ask for voice learning so real names persist across meetings, report names not picked up on a Teams call, and describe same-room multi-speaker capture on one microphone and overlapping speech as unsolved. One developer calls on-device speaker identification hard and deliberately over-segments because merges are easier to fix than bad joins. <https://metawhisp.com/blog/notion-ai-meeting-notes-review/> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48521236> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50454625> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48415709>
- Non-English and mixed-language meetings are poorly served. Trace launched English-only and garbles other languages without an error; commenters ask for German and French; Notion users report an English meeting transcribed as Russian or Thai with no way to re-run it. A speech-API vendor describes the code-switching failure modes (locking to the first detected language, silent translation, hallucinated text) and calls Whisper's behaviour unpredictable; these are vendor claims without independent benchmarks. One team built an open notetaker specifically for Hindi and English mixed meetings out of frustration with existing tools. <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48521236> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50454625> <https://metawhisp.com/blog/notion-ai-meeting-notes-review/> <https://www.gladia.io/blog/multilingual-meeting-transcription-language-coverage-accuracy-and-code-switching-challenges>
- Reliability and lost recordings. The Notion sample has 17 complaints here (transcription stopping mid-meeting, a meeting that could not be found, phone recording pausing on app switch). Hacker News commenters say existing tools crash and lose audio, raw WAV files eat disk, microphone bleed duplicates lines, and one app took over the microphone during a Teams call. Trustpilot's summary for Fireflies mentions occasional failures to record important sessions. <https://metawhisp.com/blog/notion-ai-meeting-notes-review/> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48521236> <https://www.trustpilot.com/review/fireflies.ai>
- Lock-in and data access. Granola changed its local cache around March 2026 and had encrypted it by mid-year, breaking tools and Obsidian plugins that read it; its CEO accepted responsibility and pointed to MCP and a later API. Per a critical third-party article that promotes a rival, the API is paid-only, the free MCP covers 30 days without transcripts, and CSV export is limited to once per 24 hours. An Ask HN poster whose Obsidian sync broke asked for a headless daemon with a CLI, local diarization and a menu-bar toggle. Others want plain Markdown on disk, control of file names, recurring meetings appended to one file, and an import path from Granola. <https://superframeworks.com/articles/granola-encrypted-local-notes> <https://www.weekinvoiceai.com/p/week-in-voice-ai12-granola-scramble> <https://techcrunch.com/2026/03/25/granola-raises-125m-hits-1-5b-valuation-as-it-expands-from-meeting-notetaker-to-enterprise-ai-app/> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48936123>
- Consent is an anxiety that tools leave to the user. Hacker News commenters in all-party jurisdictions ask what they must do, suggest repeating a consent prompt when new people join, and want a consent note in the FAQ. Granola's participant notices default to off, which is now pleaded against it, and its Enterprise tier lists an org-wide notification as a feature. One commenter points out that anyone who can hear a call can transcribe it, so the issue reduces to trust between participants. None of the product pages read described a jurisdiction-aware consent workflow. <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50454625> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48521236> <https://natlawreview.com/article/ai-note-taking-tools-silent-recording-and-consent-problem> <https://www.granola.ai/pricing>
- Evidence that summaries are too generic is weaker than the brief assumed. In the Notion sample only 5 people complained about summaries and action items against 17 who praised them. The sharper concerns are tone and context (jokes, sarcasm and side remarks landing in notes sent to everyone, per a 2025 consumer piece), accuracy of records relied on years later, and verbatim transcripts preserving speculative remarks that become discoverable. Some users want no built-in summary at all, only a clean transcript for their own LLM, and one asked for summary benchmarks against Granola that nobody supplied. <https://metawhisp.com/blog/notion-ai-meeting-notes-review/> <https://www.foxnews.com/tech/ai-meeting-notes-recording-your-private-conversations.print> <https://clsbluesky.law.columbia.edu/2026/06/16/cahill-discusses-a-i-note-takers-in-corporate-meetings/> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48521236>
- Subscription fatigue and plan gating. 20 people in the Notion sample objected to needing the $20 Business seat for one feature. Hacker News commenters welcome one-time pricing, say they have dropped other subscriptions and call MacWhisper Pro expensive. On Trustpilot both Otter and Fireflies hold a 4.5 TrustScore, yet 24 percent of Otter reviews and 13 percent of Fireflies reviews are one-star; on the first page of each (the only page visible), billing, unexpected seat charges and cancellation are the most visible negative themes. <https://metawhisp.com/blog/notion-ai-meeting-notes-review/> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48521236> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50454625> <https://www.trustpilot.com/review/otter.ai>
- Local AI cost and unproven quality. Commenters on an Electron-based local app worry about memory, want to skip or offload local summarisation to a cloud router, and ask for a model selector. The developer of an on-device tool reports limited success with a 9B local model on long structured prompts and expects most users to choose cloud models for larger tasks. Users also ask how local ASR accuracy compares with Otter, Granola and Google Meet, and no thread read offers a benchmark. <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=50454625> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48415709> <https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48521236>
- Security and vendor trust. A researcher disclosed a one-click account takeover in Granola's Electron desktop app in mid-2026 (unguarded window navigation left a privileged bridge attached, and the renderer exposed the local database key at the time); Granola patched within days and reported no exploitation. Universities cite lax vendor terms, training use and onward data sharing as reasons for blocking third-party assistants. <https://www.strix.ai/blog/granola> <https://news.vt.edu/notices/it/2026/03/it-ai-meeting-assistants.html> <https://uwaterloo.ca/information-systems-technology/about/policies-standards-and-guidelines/artificial-intelligence-ai/meeting-bots-ai-transcription-tools-guidelines>
- Regulated and constrained users have guidance but few fitting tools. Lawyers are told to obtain client consent, review accuracy and vet vendor terms, and one firm's guidance is to disable public AI transcription for legal-advice meetings. Clinicians are told to get affirmative consent and now face class actions over cloud scribes. HIPAA terms are reserved for higher tiers (Fathom Team, Read AI Enterprise+, an Otter Enterprise add-on, Krisp Enterprise). None of the legal commentary read evaluates on-device tools as a distinct category. Separately, some work Macs block App Store apps or any installs. <https://www.implicator.ai/ai-note-takers-face-privilege-warnings-after-new-york-bar-opinion/> <https://clsbluesky.law.columbia.edu/2026/06/16/cahill-discusses-a-i-note-takers-in-corporate-meetings/> <https://www.medicaleconomics.com/view/what-ai-vendor-contracts-and-ai-scribe-consent-mean-for-physicians-legal-risk> <https://www.fathom.ai/pricing>

**Not verified.**

- Method limits: the session's shared web search budget ran out after 17 successful searches; everything after that was direct page fetches of known URLs. Reddit could not be searched or fetched at all (the domain blocks this crawler), so all Reddit evidence is secondhand through one competitor-authored tally (MetaWhisp) that read threads via an archive. Hacker News threads were read through the Algolia HN API (hn.algolia.com/api/v1/items/<id>) and are cited by their news.ycombinator.com item URLs.
- No case, regulator statement or bar opinion was found that analyses a fully local, single-user recorder. The statement that local processing avoids the vendor-eavesdropper and training theories is my inference from what the Otter order and the Granola complaint turn on, not a sourced legal conclusion. Granola (botless capture, cloud processing) is the nearest litigated analogue.
- BIPA applicability to an individual: my recollection is that the statute defines a private entity to include any individual, which would mean an individual enrolling voiceprints is not categorically outside it, but the Illinois legislature site refused the connection and I could not verify the text. No case applying BIPA to personal on-device diarization was found.
- Spain's AEPD reportedly published voice-transcription guidance in January and April 2026 treating voice as personal data and flagging emotion inference under the AI Act; this comes from a search excerpt of a law-firm blog (insideprivacy.com) that returned 403 when opened.
- Otter docket after the ruling (an answer reportedly filed 17 September 2026 and a scheduling order on 16 September) appeared only in a search summary; the pages I opened stop at late August or are stale. Current status of Chamberlain v. Granola after mid-August 2026 (any motion to dismiss or change to default notices) is unverified.
- Fricker v. Fireflies.AI (N.D. Ill., reported March 2026), NYC Bar Formal Opinion 2026-2 (reportedly August 2026, on recording non-client conversations), and the Seventh Circuit argument on BIPA amendment retroactivity (February 2026) were seen only in search summaries.
- Conflicts between sources: Cruz v. Fireflies is pending per one article and voluntarily dismissed per another; the Sharp HealthCare plaintiff is named Saucedo in most coverage and Salgado in one; counts of all-party consent states range from nine to twelve depending on how hybrid states (Connecticut, Nevada, Oregon, Delaware, Michigan) are classed, and the nine-state list used here comes from a notetaker vendor's guide, not from statutes.
- University blocks at Eastern Washington, Tufts, Mississippi State, York, Alvernia, UT Health and VCU appeared in search results but were not opened (the EWU page returned no body). No corporate (non-university) ban on notetakers was sourced; only university policies and Microsoft Teams admin controls were. No source was found on whether Zoom, Teams or Meet can detect or restrict botless desktop audio capture.
- Pricing not verified: Fireflies (official page exposed no numbers), Zoom's paid assistant price, Microsoft Teams Copilot, Gemini in Meet, ChatGPT record mode (help page returned 403), Plaud and other hardware recorders, Anarlog's paid tier, Google AI Edge Foresight, Wispr Flow's notetaker. A reported February 2026 Granola plan change that retired an $18 individual tier and tightened the free plan came from search summaries of third-party pricing sites only.
- The Verge piece on Granola note links being viewable by anyone with the link by default (April 2026) and the AP piece on notetaker scepticism (July 2026) could not be fetched; only their Hacker News titles were seen. The Limitless acquisition by Meta (December 2025), end of pendant sales and EU and UK withdrawal came from search summaries of blogs and were not opened.
- Niches: nothing specific to journalists or academic researchers was found in 2026 sources; the suggestion that they are underserved by cloud tools and fit a local-only mode is inference from the legal and clinical material. Engineers are represented only through Hacker News threads. Therapists specifically (as opposed to physicians) were not covered by any page opened.
- Excluded as unverifiable: a vendor blog's claim of a EUR 2.4M fine in February 2026 over AI-transcribed HR meetings; a vendor claim that generic tools lose 30 to 40 percent accuracy on mid-conversation language switches; a cited benchmark of accuracy falling from 95 to 72 percent under code-switching.
- Several commentary sources are competitors or sellers and should be weighed accordingly: MetaWhisp, Anarlog, Scribbl, Gladia, Weesper, superframeworks (promotes Voibe), Layer3 Labs (sells audits).

## What this product is today

From a reader who mapped what the code does, as opposed to what the documents say. Status: wired (works end to end), partial, unclear, or documented only.

A single-user macOS menu-bar recorder that is genuinely built end to end: it detects calls in native meeting apps, captures mic and system audio with no bot, transcribes and diarizes on-device (FluidAudio), then a Python sidecar summarizes (Anthropic by default, local MLX optional) and publishes Markdown to Notion, Obsidian, disk or a LAN share. Around that core sits a large, mostly wired second layer: Library with full-text search, cited Ask, digests, voiceprint roster, glossary, workflows, MCP server, backup. It is an AI-written, source-installed personal tool (about 90K lines, 1,088 commits in six months) whose breadth outruns its proven reliability: end-of-call detection, speaker naming and transcript accuracy are the soft centre.

**What sets it apart.**

- No bot and no cloud audio: capture, ASR and diarization all run on the Mac, and one policy chokepoint (config.zero_egress / effective_backend plus a process-wide egress guard) can force the whole pipeline on-device globally or per NDA workflow.
- Channel-separated recording (mic left, system right) used as ground truth for 'me', which enables lossless capture-first with offline mute redaction, off-the-record stretches, and a recoverable kept original instead of a destructive real-time gate.
- Multi-signal call lifecycle fusion (per-process CoreAudio activity, ScreenCaptureKit windows, Accessibility Leave/mute buttons, Teams' own log) with a 'hold long, trim later' end rule, rather than a calendar or a bot join event.
- Files are the database: plain WAV / JSON / Markdown sidecars per meeting, rebuildable derived indexes (FTS5, embeddings), and idempotent fan-out into the notes system the user already owns (Notion, Obsidian, folder, LAN share) with per-sink failure isolation.
- Verifiable output: every summary bullet can carry a quote + timestamp anchor that is re-checked against the transcript before writing, published notes deep-link back to the exact second locally, and Ask drops any citation it cannot resolve.
- Per-meeting-type Workflows as one contract that routes everything: context prompt, engine, destinations, NDA flag, audio retention, and custom summary sections, resolved at record time into a sidecar both languages read.
- Local personal learning loops: vocabulary rules, a named voiceprint roster, a graded correction corpus with engine provenance, and a working on-device LoRA training and A/B harness.
- Engine and agent openness unusual for the category: six interchangeable summarization engines (including a Claude subscription via CLI and Apple Intelligence) and a read-only MCP server with a privacy visibility gate so other AI tools can query the library.

**Weak spots.**

- Not a product anyone else can install: a source build needing Xcode tools, Homebrew, uv and ffmpeg, ad-hoc signed, no notarized app, every build versioned 0.1.0; that same signing gap is what keeps native Shortcuts actions dead. Apple Silicon and macOS 14+ only.
- The headline promise (hands-off recording) is the least proven part: end-of-call detection has a run of P1 fixes (END9 to END17) still awaiting field confirmation, plain browser tabs work for Google Meet only, and all of it depends on scraping other vendors' UI, calibrated to English-UI Teams.
- Transcript quality is unmeasured and structurally coarse: no WER or DER on real audio, speakers assigned per sentence, no live transcript (declared non-goal), and vocabulary is a post-hoc text rewrite rather than ASR biasing.
- Knowing who spoke is manual labour: no calendar, no participant-list read, the roster learns only from clicks, and run-all summarizes and publishes before anyone can name speakers, so attendees and action owners ship as diarization labels (FEAT21).
- Value after the summary is thin: Facts is an undifferentiated action list (the owner's own verdict), digests / People / Meeting time are secondary surfaces with no keep-or-cut decision, Ask is a slow blocking call with a small on-device context, and there is no meeting-series continuity.
- The local-first story has gaps a privacy buyer would find: the default engine is cloud Anthropic, the default library sits in iCloud-synced ~/Documents, the egress guard covers httpx only, backups are unencrypted, and the local 7B model is visibly weaker (the one LoRA attempt lost to base).
- Shaped around one person: the shipped base prompt hard-codes a regulated-SaaS domain (pipeline/src/mp/prompts/meeting_summary.md), language verification knows only en/uk/ru scripts, UI copy says backend / sink / daemon, cloud meetings over about an hour get a paste bundle instead of a summary.
- Breadth over depth: roughly 70K Swift + 22K Python lines, six engines, four sinks, 24 CLI subcommands, logic mirrored across two languages, 170 active backlog items, and 100 to 200 KB docs that already drift from code (README says mute redaction is default; the code makes it opt-in).

**Unusual assets.**

- A private, channel-separated, speaker-labelled meeting corpus with per-meeting 256-d speaker embeddings (<stem>.embeddings.json), a named roster, mute timelines and flagged moments: rare training and evaluation data for personal speech and speaker models.
- MeetingPipeCore: a tested, strict-concurrency 'what is this person doing in a call right now' sensing layer (AX observer bus, CoreAudio HAL per-process audio, ScreenCaptureKit, Teams log parsing, mute-label catalogue, promotion engine) that is independent of the recorder and reusable for presence, focus or automation products.
- Provenance-rich distillation data and tooling: run sidecars recording which engine and model wrote each summary, human correction records, a cloud-vs-local dogfood harness with a held-out split, and an on-device LoRA trainer (mp/train_adapter.py, mp/dogfood.py).
- A complete personal retrieval stack: incremental checkpointed multilingual embedding index, FTS5, citation verification, an NDA/regulated visibility gate, and an MCP server, which together are most of a local 'work memory API' for agents.
- A schema-fenced JSONL event log of every detection and pipeline decision plus an auditor (mp/analyze_detection.py), about 3,400 Swift and 1,300 Python test functions, cross-language golden fixtures and truth-fence CI: unusually strong scaffolding for replay-based reliability work or a port.
- Typed contracts already in place for a different front end: the Workflow model, the MeetingSummary schema with timestamp-verified evidence, the meetingpipe:// deep-link grammar, and the Swift/Python sidecar formats mean the capture daemon and the intelligence layer can be separated or replaced independently.

## Ideas the project already considered

So that nothing above is proposed in ignorance of an earlier decision. Where an idea in this document reopens one, it says what changed.

**The owner's taste, read from the record.** Measures before building: probe-first spikes with thresholds pre-registered, and an honest negative is an accepted close (AI5, CAL1, DIAR4, LOCAL9). Privacy is identity: local-first, per-meeting egress policy, plain files over databases. Quiet native macOS craft; no AI sparkle or web chrome. Truthful states and loudly named failures, never a silent fallback or a silently wrong guess. One toolchain where possible, few dependencies, each approved. Personal tool first: selling deferred, P3 parked, features promoted on demand. Distrusts claims not verified on real hardware or the real library, and files the gap when a doc overstates code. Prefers reversible, overlay-based edits, and is willing to delete surface that real use does not justify.

**Open themes in the backlog.**

- Caveat on reading the backlog: of 234 task files, 139 are new and 41 partial. About 134 of the new ones were filed on or after 2026-10-21 by a run of audits (go-live, architecture, code quality, observability, performance, security, upgrade) as defects and hardening. The partials are a different group: 34 of 41 were filed July to September, mostly owner-reported fixes that shipped and await field confirmation. Net-new feature pull is small (FEAT19, FEAT20, FEAT21, ASR5, AI14). The themes below are mostly 'make existing claims true'.
- End-of-call detection that never chops a live Teams meeting: the recurring P1 class (END9 to END17, DET13 partial and awaiting field confirmation), plus non-English Teams UI (DET15) and replay-based validation (T8).
- Capture that cannot silently lose audio: stuck system-audio starts, engine deadlocks, quiet-mic detection, disk-space and write-failure handling (REC12 to REC22, MIC17), and macOS 27 AVAudio deprecations (REC29).
- Speaker identity that learns with less manual labour: passive enrolment, name picker, merge, play-sample, apply-across-library (FEAT19); participant names from the meeting app (FEAT20); wait-for-names before publish (FEAT21); channel-aware and per-word attribution (DIAR6, DIAR7); the owner's own label (UX83).
- Measured transcription quality: an owner-held English/Ukrainian/code-switched golden set with WER (ASR4), then vocabulary boosting (ASR5) and a qualified FluidAudio upgrade path (ASR6).
- Zero-egress and trust claims made true beyond HTTP: synced folders, non-httpx network paths, fail-open NDA classification, model loading, the Claude CLI agent session, hardened runtime, prompt-injection fencing of model-written text across sinks, MCP and URL verbs (SEC17 to SEC31, AUTO6 to AUTO8, DIAR9, STOR6).
- One owner per concern across the Swift/Python split: the boundary ADR and Apple Intelligence as a provider only (ARCH11), a typed owner for the per-meeting file contract (ARCH10, PIPE33), a single owner for the local model server (LOCAL21), and a typed backend (ARCH14).
- Deciding what to keep: the secondary-surface cut (FEAT18), what the Facts rail becomes or whether it is removed (DV5), and delete-or-verdict for the unwired staged local summarizer and dev tooling (PIPE20).
- Local summarization that is good enough for the privacy path: model refresh bench and staged pipeline awaiting owner runs (LOCAL13, LOCAL14), automatic local fallback when Apple Intelligence refuses Ukrainian (PIPE25), a newer multilingual embedder for Ask (AI14), LoRA retry on a numeric trigger (LOCAL17).
- Installable without the git clone, short of selling: embedded runtime bundle (DIST1), versioned builds with rollback (DIST6), honest install/uninstall (DIST5), ffmpeg removal (DEP2), Python 3.12 (DEP3), release-build CI (CI9). Developer ID stays deferred.
- Honest failure surfacing and plain-language UX: failures that reach the menu and notifications, run ids and reasons in logs (OBS1 to OBS6, UX90 to UX94, STOR9 to STOR11), first-run steps that feed each other, no jargon, no raw regex or bundle ids in settings (UX84 to UX89), a neutral base prompt (PIPE26), a saner long-meeting gate (PIPE27).
- Agent and automation surface maturing rather than growing: MCP paging and policy correctness (AUTO5, AUTO6, AUTO8), confirmation on URL-triggered state changes (AUTO7); native Shortcuts actions and browser-meeting support are built or designed but parked on external triggers.

**Stated non-goals.**

- Anything tied to selling stays deferred unless a task explicitly calls for it: licence keys, telemetry, code signing / notarization / Sparkle, landing site, marketing copy, compliance docs (root CLAUDE.md 'Out of scope'). The launch band BRAND1..9 is 'do not pull forward'.
- P3 tasks are parked and left alone; they move only when the owner promotes one on demand.
- Carve-outs that ARE in scope: setup ease (docs/SETUP.md kept true in the same commit as any install change), the how-to guide (docs/GUIDE.md plus one menu item), GitHub repo presence, and the app's visual identity.
- No onboarding machinery: first-run wizards, coach marks, videos, a hosted docs site and any further interactive tour stay deferred. Exactly one menu-opened Tutorial exists, it is 'not a precedent', and nothing may start it from a launch path.
- README 'Non-goals, by design': video capture, a live in-call transcript, multi-user sharing, cross-platform, and a mobile companion.
- Single user, single Mac. No multi-user or team features; go-live verdict: do not go paid or B2B now; two-Mac sync is deferred.
- No cloud database and no database of record. Audio, transcript and diarization stay on the Mac; plain files are the source of truth and every data view is a derived, read-only index (ADR 0003).
- No new always-on egress. Every AI capability routes through the configured engine and the egress clamp; under regulated or NDA mode nothing silently falls back to cloud (AI/DV standing rule). The daemon makes no outbound API calls; egress belongs to the pipeline (ADR 0007).
- No AI branding or celebration: no sparkle, no 'AI-powered' badges, no Claude branding in chrome, no emoji, no exclamation marks, no celebration toasts (PRODUCT.md).
- No Electron-style or web-rendered chrome and no dark-glass 'AI tool' aesthetic; native AppKit/SwiftUI materials, fixed window sizes, menu-bar only (PRODUCT.md anti-references, ADR 0004).
- No trend, coaching or CRM-shaped analytics: talk-time coaching, sentiment, filler-word metrics, scorecards, pipeline tracking, and no notification channel beyond the one digest (research docs, SKIP).
- The CLI is a debugging escape hatch, not a product surface; a capability shipped CLI-only counts as unfinished (de-CLI standing rule).
- No fuzzy inference where a wrong answer is silent: no series grouping by title similarity, attendee overlap or time-of-week (CAL3, AI6); no permanent meeting-type field (AI5); no People view built on raw model-inferred attendees (DV2).
- No calendar read for this owner (ADR 0011, CAL1): revisit only if the owner connects their calendar to macOS.
- No second ASR engine or Python transcription fallback (ADR 0007); no real-time destructive mute gating by default (ADR 0016; README says regulated and NDA modes keep a real-time gate); no meeting bots (local capture only).
- No new dependencies without asking the owner first; the dependency set is deliberately small.
- No Swift port of the summarizer on the strength of the code-duplication tax alone (LOCAL12): only a measured A/B against the operational trigger reopens it.

## Limits

- The market research is a snapshot from 2026-11-12. One researcher hit a search limit and listed what it could not verify; those items are under "Not verified".
- The ideators and judges did no web research of their own; they worked from the market file and cite its sources.
- The rewrite assessor built and ran nothing. Its claims about the Swift local-model stack come from the market file's summaries, and it says which could not be checked.
- The field data arrived after ideation and judging. It is used in the short version and the suggested quarter, not in the scores.
- Scores are three opinions averaged. Where the judges disagree, their own words under "What the judges would do" matter more than the mean.
